* About
This is my personal Emacs configuration, written and maintained in Org mode.

It contains my preferred packages, UI tweaks, and programming helpers. I tangle
and load the relevant Emacs Lisp from this file so the Org source is the single
source of truth for my setup. Learn more about Org mode at
[[https://orgmode.org][the Org mode website]].


* General configuration

** The beginning of the Emacs configuration

We start by activating [[https://www.gnu.org/software/emacs/manual/html_node/elisp/Lexical-Binding.html][Lexical binding]] to our init files. Note that we
do it with 2 files: =init.el= and =early-init.el=.

The =early-init.el= file was introduced on Emacs 27. This file, if
present, is evaluated before package and GUI initialization, which
makes it the ideal place to setup some initializations we will
introduce later.

The =init.el= is the default Emacs initialization file.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
;;; init.el --- Personal emacs configuration -*- lexical-binding: t; -*-
#+end_src

#+begin_src emacs-lisp :tangle early-init.el
;;; early-init.el --- Instructions loaded before the package system and GUI -*- lexical-binding: t; -*-
#+end_src

We adhere to common Emacs Lisp conventions by establishing clear section
headers for file organization. This structure divides the file into two
primary sections:

- =Commentary:= Provides a high-level, human-readable summary of the
  file's contents, organization, and history (e.g., date, author, or
  major sections). It is the first thing a reader sees.
- =Code:= Marks the clear starting point of the executable Emacs Lisp
  configuration. Everything following this header is the active setup
  logic.

For the main file:

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
;;; Commentary:
;;;
;;; This file contains my personal Emacs configuration.
;;;
;;; Code:
#+end_src

For the early init file:

#+begin_src emacs-lisp :tangle early-init.el
;;; Commentary:
;;;
;;; This file contains instructions that must be loaded before the
;;; package system and the Emacs GUI.
;;;
;;; Code:
#+end_src


** Key bindings

I am following [[https://github.com/larstvei/dot-emacs/blob/main/init.org][the dot-emacs repository]] again on maintaining a
~custom-bindings-map~ that holds all my custom bindings. This
is based on [[http://stackoverflow.com/questions/683425/globally-override-key-binding-in-emacs][this StackOverflow post]].
This map can be activated by toggling a simple ~minor-mode~ that just
activates the map, preventing other ~major-modes~ from overridding
these bindings.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(defvar custom-bindings-map (make-sparse-keymap)
  "Keymap used for user custom bindings.")

(define-minor-mode custom-bindings-mode
  "A minor mode for custom keybindings that override major modes."
  :global t
  :group 'keybindings
  :keymap custom-bindings-map)

(custom-bindings-mode 1)
#+end_src


** Meta settings

We generate the =init.el= file from the source blocks in the
=init.org=. We can use ~org-babel-tangle~ to do this. However, to make
this process automatic after saving this file, we reuse the function
below and attach it to the ~after-save-hook~. This solution is copied
from [[https://github.com/larstvei/dot-emacs/blob/main/init.org][the dot-emacs repository]].

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(declare-function org-babel-tangle "org")
(defun tangle-init ()
  "Tangle the init.org file into other necessary files.
If the current buffer is init.org the code-blocks are
tangled, and the tangled file is compiled."
  (when (equal (buffer-file-name)
               (expand-file-name (concat user-emacs-directory "init.org")))
    ;; Avoid running hooks when tangling.
    (let ((prog-mode-hook nil))
      (org-babel-tangle)
      ;; The following line is not necessary in Emacs 28+, because it
      ;; generates native compilation files (.eln) instead of .elc:
      ;; (byte-compile-file (concat user-emacs-directory "init.el"))
      ;; We use native compilation instead:
      (setq native-comp-jit-compilation t)
      (native-compile-async (concat user-emacs-directory "init.el")))))

(add-hook 'after-save-hook 'tangle-init)
#+end_src

The Emacs Customize system utilizes the value of the variable
~custom-file~ to write its customization forms. If the value of this
variable is not defined, it will write the settings to the value of
~user-init-file~, which is our =init.el= at the moment. To prevent
this from happening, we define the value of ~custom-file~ below:

#+begin_src emacs-lisp :tangle early-init.el
(setq custom-file (expand-file-name "custom.el" user-emacs-directory))
#+end_src

Below we will load the value of ~custom-file~, but we also create a
=local.el= file for local settings. This is useful for configurations
that do not need to be pushed to the git repository and that are local
to the environment in use.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(require 'server)
(add-hook 'after-init-hook
          (lambda ()
            (let ((local-file (concat user-emacs-directory "local.el")))
              (when (file-exists-p local-file)
                (load-file local-file))
              (when (file-exists-p custom-file)
                (load-file custom-file))
              (unless (server-running-p)
                (server-start)))))
#+end_src

Modern Emacs versions (typically 27+) support direct package installation
from Version Control (VC) using ~package-vc-install~.

The ~when (fboundp ...)~ below guard ensures this code only runs if the
~package-vc~ extension is available, maintaining compatibility with older
or minimal Emacs setups.

A critical limitation: The native ~package-installed-p~ often fails to
recognize packages installed via VC. This custom function checks the file
system directory directly to reliably confirm their presence.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(declare-function my/package-vc-installed-p nil)
(when (fboundp 'package-vc-install)
  (defun my/package-vc-installed-p (package &optional repo-name)
    "Check whether a package is installed via package-vc.
PACKAGE is the package symbol to check.
REPO-NAME is the optional repository directory name (if different
from package name)."
    (let* ((installed-p (package-installed-p package))
           (vc-installed-p (and (fboundp 'package-vc-installed-p)
                                (package-vc-installed-p package)))
           (feature-p (featurep package))
           (pkg-name (format "%s" package))
           (actual-repo-name (or repo-name pkg-name))
           (pkg-dir (expand-file-name pkg-name package-user-dir))
           (repo-dir (expand-file-name actual-repo-name package-user-dir))
           (dir-exists-p (or (file-directory-p pkg-dir)
                             (and repo-name (file-directory-p repo-dir)))))
      (and (or installed-p vc-installed-p feature-p) dir-exists-p))))
#+end_src


** Setup script

To facilitate setting up the external tools needed by our Emacs environment, we
provide a setup script that should be executed before starting Emacs.

Ideally, all tools in the script should be installed or configured for you to
have a proper environment in place, but the script allows you to select the
tools you want to install.

The current version of the script requires =bash= to run: =bash setup.sh=.

#+begin_src sh :tangle setup.sh
#!/bin/bash

# --- Styling ---
TITLE_COLOR='\033[1;34m'
CHECK_COLOR='\033[1;32m'
UNCHECK_COLOR='\033[0;35m'
KEY_COLOR='\033[1;36m'
WARN_COLOR='\033[1;33m'
ERR_COLOR='\033[1;31m'
NC='\033[0m'

# --- Logging Functions ---
log_info()    { echo -e "${KEY_COLOR}[*]${NC} $1"; }
log_success() { echo -e "${CHECK_COLOR}[+]${NC} $1"; }
log_warn()    { echo -e "${WARN_COLOR}[!]${NC} $1"; }
log_error()   { echo -e "${ERR_COLOR}[!]${NC} $1"; }
log_skip()    {
  local label="$1"
  local status="${2:-SKIPPED}"
  echo -e "  ${UNCHECK_COLOR}→${NC} ${label}... ${WARN_COLOR}[${status}]${NC}"
}

# --- Global Registry ---
TOOLS=()
MENU_INDICES=()
MENU_SELECTED_INDICES=()
INSTALLED_ITEMS=()

declare -A SYSTEM_PACKAGES_MAP

# --- Installer Group Titles (Order defines UI sequence) ---
INSTALLER_GROUPS=(
  "secrets|Security & Credentials"
  "node|Node.js Toolchain (Shared)"
  "jvm|JVM Toolchain (SDKMAN!)"
  "standalone|General Purpose Tools"
)

# --- Help Function ---
show_help() {
  cat << EOF
Usage: ./setup.sh [options]

Options:
  -h                  Show this help message and exit
  --force-reinstall   Force a clean reinstall of all managed tools
  -v/--verbose        Enable verbose output

This script manages the lifecycle of your development tools,
including SDKMAN! versions, language servers, and Node.js utilities.
EOF
  exit 0
}

FORCE_REINSTALL=false
VERBOSE=false

parse_args() {
  while [[ "$#" -gt 0 ]]; do
    case "$1" in
      -h|--help)
        show_help
        ;;
      --force-reinstall)
        FORCE_REINSTALL=true
        shift
        ;;
      -v|--verbose)
        VERBOSE=true;
        shift
        ;;
    esac
  done
}

parse_args "$@"

# --- Execution Wrapper ---
run_task() {
  local label="$1"
  local cmd="$2"

  if [[ "$VERBOSE" == "true" ]]; then
    # Use a subtle separator instead of a giant box
    echo -e "\n${TITLE_COLOR}--- [ RUNNING: $label ] ---${NC}"

    if eval "$cmd"; then
      log_success "$label completed successfully."
      echo -e "${TITLE_COLOR}----------------------------------------------------${NC}"
      return 0
    else
      log_error "$label failed."
      echo -e "${TITLE_COLOR}----------------------------------------------------${NC}"
      return 1
    fi
  else
    # Quiet Mode remains exactly the same
    echo -ne "  ${UNCHECK_COLOR}→${NC} ${label}... "
    local tmp_log
    tmp_log=$(mktemp)

    if [[ "$cmd" == *"sudo"* ]] && ! sudo -n true 2>/dev/null; then
      # Move cursor to the next line for the password prompt
      # and do not break the UI:
      echo ""
    fi

    if eval "$cmd" > "$tmp_log" 2>&1; then
      echo -e "${CHECK_COLOR}[OK]${NC}"
      rm "$tmp_log"
      return 0
    else
      echo -e "${ERR_COLOR}[FAILED]${NC}"
      log_error "Error details for $label:"
      sed 's/^/    /' "$tmp_log"
      rm "$tmp_log"
      return 1
    fi
  fi
}

# --- Force Reinstall Check ---
if [[ "$FORCE_REINSTALL" == "true" ]]; then
  echo -n "Are you sure you want to force reinstall of managed libraries? (y/N): "
  read -r confirm
  if [[ "$confirm" != "y" ]]; then
    log_info "Setup with reinstall aborted."
    exit 0
  fi
fi

# --- Registration Function ---
register_action() {
  local name="$1"
  local id="$2"
  local group="${3:-standalone}" # Default to standalone if not provided
  local func_id="${id//-/_}"

  # Format: name | flag | checker | installer | uninstaller | group
  TOOLS+=("$name|--install-$id|is_installed_$func_id|install_$func_id|uninstall_$func_id|$group")
}

# --- UI Helpers ---
print_titled_header() {
  local title_text="$1"
  echo -e "${TITLE_COLOR}────────────────────────────────────────────────────${NC}"
  printf "${TITLE_COLOR}%b${NC}\n" "$title_text"
  echo -e "${TITLE_COLOR}────────────────────────────────────────────────────${NC}"
}

log_action() {
  local action="$1"
  local name="$2"
  echo -e "\n${TITLE_COLOR}----------------------------------------------------${NC}"
  echo -e "${TITLE_COLOR}[${NC} ${WARN_COLOR}${action}${NC} ${TITLE_COLOR}]${NC} ${KEY_COLOR}${name}${NC}"
  echo -e "${TITLE_COLOR}----------------------------------------------------${NC}"
}

log_installing() {
  local name="$1"
  log_action "INSTALLING" "$1"
}

log_uninstalling() {
  local name="$1"
  log_action "UNINSTALLING" "$1"
}

# --- Other ---
sort_tools_by_group() {
  local sorted=()

  for entry in "${INSTALLER_GROUPS[@]}"; do
    IFS='|' read -r g_key _ <<< "$entry"

    for tool in "${TOOLS[@]}"; do
      IFS='|' read -r _ _ _ _ _ t_group <<< "$tool"

      if [[ "$t_group" == "$g_key" ]]; then
        sorted+=("$tool")
      fi
    done
  done

  # Catch-all for tools registered with a group not in INSTALLER_GROUPS
  for tool in "${TOOLS[@]}"; do
    IFS='|' read -r _ _ _ _ _ t_group <<< "$tool"
    local found=false
    for entry in "${INSTALLER_GROUPS[@]}"; do
      [[ "${entry%%|*}" == "$t_group" ]] && found=true && break
    done
    [[ "$found" == "false" ]] && sorted+=("$tool")
  done

  TOOLS=("${sorted[@]}")
}

get_group_title() {
  local search_key="$1"
  for entry in "${INSTALLER_GROUPS[@]}"; do
    if [[ "${entry%%|*}" == "$search_key" ]]; then
      echo "${entry#*|}"
      return
    fi
  done
  echo "$search_key"
}

get_os_family() {
  if [[ "$OSTYPE" == "darwin"* ]]; then
    echo "mac"
    return 0
  fi

  if [[ -f /etc/os-release ]]; then
    local os_id
    os_id=$(grep -i '^ID=' /etc/os-release | cut -d= -f2 | tr -d '"' | tr -d "'" | tr '[:upper:]' '[:lower:]')

    case "$os_id" in
      "fedora"|"rhel"|"centos") echo "fedora" ;;
      "ubuntu"|"debian"|"pop"|"mint") echo "debian-ubuntu" ;;
      "alpine") echo "alpine" ;;
      ,*) echo "$os_id" ;;
    esac
    return 0
  fi

  echo "unknown"
}

OS_FAMILY=$(get_os_family)

# --- Interactive Menu ---
menu_select_actions() {
  declare -n _indices=$1
  local cursor=0
  local selected=()
  local num_items=${#_indices[@]}

  for i in "${!_indices[@]}"; do selected[i]=0; done

  tput civis
  stty -echo -icanon isig
  clear
  trap "stty echo icanon; tput cnorm; exit" INT TERM EXIT

  while true; do
    tput cup 0 0
    local frame=""
    print_titled_header "Select the actions to perform"

    for i in "${!_indices[@]}"; do
      local idx=${_indices[$i]}
      IFS='|' read -r name _ _ _ _ <<< "${TOOLS[$idx]}"
      local pointer="  "
      local bracket="[ ]"
      [[ $i -eq $cursor ]] && pointer="${TITLE_COLOR}> ${NC}"
      [[ ${selected[$i]} -eq 1 ]] && bracket="${CHECK_COLOR}[✓]${NC}"

      if [[ $i -eq $cursor ]]; then
        frame+="  ${pointer}${bracket} ${TITLE_COLOR}${name}${NC}$(tput el)\n"
      else
        frame+="    ${bracket} ${name}$(tput el)\n"
      fi
    done

    frame+="${TITLE_COLOR}────────────────────────────────────────────────────${NC}\n"
    frame+=" $(printf "%b↑/↓/n/p%b Navigate  %bSPACE%b Toggle  %bENTER%b Run  %bA%b Abort" "${KEY_COLOR}" "${NC}" "${KEY_COLOR}" "${NC}" "${CHECK_COLOR}" "${NC}" "${ERR_COLOR}" "${NC}")\n"
    frame+="${TITLE_COLOR}────────────────────────────────────────────────────${NC}\n"
    frame+="$(tput ed)"

    echo -ne "$frame"

    # Input gatekeeper: wait for the first interaction
    local key
    if ! IFS= read -rsn1 key; then break; fi

    # Then we process the immediate key
    case "$key" in
      $'\e')
        read -rsn2 -t 0.01 seq
        [[ "$seq" == "[A" ]] && ((cursor = (cursor - 1 + num_items) % num_items))
        [[ "$seq" == "[B" ]] && ((cursor = (cursor + 1) % num_items))
        ;;
      " ") ((selected[cursor] ^= 1)) ;;
      "p") ((cursor = (cursor - 1 + num_items) % num_items)) ;;
      "n") ((cursor = (cursor + 1) % num_items)) ;;
      # The "Triple-Catch" for Enter:
      # 1. $'\x0a' (Line Feed)
      # 2. $'\x0d' (Carriage Return)
      # 3. "" (Empty string - often how read -n1 returns a newline)
      $'\x0a'|$'\x0d'|"") break ;;
      "a"|"A") stty echo icanon; tput cnorm; clear; exit 1 ;;
    esac

    # Below we drain the buffer and jump to the last key
    # We attempt to read a large chunk (100 chars) that accumulated
    # while the script was redrawing. This might happen when keeping
    # the key pressed and the keyboard sends repeated keys fast:
    local extra=""
    if IFS= read -rsn100 -t 0.01 extra; then
      # If the chunk contains arrow sequences, find the LAST one
      # and move the cursor there immediately.
      if [[ "$extra" == *$'\e[A'* ]]; then
        ((cursor = (cursor - 1 + num_items) % num_items))
      elif [[ "$extra" == *$'\e[B'* ]]; then
        ((cursor = (cursor + 1) % num_items))
      fi

      # Clear any toggles (spaces) that were in the burst
      local space_count
      space_count=$(echo "$extra" | tr -cd ' ' | wc -c)
      if (( space_count > 0 )); then
         # This part is tricky; usually we don't want to "burst" toggle
         # but we can toggle the current cursor if spaces were found.
         ((selected[cursor] ^= (space_count % 2)))
      fi

      # Immediately drop everything else in the hardware buffer.
      # This stops the "sliding" effect when pressing and holding the key.
      stty flush ioproc >/dev/null 2>&1
    fi

    # Giving the terminal some time to breath
    sleep 0.01
  done

  stty echo icanon
  tput cnorm
  clear

  MENU_SELECTED_INDICES=()
  for i in "${!selected[@]}"; do
    if [[ ${selected[$i]} -eq 1 ]]; then
      MENU_SELECTED_INDICES+=("${_indices[$i]}")
    fi
  done
}

# --- Logic Functions ---
build_menu_lists() {
  MENU_INDICES=()
  INSTALLED_ITEMS=()
  for i in "${!TOOLS[@]}"; do
    IFS='|' read -r name _ checker _ _ <<< "${TOOLS[$i]}"
    if [[ "$FORCE_REINSTALL" == "false" ]] && "$checker"; then
      INSTALLED_ITEMS+=("$name")
    else
      MENU_INDICES+=("$i")
    fi
  done
}

display_installed_tools() {
  if [ ${#INSTALLED_ITEMS[@]} -gt 0 ]; then
    log_warn "The following items are already handled and will be skipped:"
    for item in "${INSTALLED_ITEMS[@]}"; do
      echo -e " - ${UNCHECK_COLOR}$item${NC}"
    done
    echo ""
    read -n 1 -s -r -p "Press any key to continue..."
    echo ""
  fi
}

run_menu() {
  if [ ${#MENU_INDICES[@]} -eq 0 ]; then
    log_success "All tools are already installed."
    exit 0
  fi
  menu_select_actions MENU_INDICES
}

get_safe_key() {
  echo "$1" | xargs | tr '[:upper:]' '[:lower:]' | sed 's/+/p/g' | tr '-' '_'
}

register_system_package() {
  local friendly_name="$1"
  local internal_key
  internal_key=$(get_safe_key "$friendly_name")

  shift

  SYSTEM_PACKAGES_MAP["$internal_key"]+="${SYSTEM_PACKAGES_MAP["$internal_key"]:+ }$*"
}

get_registered_package_name() {
  local friendly_name="$1"
  local internal_key
  internal_key=$(get_safe_key "$friendly_name")
  local all_mappings="${SYSTEM_PACKAGES_MAP["$internal_key"]}"

  [[ -z "$all_mappings" ]] && return 1

  for mapping in $all_mappings; do
    if [[ "$mapping" == "$OS_FAMILY:"* ]]; then
      echo "${mapping#*:}"
      return 0
    fi
  done
  return 1
}

print_system_packages_map() {
  echo -e "${TITLE_COLOR}Current System Package Mappings:${NC}"
  echo "────────────────────────────────────────────────────"

  # Check if the map is empty first
  if [[ ${#SYSTEM_PACKAGES_MAP[@]} -eq 0 ]]; then
    echo "  (Map is empty. Ensure 'declare -A SYSTEM_PACKAGES_MAP' is at the top.)"
    return 1
  fi

  # Iterate through all keys in the associative array
  for key in "${!SYSTEM_PACKAGES_MAP[@]}"; do
    printf "  ${KEY_COLOR}%-20s${NC} | %s\n" "$key" "${SYSTEM_PACKAGES_MAP[$key]}"
  done

  echo "────────────────────────────────────────────────────"
}

is_system_package_installed() {
  local friendly_name="$1"
  local pkg_name

  # Let the helper handle the safe_key conversion and mapping
  pkg_name=$(get_registered_package_name "$friendly_name")
  [[ -z "$pkg_name" ]] && return 1

  case "$OS_FAMILY" in
    "fedora")
      rpm -q "$pkg_name" >/dev/null 2>&1 || \
      rpm -q --whatprovides "$pkg_name" >/dev/null 2>&1
      ;;
    "debian-ubuntu")
      dpkg-query -W -f='${Status}' "$pkg_name" 2>/dev/null | grep -q "ok installed"
      ;;
    "mac"|"linuxbrew")
      brew list "$pkg_name" >/dev/null 2>&1
      ;;
    "alpine")
      apk info -e "$pkg_name" >/dev/null 2>&1
      ;;
    ,*)
      return 1
      ;;
  esac
}

are_these_system_packages_installed() {
  for package in "$@"; do
    if ! is_system_package_installed "$package"; then
      return 1
    fi
  done

  return 0
}

install_system_package() {
  local friendly_name="$1"
  local os_label=""
  local cmd_prefix=""
  local distro_key=""

  if [[ "$OSTYPE" == "darwin"* ]]; then
    distro_key="mac"
    os_label="macOS (Homebrew)"
    cmd_prefix="brew install"
  elif [ -f /etc/debian_version ]; then
    distro_key="debian-ubuntu"
    os_label="Debian/Ubuntu"
    cmd_prefix="sudo DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive apt update && sudo apt install -y"
  elif [ -f /etc/fedora-release ] || command -v dnf >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    distro_key="fedora"
    os_label="Fedora/RHEL"
    cmd_prefix="sudo dnf install -y"
  elif [ -f /etc/alpine-release ] || command -v apk >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    distro_key="alpine"
    os_label="Alpine"
    cmd_prefix="sudo apk add"
  elif command -v brew >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    # This handles Homebrew on Linux if no other match was found
    distro_key="linuxbrew"
    os_label="Linux (Homebrew)"
    cmd_prefix="brew install"
  else
    log_error "OS not supported for auto-installation."
    return 1
  fi

  local pkg_to_install
  pkg_to_install=$(get_registered_package_name "$friendly_name")

  if [ -z "$pkg_to_install" ]; then
    log_error "Package '$friendly_name' not found in setup for $os_label."
    return 1
  fi

  if [ -z "$pkg_to_install" ]; then
    log_error "Package '$friendly_name' not found in setup for $os_label."
    return 1
  fi

  local final_cmd="$cmd_prefix $pkg_to_install"

  if [[ "$FORCE_REINSTALL" == "true" ]]; then
    case "$distro_key" in
      debian-ubuntu)
        final_cmd="$cmd_prefix --reinstall $pkg_to_install"
        ;;
      fedora|mac|linuxbrew)
        final_cmd="${cmd_prefix/install/reinstall} $pkg_to_install"
        ;;
      alpine)
        final_cmd="$cmd_prefix --force-reinstall $pkg_to_install"
        ;;
    esac
  fi

  run_task "System: Installing $friendly_name ($os_label)" "$final_cmd"
}

install_system_packages() {
  for package in "$@"; do
    if ! is_system_package_installed "$package" || [[ "$FORCE_REINSTALL" == "true" ]]; then
      install_system_package "$package"
    else
      log_skip "$package" "ALREADY INSTALLED"
    fi
  done
}

register_shell_path() {
  local line="$1"
  local shell_files=("$HOME/.zshrc" "$HOME/.bashrc")
  local changed=false

  for file in "${shell_files[@]}"; do
    if [ -f "$file" ]; then
      if ! grep -qF "$line" "$file"; then
        echo "$line" >> "$file"
        changed=true
      fi
    fi
  done

  if [ "$changed" = true ]; then
    log_success "Shell environment updated."
  else
    log_skip "Shell environment" "ALREADY SET"
  fi
}

register_system_package "pipx" \
                        "alpine:pipx" \
                        "fedora:pipx" \
                        "linuxbrew:pipx" \
                        "mac:pipx" \
                        "debian-ubuntu:pipx"

install_pipx() {
  if ! command -v pipx >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    install_system_packages "pipx"

    run_task "Configuring pipx path" "export PATH=\"\$PATH:\$HOME/.local/bin\""
    # shellcheck disable=SC2016
    register_shell_path 'export PATH="$PATH:$HOME/.local/bin"'
  else
    log_skip "Pipx" "EXISTS"
  fi
}

ensure_pipx_available() {
  if command -v pipx >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    return 0
  fi

  log_warn "pipx not found in PATH. Initiating installation..."
  install_pipx

  # Refresh PATH for the current session immediately
  export PATH="$PATH:$HOME/.local/bin"

  # Final verification
  if command -v pipx >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    log_success "pipx is now ready to use."
    return 0
  else
    log_error "Critical error: pipx could not be found after installation."
    return 1
  fi
}

install_via_pipx() {
  local friendly_name="$1"
  local package="$2"

  if ensure_pipx_available; then
    run_task "$friendly_name via pipx" "pipx install $package"
  else
    log_error "Installation of $package skipped because pipx is unavailable."
    return 1
  fi
}

execute_uninstallers() {
  [[ ${#MENU_SELECTED_INDICES[@]} -eq 0 ]] && return 0

  print_titled_header "Starting Cleanup Phase"
  LAST_GROUP_PRINTED=""

  for idx in "${MENU_SELECTED_INDICES[@]}"; do
    IFS='|' read -r name _ checker installer uninstaller group <<< "${TOOLS[$idx]}"

    if [[ "$group" != "$LAST_GROUP_PRINTED" ]]; then
      local group_title
      group_title=$(get_group_title "$group")
      log_uninstalling "$group_title"
      LAST_GROUP_PRINTED="$group"
    fi

    if declare -f "$uninstaller" > /dev/null; then
      $uninstaller
    fi
  done
  echo ""
}

execute_installers() {
  [[ ${#MENU_SELECTED_INDICES[@]} -eq 0 ]] && return 0

  print_titled_header "Starting Installation Phase"
  LAST_GROUP_PRINTED=""

  for idx in "${MENU_SELECTED_INDICES[@]}"; do
    IFS='|' read -r name _ checker installer uninstaller group <<< "${TOOLS[$idx]}"

    if [[ "$group" != "$LAST_GROUP_PRINTED" ]]; then
      # We use the helper function to find the friendly name in our unified array
      local group_title
      group_title=$(get_group_title "$group")

      log_installing "$group_title"
      LAST_GROUP_PRINTED="$group"
    fi

    if declare -f "$installer" > /dev/null; then
      $installer
    else
      log_error "Installer function $installer not found."
    fi
  done

  echo ""
  log_success "All selected tasks completed."
}
#+end_src

Multiple tools we use to support our development flow are based on =Node.js=.
They are all installed together by the functions below.

#+begin_src sh :tangle setup.sh
NVM_VERSION="v0.40.4"
NODE_TARGET_VERSION="22.22.1"
TOOLS_DIR="$HOME/.emacs.d/nodejs_tools"

load_nvm() {
  export NVM_DIR="$HOME/.nvm"
  # shellcheck disable=SC1091
  [ -s "$NVM_DIR/nvm.sh" ] && \. "$NVM_DIR/nvm.sh"
}

is_installed_nvm() {
  [ -d "$HOME/.nvm" ]
}

install_nvm() {
  if ! is_installed_nvm; then
    run_task "Installing NVM ($NVM_VERSION)" "curl -sL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/nvm-sh/nvm/${NVM_VERSION}/install.sh | bash"
    load_nvm
  else
    load_nvm
    # Use our new skip helper instead of log_info
    log_skip "NVM" "ACTIVE"
  fi
}

ensure_nvm_available() {
  load_nvm
  if ! command -v nvm >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    install_nvm
  fi
  command -v nvm >/dev/null 2>&1
}

ARE_NODEJS_TOOLS_INSTALLED=false
ARE_NODEJS_TOOLS_UNINSTALLED=false
install_nodejs_tools() {
  [[ "$ARE_NODEJS_TOOLS_INSTALLED" == "true" ]] && return 0

  ensure_nvm_available || { log_error "NVM bootstrap failed"; return 1; }

  run_task "Node.js $NODE_TARGET_VERSION" "nvm install $NODE_TARGET_VERSION" || return 1

  if [ -d "$TOOLS_DIR" ]; then
    run_task "Updating node_modules" "cd $TOOLS_DIR && nvm exec $NODE_TARGET_VERSION npm install"
  else
    log_error "Tools directory missing: $TOOLS_DIR"
    return 1
  fi

  # shellcheck disable=SC2034
  ARE_NODEJS_TOOLS_UUNINSTALLED=false
  ARE_NODEJS_TOOLS_INSTALLED=true
}

uninstall_nodejs_tools() {
  [[ "$ARE_NODEJS_TOOLS_UNINSTALLED" == "true" ]] && return 0

  if [ -d "$TOOLS_DIR/node_modules" ]; then
    run_task "Node.js toolchain: Removing node_modules" "rm -Rf $TOOLS_DIR/node_modules"
  else
    log_skip "Node.js toolchain (node_modules)" "NOT FOUND"
  fi

  ARE_NODEJS_TOOLS_INSTALLED=false
  ARE_NODEJS_TOOLS_UNINSTALLED=true
}
#+end_src

When there is no =init.el=, we must initialize it from the bootstrap file:

#+begin_src sh :tangle setup.sh
if [ ! -f "./init.el" ]; then
  print_titled_header "Bootstraping init.el..."
  cp -v "init-bootstrap.el" "init.el"
fi
#+end_src


** Storing Secrets

We use the the Emacs built-in ~auth-source~ package to securely retrieve API keys from
encrypted storage. For several cases, we set them as environment variables for later
usage.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package auth-source
  :ensure nil  ; It's built-in, don't download it
  :config
  ;; Tell Emacs to look at your encrypted file first
  (setq auth-sources '("~/.authinfo.gpg")))
#+end_src

In =~/.authinfo.gpg=, a GPG-encrypted file storing your credentials, you'd have
entries like the following:

#+begin_example
machine api.anthropic.com login apikey password YOUR_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
machine api.openai.com login apikey password YOUR_OPENAI_API_KEY
#+end_example

The example above shows how to store API keys. In this case, the "login" is "apikey"
and the  password is the API key. This approach keeps API keys encrypted in
=~/.authinfo.gpg= and avoids hardcoding them in your configuration files.

If you do not have a GPG-encrypted =~/.authinfo.gpg= file, follow these steps:

1. **Generate or use an existing GPG key**:
   - If you don't have a GPG key, generate one with: =gpg --full-generate-key=
   - You'll need to provide a name, email, and optionally a passphrase
   - Note: If your GPG key has no passphrase, decryption will be transparent
     on your machine (no password prompt), which is convenient but less secure
     if someone gains access to your machine.

2. **Create the authinfo file**:
   - Create a plain text file =~/.authinfo= with your credentials (API keys):

#+begin_example
machine api.anthropic.com login apikey password YOUR_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
machine api.openai.com login apikey password YOUR_OPENAI_API_KEY
machine api.mistral.ai login apikey password YOUR_MISTRAL_API_KEY
#+end_example

3. **Encrypt the file with GPG**:
   - Encrypt the file: =gpg --encrypt --recipient YOUR_EMAIL ~/.authinfo=
   - This creates =~/.authinfo.gpg= (the encrypted version)
   - Delete the plain text file: =rm ~/.authinfo= (for security)

4. **Editing the encrypted file in Emacs**:
   - Emacs can transparently decrypt and edit GPG-encrypted files
   - Simply open =~/.authinfo.gpg= in Emacs: =C-x C-f ~/.authinfo.gpg=
   - Emacs will prompt for your GPG passphrase (if set) and decrypt the file
   - After editing, save with =C-x C-s= and Emacs will automatically re-encrypt
   - The ~epa-file~ package (built into Emacs) handles this automatically

To automate this process, you can run our setup script:

#+begin_src sh
bash setup.sh
#+end_src

This will interactively prompt you for your API keys (GitHub, Gemini, Groq, DeepSeek)
and update your =~/.authinfo.gpg= securely, preserving any existing entries.

The secrets setup functions are defined below:

#+begin_src sh :tangle setup.sh
is_installed_list_of_secrets() {
  [ -f "$HOME/.authinfo.gpg" ]
}

install_list_of_secrets() {
  AUTH_FILE="$HOME/.authinfo.gpg"
  TEMP_FILE=$(mktemp)
  chmod 600 "$TEMP_FILE"

  if [ -f "$AUTH_FILE" ]; then
    if ! run_task "Decrypting existing credentials" "gpg --quiet --decrypt $AUTH_FILE > $TEMP_FILE"; then
      log_error "Decryption failed. Aborting to prevent data loss."
      rm "$TEMP_FILE"
      return 1
    fi
  else
    run_task "Initializing new vault" "touch $TEMP_FILE"
  fi

  echo -e "\n${KEY_COLOR}[*]${NC} Setting up AI API Tokens"
  echo -e "    (Vault: $AUTH_FILE)\n"

  update_key() {
    local machine=$1
    local user=$2
    local name=$3
    local key

    read -rs -p "  Enter $name API Key (leave empty to keep existing): " key
    echo ""

    if [ -n "$key" ]; then
      sed -i "/machine $machine login $user/d" "$TEMP_FILE"
      echo "machine $machine login $user password $key" >> "$TEMP_FILE"
      log_success "Updated $name"
    else
      log_info "Skipping $name (unchanged)"
    fi
  }

  update_key "api.github.com" "apikey" "GitHub Copilot"
  update_key "generativelanguage.googleapis.com" "apikey" "Google Gemini"
  update_key "api.groq.com" "apikey" "Groq"
  update_key "api.deepseek.com" "apikey" "DeepSeek"
  update_key "api.openai.com" "apikey" "OpenAI"

  echo ""
  RECIPIENT=$(whoami)

  run_task "Encrypting vault" "gpg --yes --quiet --batch --encrypt --recipient $RECIPIENT --output $AUTH_FILE $TEMP_FILE"

  run_task "Securely cleaning temporary data" "shred -u $TEMP_FILE 2>/dev/null || rm $TEMP_FILE"
}

uninstall_list_of_secrets() {
  log_skip "Encrypted secrets vault"
}

register_action "Setup encrypted authinfo" "list-of-secrets" "secrets"
#+end_src


** Emacs socket and server settings

When running Emacs as a daemon using =emacs --daemon=, the ~(server-start)~ command is
triggered implicitly, allowing the fast client, =emacsclient=, to connect. For remote
or containerized environments (like Docker), the default local file socket often
creates challenges with network exposure and file permissions. We must configure a
reliable server connection method.

*** Option 1: TCP/IP Socket (For Direct Network Exposure)

This approach is best if you want the Emacs server to listen on a network interface,
which is useful when running Emacs inside a Docker container that is exposed via
port forwarding or an SSH tunnel.

#+begin_example emacs-lisp
;; Configure the Emacs server to listen on a TCP socket
(setq server-use-tcp t)

;; Define the specific port the Emacs daemon should listen on inside the container
(setq server-port 4000)

;; Define the host interface to listen on. Setting this to "127.0.0.1" (localhost)
;; inside the container provides enhanced security, as Emacs will only accept
;; connections originating from within the container itself. This relies on the
;; external SSH tunnel to securely forward traffic to this local address.
(setq server-host "127.0.0.1")
#+end_example


*** Option 2: File Socket Directory (For Simpler SSH Forwarding)

Using a dedicated socket file location is often simpler for secure connections over
SSH, especially when using tools like =ssh -L=. This method is preferred because it
bypasses the common challenge of file permission issues associated with the default
socket location (usually in the user's home directory), which might be inaccessible
to the =sshd= process or other services. We address this directly by moving the
socket to the universally accessible =/tmp= directory, which centralizes its location
and ensures accessibility. Ultimately, given its ease of use for remote access, we
opted for this file socket approach in our configuration:

#+begin_src emacs-lisp :tangle init.el
(setq server-socket-dir "/tmp/emacs-server")
#+end_src


** Managing the PATH variable

This configuration ensures Emacs inherits your shell’s environment variables
(such as =$PATH=) when launched graphically.

By default, graphical Emacs starts independently of your terminal, which often
leads to "command not found" errors because it doesn't know where to look for
tools installed via managers like SDKMAN! or Homebrew. This snippet synchronizes
your environment, ensuring Emacs can correctly locate your compilers, language
servers, and build tools.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package exec-path-from-shell
  :ensure t ; Needed because "use-package-always-ensure" is not yet configured at this point
  :config
  ;; 1. Disable the "interactive" flag (-i) which causes most hangs on Linux
  (setq exec-path-from-shell-arguments '("-l"))
  ;; Sync environment variables only when running in a graphical interface
  (when (memq window-system '(mac ns x))
    (exec-path-from-shell-initialize)))
#+end_src

We also add our own custom =nodejs_tools= directory to the =PATH= variable, to
ensure that language servers and formatters installed locally are found
throughout the configuration:

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(with-eval-after-load 'exec-path-from-shell
  (let ((local-node-bin (expand-file-name "nodejs_tools/node_modules/.bin" user-emacs-directory)))
    (when (file-directory-p local-node-bin)
      (add-to-list 'exec-path local-node-bin)
      (setenv "PATH" (concat local-node-bin path-separator (getenv "PATH"))))))
#+end_src


** General interface tweaks

Minimal startup + UI tweaks: quiet boot, 120-col default (80 for Org),
y/n prompts, visible bell, instant which-key, and no GUI
chrome. ~which-key-mode~ displays available key bindings after an
incomplete command.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
;; Line limits
(setq-default fill-column 120)
(add-hook 'org-mode-hook (lambda ()
                           (setq-local fill-column 80)
                           (auto-fill-mode 1)))

;; Cursor settings
(setq-default cursor-type 'bar)

;; Other useful mode-specific settings
(dolist (hook '(conf-unix-mode-hook
                markdown-mode-hook
                nxml-mode-hook
                org-mode-hook
                prog-mode-hook
                text-mode-hook
                yaml-ts-mode-hook))
  (add-hook hook 'column-number-mode) ; Turn on column number display
  (add-hook hook 'display-line-numbers-mode) ; Turn on line numbers
  (add-hook hook (lambda () (setq-local show-trailing-whitespace t)))) ; Show trailing space

;; Highlighting current line
(global-hl-line-mode 1)
;; (custom-set-faces
;;   '(line-number-current-line ((t (:inherit line-number :background "#3B4252" :foreground "#88C0D0" :weight bold)))))
;; (custom-set-faces
;;   '(hl-line ((t (:background "#3B4252" :extend t)))))

;; Only auto-fill inside comments
(setq-default comment-auto-fill-only t)

(add-hook 'prog-mode-hook
          (lambda ()
            (setq-local comment-auto-fill-only t)
            (auto-fill-mode 1)))

;; Indent and tab characters
(setq-default whitespace-style '(face tabs tab-mark))
(define-key custom-bindings-map (kbd "S-SPC") (lambda () (interactive) (insert "\t")))
(global-whitespace-mode 1)
(setq-default whitespace-display-mappings
              '((tab-mark 9 [8594 32] [92 9])))

;; Other tweaks
(fset 'yes-or-no-p 'y-or-n-p)
(menu-bar-mode -1)
(scroll-bar-mode -1)
(set-fringe-mode 10)
(tool-bar-mode -1)
(which-key-mode)
(global-visual-line-mode 1)
#+end_src


** Font and theme

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(when (member "Roboto Mono" (font-family-list))
  (set-face-attribute 'default nil :font "Roboto Mono" :height 108)
  (set-face-attribute 'fixed-pitch nil :family "Roboto Mono"))

(when (member "Source Sans Pro" (font-family-list))
  (set-face-attribute 'variable-pitch nil :family "Source Sans Pro" :height 1.18))

** Interactive DO settings

IDO (Interactively DO things) replaces many minibuffer prompts with a
compact interactive matcher that narrows candidates as you type. These
options tune its behavior:

- ~ido-enable-flex-matching~ - allow substring and flexible matching
(not only prefix matches). Type parts of a name in any order and IDO
will still find it.
- ~ido-everywhere~ - use IDO's interface for most buffer/file
minibuffer prompts (where sensible), giving a consistent
completion experience across commands.
- ~(ido-mode 1)~ - enable IDO globally.

IDO is built into Emacs, lightweight, and improves the speed of file
and buffer selection without external dependencies.

However, not all commands use IDO by default. The package
[[ido-completing-read+]] provides a more powerful interface for
completing read commands using IDO.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(setq-default ido-enable-flex-matching t) ; better string matching on search
(setq-default ido-everywhere t)           ; toggle use of Ido for all buffer/file reading
(ido-mode 1)

(use-package ido-completing-read+
  ;; "defer" is needed to prevent errors during initialization in some systems
  :defer t
  :config
  (declare-function ido-ubiquitous-mode "ido-completing-read+")
  (ido-ubiquitous-mode 1))
#+end_src


** Custom key bindings

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(defun reload-init-file ()
  "Reload the Emacs configuration file."
  (interactive)
  (load-file user-init-file)
  (message "Emacs configuration reloaded!"))

(global-set-key (kbd "C-c M-r") 'reload-init-file)
(global-set-key (kbd "C-c r") 'revert-buffer) ;; Reload the current buffer from its backing file.
#+end_src


** Focus mode

Focus mode dims all text except for the current paragraph or line,
helping you concentrate on the task at hand.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package focus
  ;; "defer" is needed to prevent errors during initialization in some systems
  :defer t
  :config
  (declare-function focus-mode "focus")
  (focus-mode 1))
#+end_src


** Smart tweaks

The following navigation tweaks turn =C-a=/=HOME= and =C-e=/=END= into smart
toggles, prioritizing your actual code content over absolute line boundaries.

- =C-a=/=HOME= (Beginning) - The first press stops at the first non-whitespace
  character (the indentation level). A second press moves you to the absolute
  start of the line (column 0).
- =C-e=/=END= (End): - The first press stops at the end of your text content. A
  second press moves you to the absolute physical end of the line, which is
  particularly useful if your text wraps.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(defun smart-beginning-of-line ()
  "Move between the first non-whitespace character and beginning of line."
  (interactive)
  (let ((old-pos (point)))
    (back-to-indentation)
    (when (= old-pos (point))
      (beginning-of-line))))

(defun smart-end-of-line ()
  "Move between after the last non-whitespace character and end of line."
  (interactive)
  (let ((eol (save-excursion (end-of-line) (point)))
        (eot (save-excursion (end-of-line) (skip-syntax-backward " \t") (point))))
    (if (= (point) eot)
        (goto-char eol)
      (goto-char eot))))

(global-set-key (kbd "C-a") #'smart-beginning-of-line)
(global-set-key (kbd "<home>") #'smart-beginning-of-line)
(global-set-key (kbd "C-e") #'smart-end-of-line)
(global-set-key (kbd "<end>") #'smart-end-of-line)
#+end_src


** Other configurations

The following variables are global:

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
;; Replacements for the classic buffer list
;; (defalias 'list-buffers 'ibuffer) ;; opens it in the same window
(defalias 'list-buffers 'ibuffer-other-window) ;; opens it in another window

;; Ensure newline at end of file
(setq require-final-newline t)

;; Set UTF-8 as default encoding
(set-language-environment "UTF-8")
(prefer-coding-system 'utf-8)

;; The following variables are global:
(setq auto-revert-interval 1          ; Refresh buffers faster
      create-lockfiles nil            ; Disable creation of lockfiles
      echo-keystrokes 0.1             ; Show keystrokes faster
      enable-recursive-minibuffers t  ; Allow recursive minibuffers
      frame-inhibit-implied-resize 1  ; Don't resize frame implicitly
      history-delete-duplicates t     ; Delete duplicates in M-x history log
      inhibit-startup-screen t        ; Disables splash screen
      initial-scratch-message nil     ; Clean scratch buffer
      ring-bell-function 'ignore      ; Quiet
      scroll-margin 1                 ; Space between cursor and top/bottom
      sentence-end-double-space nil   ; No double space
      visible-bell t                  ; Visual flash instead of audible bell
      )

;; The following variables are buffer-local and require setq-default
;; to prevent warnings with assignment to free variables:
(setq-default recentf-max-saved-items 10000   ; Show more recent files
              which-key-idle-delay 0          ; Show which-key immediately when an incomplete key sequence is entered
              )

;; Saving Emacs backups and auto-save files in a different place
(setq backup-directory-alist
      `((".*" . ,(expand-file-name "backups/" user-emacs-directory))))
(make-directory (expand-file-name "auto-save-list/" user-emacs-directory) t)
(setq auto-save-file-name-transforms
      `((".*" ,(expand-file-name "auto-save-list/" user-emacs-directory) t)))

;; Prevent mac-bindings from interfering with Emacs bindings:
(when (boundp 'mac-pass-command-to-system)
  (setq mac-pass-command-to-system nil))
#+end_src

Other variables are buffer-local by default, but we can set their
default values using ~setq-default~ as follows:

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(setq-default split-width-threshold 160           ; Split verticly by default
              split-height-threshold nil          ; Split verticly by default
              frame-resize-pixelwise t            ; Fine-grained frame resize
              ;; auto-fill-function 'do-auto-fill ; Auto-fill-mode everywhere
              )
#+end_src

As for the indentation settings, we can create a function that
applies our preferred defaults and attach it to the hooks of the
relevant modes. This way, we ensure that our settings are applied
even if a mode overrides them later. Note that these settings are
intended to be overridden by ~editorconfig-mode~, if in use.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(defvar sh-basic-offset)
(defvar sh-indentation)
(defvar c-basic-offset)
(defvar java-ts-mode-indent-offset)
(defvar js-indent-level)
(defvar typescript-indent-level)
(defvar css-indent-offset)
(defvar python-indent-offset)
(defvar nxml-child-indent)
(defvar coffee-tab-width)

;; Override java-ts-mode default (4) before any buffer is created.
;; This is needed because java-ts-mode uses defcustom for java-ts-mode-indent-offset
;; with a default of 4. When the mode initializes, it reads the global default -- not
;; the buffer-local value set by hooks. Hooks like prog-mode-hook run after mode
;; initialization, but setq only sets the buffer-local value, which gets overwritten
;; by the mode's own defvar/defcustom default during java-ts-mode setup.
(setq-default java-ts-mode-indent-offset 2)

(defun my/default-indentation ()
  "Apply my preferred 2-space indentation settings across modes.
These settings are intended to be overridden by `editorconfig-mode'."
  ;; **CRITICAL CHECK:** Org mode uses a tab width of 8 by default.
  ;; Changing that leads to an error:
  (unless (eq major-mode 'org-mode)
    (setq
     ;; Global basics
     tab-width 2
     indent-tabs-mode nil
     standard-indent 2

     ;; Language-specific offsets
     sh-basic-offset 2
     sh-indentation 2
     c-basic-offset 2
     java-ts-mode-indent-offset 2
     js-indent-level 2
     typescript-indent-level 2
     css-indent-offset 2
     python-indent-offset 2
     nxml-child-indent 2
     coffee-tab-width 2)))

(dolist (hook '(conf-unix-mode-hook
                java-ts-mode-hook
                markdown-mode-hook
                nxml-mode-hook
                prog-mode-hook
                text-mode-hook
                yaml-ts-mode-hook))
  (add-hook hook #'my/default-indentation))
#+end_src

Finally, automatically revert DocView buffers when the file changes on
disk. Those are Emacs buffers shown when you open a non-text document
(PDF, PostScript, DjVu, etc.) with Emacs' built-in DocView mode. Emacs
renders document pages into images and displays those images inside a
special major mode (DocView mode) so you can read and navigate the
document inside Emacs.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(add-hook 'doc-view-mode-hook 'auto-revert-mode)
#+end_src


* Emacs hacks

** Faster startup

A common startup speed-up is to disable the garbage collection during
Emacs initialization, and enable it back after it. The code below
temporarily raises ~gc-cons-threshold~ to an extremely large value and
clears file.

A common startup speed-up is to defer garbage collection and file-name
handler processing while Emacs initializes. The code below temporarily
raises ~gc-cons-threshold~ to an extremely large value and clears
file-name-handler-alist before init so GC cycles and file-handler
overhead are avoided during startup. It then restores the original
file-name-handler-alist and a reasonable gc-cons-threshold in an
emacs-startup-hook after initialization completes. Finally,
~inhibit-default-init~ prevents loading your environment's default
global settings together with your init file (adapted from
[[https://github.com/larstvei/dot-emacs/blob/main/init.org][the dot-emacs-repository]]).

#+begin_src emacs-lisp :tangle early-init.el
(let ((file-name-handler-alist-old file-name-handler-alist))
  (add-hook 'emacs-startup-hook
            (lambda ()
              (setq gc-cons-threshold (* 1024 1024 20))
              (setq file-name-handler-alist file-name-handler-alist-old)))
  (setq gc-cons-threshold most-positive-fixnum)
  (setq file-name-handler-alist nil))

(setq inhibit-default-init t)
#+end_src


** Resize issue on certain terminals

When moving a terminal window between monitors or resizing it quickly,
Emacs running in a tty can briefly see inconsistent size or DPI info.
That can make Emacs compute the wrong number of rows/columns and push
lines or UI elements off-screen even though the terminal already grew.

The root causes are timing and handshake differences between the
terminal emulator and Emacs (TERM or DPI changes and delayed resize
notifications). The terminal may update geometry after Emacs has
already calculated layout.

The workaround forces an immediate redisplay, a gentle recenter, and a
few retry attempts after short delays; as a last resort it re-applies
the frame size. These retries catch late-arriving terminal updates and
bring Emacs' view into alignment with the terminal once the emulator
finishes reporting geometry.

The code only runs for non-graphical (tty) sessions and uses safe
ignore-errors wrappers, so risk is low.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
;; Resize fixes and logging (preserved)
(defun my/tty-resize-fix (&rest _args)
  "Robustly fix terminal/frame resize artifacts.
When running in a terminal, some resize events (DPI/monitor switch)
leave the UI in a bad state. Try several redisplay/recenter attempts
and a safe frame-size reapply as a fallback.

This is intentionally conservative (ignore-errors) and only runs when
display is non-graphical, so it is safe for GUI Emacs too."
  (interactive)
  (let ((dap-active (and (fboundp 'dap--cur-session) (dap--cur-session))))
    (if dap-active
        ;; If DAP is active, EXIT immediately.
        ;; This prevents it from running unnecessary timers when debugging.
        nil

      ;; Otherwise, proceed with the original logic
      (ignore-errors
        (when (not (display-graphic-p))
          (redisplay)
          (run-with-idle-timer 0.03 nil (lambda () (redisplay)))
          (run-with-idle-timer 0.07 nil (lambda () (redisplay) (ignore-errors (recenter 0))))
          (run-with-idle-timer 0.15 nil
                               (lambda ()
                                 (redisplay)
                                 (ignore-errors (recenter 0))
                                 (when (fboundp 'set-frame-size)
                                   (let ((fw (frame-width)) (fh (frame-height)))
                                     (set-frame-size (selected-frame) fw fh))))))))))

(add-hook 'window-size-change-functions #'my/tty-resize-fix)
(add-hook 'frame-size-change-functions #'my/tty-resize-fix)
(add-hook 'window-configuration-change-hook #'my/tty-resize-fix)
#+end_src


** TRAMP configuration

TRAMP (Transparent Remote Access, Multiple Protocol) is a built-in Emacs library
that lets you edit files on remote systems-like servers or Docker containers as
if they were on your local machine. It handles the underlying connections (via
SSH, Docker, etc.) automatically, letting you open and edit remote files
directly without needing to manually sync or download them.

Emacs is designed with the assumption that your filesystem is local and
instantaneous, but when you introduce TRAMP and Docker, every directory probe
becomes a network-bound request that can easily overwhelm your connection. There
might be crashes resulting of this "startup storm," where aggressive LSP clients
flood the network with filesystem checks before the TRAMP handshake is even
complete, triggering race conditions and file-missing errors within your custom
project context logic. By applying "remote-safe" restrictions, we shift our
environment from an eager, "probe-everything-now" model to an on-demand,
lazy-loading architecture; this synchronizes Emacs' demands with the realities
of network latency.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(defun my/lsp-remote-safety-hook ()
  "Apply restrictive LSP settings only when working on TRAMP/remote buffers."
  (declare-function tramp-tramp-file-p "tramp")
  (when (tramp-tramp-file-p default-directory)
    ;; Kill the heavy watchers that cause TRAMP file-missing errors
    (setq-local lsp-enable-file-watchers nil)

    ;; Stop LSP from trying to guess the root, which causes directory probes
    (setq-local lsp-auto-guess-root nil)

    (message "LSP: Applied remote-safe settings to %s" (buffer-name))))

;; Trigger this whenever a buffer enters lsp-mode
(add-hook 'lsp-mode-hook #'my/lsp-remote-safety-hook)
#+end_src


* Packages initial setup

Starting with Emacs 29, [[https://github.com/jwiegley/use-package][use-package]] is bundled with Emacs as an
[[https://lists.gnu.org/archive/html/emacs-devel/2022-12/msg00261.html][official part of the distribution]]. That means you can ~require~ it
directly on Emacs 29+. However, this configuration retains the
traditional bootstrap/install steps to preserve compatibility with
older Emacs versions.

In addition to that, the GPG key used to sign the GNU ELPA archives
might be expired and requires update, leading to signature errors
when installing packages in some systems. One easy way to keep this
key updated is through the package ~gnu-elpa-keyring-update~, which
is installed below.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(require 'package)
(setq package-enable-at-startup nil)

;; Configure package directories to use user's elpa directory
(setq package-user-dir (expand-file-name "elpa" user-emacs-directory))
(add-to-list 'package-directory-list package-user-dir)

(setq package-archives
      '(("MELPA Stable"   . "https://stable.melpa.org/packages/")
        ("GNU ELPA"       . "https://elpa.gnu.org/packages/")
        ("MELPA Unstable" . "https://melpa.org/packages/")
        ("Non-GNU"        . "https://elpa.nongnu.org/nongnu/")
        ("GNU-devel"      . "https://elpa.gnu.org/devel/")
        ("JCS-ELPA"       . "https://jcs-emacs.github.io/jcs-elpa/packages/"))

      package-archive-priorities
      '(("MELPA Stable"   . 25)
        ("GNU ELPA"       . 20)
        ("MELPA Unstable" . 15)
        ("Non-GNU"        . 10)
        ("GNU-devel"      . 5)
        ("JCS-ELPA"       . 0)))

(package-initialize)
(unless 'package-archive-results
  (package-refresh-contents))

(unless (package-installed-p 'use-package)
  (package-refresh-contents)
  (package-install 'use-package))

(unless (package-installed-p 'gnu-elpa-keyring-update)
  (package-install 'gnu-elpa-keyring-update))

(require 'gnu-elpa-keyring-update)
(require 'use-package)
(setq use-package-always-ensure t)
(setq use-package-compute-statistics t)
#+end_src

** Themes

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package doom-themes
  :pin "MELPA Unstable"
  :custom
  ;; Global settings (defaults)
  (doom-themes-enable-bold t)   ; if nil, bold is universally disabled
  (doom-themes-enable-italic t) ; if nil, italics is universally disabled
  ;; for treemacs users
  ;; (doom-themes-treemacs-theme "doom-nova") ; use "doom-colors" for less minimal icon theme
  :config
  (load-theme 'doom-one t) ; See also doom-nord or doom-nord-light

  ;; (custom-set-faces
  ;;   '(org-block ((t (:background "#ff0000")))))

  ;; Enable flashing mode-line on errors
  ;; (doom-themes-visual-bell-config)
  ;; For treemacs users
  ;; (doom-themes-treemacs-config)
  ;; Corrects (and improves) org-mode's native fontification.
  ;; Ensure we only call `doom-themes-org-config' when it's defined. This
  ;; prevents native-compiler warnings and avoids trying to call the
  ;; function before the package has been loaded.
  (cond
   ((fboundp 'doom-themes-org-config)
    (doom-themes-org-config))
   (t
    (with-eval-after-load 'doom-themes
      (when (fboundp 'doom-themes-org-config)
        (doom-themes-org-config))))))

;; We are using All Icons, but doom-modeline requires Nerd Icons for some parts.

    (nerd-icons-install-fonts t)))

(use-package doom-modeline
  :after all-the-icons nerd-icons
  :init (doom-modeline-mode 1)

** Tabs

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(declare-function centaur-tabs-2str "centaur-tabs")
(declare-function project-root "project")

(use-package centaur-tabs
  :pin "MELPA Unstable"
  :ensure
  :demand
  :after all-the-icons
  :config
  (centaur-tabs-mode t)
  (setq centaur-tabs-set-icons t
        centaur-tabs-icon-type 'all-the-icons   ;; use all-the-icons, not nerd-icons
        centaur-tabs-plain-icons t
        centaur-tabs-height 32
        centaur-tabs-style "bar"
        centaur-tabs-gray-out-icons 'buffer
        centaur-tabs-set-bar 'over
        centaur-tabs-set-modified-marker t
        centaur-tabs-adjust-buffer-order t)

  ;; Group tabs by project
  (defun centaur-tabs-buffer-groups ()
    "Organize tabs into groups by project."
    (list
     (cond
      ((derived-mode-p 'eshell-mode 'term-mode 'shell-mode 'vterm-mode)
       "Terminal")
      ((string-prefix-p "*" (buffer-name))
       "System")
      ((memq major-mode '(magit-process-mode
                          magit-status-mode
                          magit-diff-mode
                          magit-log-mode
                          magit-file-mode
                          magit-blob-mode
                          magit-blame-mode))
       "Magit")
      ((derived-mode-p 'dired-mode)
       "Dired")
      ((file-remote-p default-directory)
       "Remote")
      ((project-current)
       (format "Project: %s" (file-name-nondirectory
                              (directory-file-name
                               (project-root (project-current))))))
      (t "Default"))))

  (defun centaur-tabs-hide-tab (x)
    "Do no to show buffer X in tabs."
    (let ((name (format "%s" x)))
      (or
       ;; Current window is not dedicated window.
       (window-dedicated-p (selected-window))

       ;; Buffer name not match below blacklist.
       (string-prefix-p "*epc" name)
       (string-prefix-p "*helm" name)
       (string-prefix-p "*Helm" name)
       (string-prefix-p "*Compile-Log*" name)
       (string-prefix-p "*lsp" name)
       (string-prefix-p "*company" name)
       (string-prefix-p "*Flycheck" name)
       (string-prefix-p "*tramp" name)
       (string-prefix-p " *Mini" name)
       (string-prefix-p "*help" name)
       (string-prefix-p "*straight" name)
       (string-prefix-p " *temp" name)
       (string-prefix-p "*Help" name)
       (string-prefix-p "*mybuf" name)
       (string-prefix-p "*scratch*" name)

       ;; Is not magit buffer.
       (and (string-prefix-p "magit" name)
            (not (file-name-extension name)))
       )))
  :hook
  (dired-mode . centaur-tabs-local-mode)
  :bind
  ("C-<prior>" . centaur-tabs-backward)
  ("C-<next>" . centaur-tabs-forward)
  ("C-M-S-<next>" . centaur-tabs-forward-group)
  ("C-M-S-<prior>" . centaur-tabs-backward-group))
#+end_src

** Tree navigation sidebar

Treemacs provides a file and project explorer sidebar for Emacs, similar to
the file explorer in VSCode or other IDEs. It integrates with version control
systems to show file status and provides a tree view of the project structure.

We start by loading the icons for Treemacs.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(declare-function treemacs-load-theme "treemacs")
(use-package treemacs-all-the-icons
  :after (treemacs all-the-icons)
  :config
  (treemacs-load-theme "all-the-icons"))

;; (use-package treemacs-nerd-icons
;;  :after (nerd-icons)
;;  ;; :custom-face (cfrs-border-color ((t (:inherit posframe-border))))
;;  :config
;;  (treemacs-nerd-icons-config)
;;  (treemacs-load-theme "nerd-icons"))
#+end_src

Then we configure Treemacs itself.
If you need to shrink or enlarge the treemacs window later with the keyboard,
you can use:

- =C-x {= - Shrink the window horizontally.
- =C-x }= - Enlarge the window horizontally.

Treemacs has also several useful shortcuts. A few examples:

| Key | Action                              | Description                                                           |
|-----+-------------------------------------+-----------------------------------------------------------------------|
| th  | treemacs-toggle-show-dotfiles       | Toggle the hiding and displaying of dotfiles.                         |
| ti  | treemacs-hide-gitignored-files-mode | Toggle the hiding and displaying of gitignored files.                 |
| tf  | treemacs-follow-mode                | Toggle treemacs-follow-mode.                                          |
| ta  | treemacs-filewatch-mode             | Toggle treemacs-filewatch-mode.                                       |
| tv  | treemacs-fringe-indicator-mode      | Toggle treemacs-fringe-indicator-mode.                                |
| td  | treemacs-git-commit-diff-mode       | Toggle treemacs-git-commit-diff-mode.                                 |
| =   | treemacs-fit-window-width           | Adjust the width of the treemacs window to that of the longsest line. |

See full list at [[https://github.com/Alexander-Miller/treemacs?tab=readme-ov-file#default-keymaps][this link]].

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(declare-function my/treemacs-refresh-on-file-open nil)
(use-package treemacs
  :after (doom-themes)
  :demand t
  :hook (treemacs-mode . (lambda () (setq-local context-menu-mode nil)))
  :hook (treemacs-mode . (lambda () (display-line-numbers-mode -1)))
  :bind (:map treemacs-mode-map
              ("<delete>" . treemacs-delete-file))

  :preface
  ;; (defun doom-themes-enable-treemacs-variable-pitch-labels (&rest _)
  ;;   (dolist (face '(treemacs-root-face
  ;;                   treemacs-git-unmodified-face
  ;;                   treemacs-git-modified-face
  ;;                   treemacs-git-renamed-face
  ;;                   treemacs-git-ignored-face
  ;;                   treemacs-git-untracked-face
  ;;                   treemacs-git-added-face
  ;;                   treemacs-git-conflict-face
  ;;                   treemacs-directory-face
  ;;                   treemacs-directory-collapsed-face
  ;;                   treemacs-file-face
  ;;                   treemacs-tags-face))
  ;;      (let ((faces (face-attribute face :inherit nil)))
  ;;        (set-face-attribute
  ;;         face nil :inherit
  ;;         `(variable-pitch
  ;;          ,@(delq 'unspecified (if (listp faces) faces (list faces))))))))

  (defun my/get-current-folder ()
    "Get the folder of the current buffer, but only for real Files or Dired."
    (let ((path (or (buffer-file-name)
                    (and (derived-mode-p 'dired-mode)
                         (fboundp 'dired-current-directory)
                         (dired-current-directory)))))
      (when path
        (file-name-as-directory (if (file-directory-p path) path (file-name-directory path))))))

  (defun my/treemacs-toggle ()
    "Toggle the visibility of the Treemacs window."
    (interactive)
    (declare-function treemacs-get-local-window "treemacs")
    (if (treemacs-get-local-window)
        (delete-window (treemacs-get-local-window))
      (treemacs)))

  (declare-function treemacs-do-add-project-to-workspace "treemacs")
  (declare-function treemacs-get-local-window "treemacs")
  (declare-function treemacs-refresh "treemacs")
  (declare-function treemacs-root-down "treemacs")
  (declare-function treemacs-root-up "treemacs")
  (declare-function treemacs-select-window "treemacs")

  (defun my/treemacs-set-root-to-current-folder (&optional target-folder)
    "Set the root of Treemacs to the folder of the current file or default directory.
If TARGET-FOLDER is provided, use it directly. Otherwise, try to find the folder
of the `source' buffer that called Treemacs."
    (let ((folder (or target-folder
                      ;; If we are inside the Treemacs sidebar, look at the last visible buffer
                      ;; that is NOT a treemacs window.
                      (with-current-buffer (other-buffer (current-buffer) t)
                        (my/get-current-folder)))))
      (when (and folder (file-directory-p folder))
        ;; If this is a TRAMP path and a matching local path exists, prefer the
        ;; local path to avoid adding host:/docker:... entries when the intent
        ;; was to add the local project root. Otherwise keep the TRAMP path so
        ;; remote projects still work when intended.
        (when (and (fboundp 'tramp-dissect-file-name)
                   (file-remote-p folder))
          (condition-case _err
              (let* ((tfn (tramp-dissect-file-name folder))
                     (local (when tfn
                              (declare-function tramp-file-name-localname "tramp")
                              (tramp-file-name-localname tfn))))
                (when (and local (file-exists-p local))
                  (setq folder (file-name-as-directory (expand-file-name local)))))
            (error nil)))
        (when (and (file-remote-p folder)
                   (not (file-readable-p folder)))
          (message "Warning: remote path %s appears unreadable" folder))
        (ignore-errors
          (treemacs-select-window)
          (treemacs-do-add-project-to-workspace folder (file-name-nondirectory (directory-file-name folder)))
          (treemacs-refresh)))))

  (defun my/update-treemacs-faces-except (excluded-faces)
    "Update all Treemacs-related faces, excluding specified faces."
    (dolist (face (face-list))
      (when (and (string-match-p "treemacs" (symbol-name face))
                 (not (member face excluded-faces)))
        ;;(set-face-attribute face nil :family "Fira Code" :height 105))
        (set-face-attribute face nil :family "JetBrainsMono Nerd Font" :height 105))))

  (defun my/set-treemacs-width-pixels (pixels)
    "Set Treemacs width in pixels."
    (setq treemacs-width (truncate (/ pixels (frame-char-width)))))

  :config
  (my/set-treemacs-width-pixels 300)
  (global-set-key (kbd "C-c t") #'my/treemacs-toggle)
  (global-set-key (kbd "C-c 0") #'treemacs-select-window)

  (declare-function treemacs-indent-guide-mode "treemacs")
  (declare-function treemacs-filewatch-mode "treemacs")

  ;; These are minor modes - they must be activated as function calls,
  ;; not set via setq, so their setup hooks are properly installed.
  (treemacs-indent-guide-mode 1)
  (treemacs-project-follow-mode 1)
  (treemacs-filewatch-mode 1)

  (setq treemacs-is-never-other-window t
        treemacs-indent-guide-style 'line
        treemacs-user-mode-line-format 'line
        treemacs-space-between-root-nodes nil
        treemacs-width-is-initially-locked t
        ;; treemacs-icon-size 10
        ;; treemacs-icon-padding 20
        ;; treemacs-indentation '(10 px)
        ;; treemacs-indentation-string (propertize " " 'display '(space :width 15))
        )

  (set-window-fringes (treemacs-get-local-window) 0 0 nil)
  ;; (set-face-attribute 'treemacs-fringe-indicator-face nil :foreground nil)

  ;; Doom theme support
  ;; (doom-themes-enable-treemacs-variable-pitch-labels)
  ;; (doom-themes-treemacs-config)

  ;; Icon size
  ;; (treemacs-resize-icons 44)

  ;; See https://github.com/Alexander-Miller/treemacs?tab=readme-ov-file#all-the-icons-indent-issues
  ;; Disabled because it causes misalignment between folders and files and affects indentation
  ;; (treemacs-load-all-the-icons-with-workaround-font "Hermit")

  ;; (advice-add #'load-theme
  ;;             :after #'doom-themes-enable-treemacs-variable-pitch-labels)

  ;; Custom double-click action
  ;; (with-eval-after-load 'treemacs-mouse-interface
  ;;   (treemacs-define-doubleclick-action 'my-action
  ;;                                       (lambda () (message "Double clicked!"))))

  (with-eval-after-load 'doom-themes
    ;; Disable variable pitch fonts in Treemacs. Not un use for now
    ;; (setq doom-themes-treemacs-enable-variable-pitch nil)

    (my/update-treemacs-faces-except '(treemacs-all-the-icons-file-face
                                       treemacs-all-the-icons-root-face
                                       treemacs-hl-line-face
                                       treemacs-window-background-face))

    ;; Other settings to investigate later
    ;; (setq mixed-pitch-set-height t)
    ;; (set-face-attribute 'variable-pitch nil :height 1.5)
    ;; (set-face-attribute 'variable-pitch nil :family "Open Sans" :height 1.0)
    )

  (my/update-treemacs-faces-except '(treemacs-all-the-icons-file-face
                                     treemacs-all-the-icons-root-face
                                     treemacs-git-ignored-face
                                     treemacs-hl-line-face
                                     treemacs-window-background-face))

  ;; Uncomment below to start treemacs automatically:
  ;; (unless (treemacs-get-local-window)
  ;;   (treemacs))
  )

;; Fix for for https://github.com/Alexander-Miller/treemacs/issues/1016
(with-eval-after-load 'treemacs-all-the-icons
  (treemacs-modify-theme "all-the-icons"
    :config
    (progn
      (load (expand-file-name "treemacs-custom-items-fix.el" user-emacs-directory)))))

** Centering text in the buffer (C-c o)

[[https://github.com/rnkn/olivetti][Olivetti]] allows us to center the text of a buffer.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package olivetti
  :defer t
  :bind (:map custom-bindings-map ("C-c o" . olivetti-mode))
  :config
  (setq-default olivetti-body-width (max 120 (+ fill-column 3))))
#+end_src

Other useful shortcuts for the extension are:

- =C-c {= : Decreases width (=olivetti-shrink=).
- =C-c }= : Increases width (=olivetti-expand=).
- =C-c |= : Sets a specific width (=olivetti-set-width=).


** Modes configuration

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(dolist (mode
         '(abbrev-mode              ; E.g. sopl -> System.out.println
           delete-selection-mode    ; Replace selected text
           dirtrack-mode            ; directory tracking in *shell*
           global-auto-revert-mode  ; Revert files when changed on disk
           global-so-long-mode      ; Mitigate performance for long lines
           recentf-mode             ; Recently opened files
           show-paren-mode))        ; Highlight matching parentheses
  (funcall mode 1))
#+end_src


** Minibuffer Completion, Search, and Navigation

*** Ivy, Counsel, and Ivy-rich

Ivy, Counsel, and Swiper provide minibuffer completion -- they enhance the
interactive prompts at the bottom of Emacs for commands like =M-x=, ~find-file~,
and search. Ivy is the core completion framework. Counsel adds Ivy-powered
versions of common commands (like =M-x=, ~find-file~, ~rg~). Swiper integrates
search with Ivy for better navigation. Together, they replace IDO and Helm for a
faster, cleaner experience.

~ivy-mode~ enables Ivy's UI for all compatible prompts. ~counsel-mode~ remaps
standard Emacs commands to their enhanced Counsel versions. Use both for the
best experience.

~ivy-switch-buffer~ (from Ivy) is a fast, simple buffer switcher. ~counsel-ibuffer~
(from Counsel) is a more powerful buffer manager with groups and metadata. Bind
either to =C-x b= depending on whether you want a quick switcher or a full buffer
manager.

Ivy-rich enhances Ivy's completion menus by adding extra information to each
candidate (like file paths, buffer modes, or recent file details). It makes
Ivy and Counsel popups more informative and visually appealing, without
changing how you interact with them.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package ivy
  :diminish (ivy-mode)
  :config
  (ivy-mode 1)
  (setq ivy-use-virtual-buffers t)
  (setq ivy-display-style 'fancy))

(use-package counsel
  :bind (("M-x" . counsel-M-x)
         ("C-x b" . counsel-ibuffer)
         ("C-x C-f" . counsel-find-file)
         :map minibuffer-local-map
         ("C-r" . 'counsel-minibuffer-history))
  :config
  (counsel-mode 1))

(declare-function ivy-rich-mode "ivy-rich")
(use-package ivy-rich
  :after ivy
  :defer t
  :config
  (ivy-rich-mode 1))
#+end_src


*** Swiper and Avy: Fast Search and Navigation

Swiper gives us incremental search and is integrated with Ivy and Counsel.
To search for the item at point, type =M-n= with Swiper open.

Avy is a fast navigation tool: it lets you jump to any visible character,
word, or line by typing a short key sequence. It's great for quickly moving
the cursor anywhere on the screen without using the mouse or arrow keys.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(declare-function swiper-isearch-thing-at-point "swiper")

(defun my/swiper-thing-or-region ()
  "Call `swiper-isearch-thing-at-point' or `swiper-isearch'.
If the region is active and contains more than one character, call
`swiper-isearch-thing-at-point'. Otherwise, call `swiper-isearch'."
  (interactive)
  (if (and (use-region-p)
           (> (abs (- (point) (mark))) 1)) ; the selection is more than 1 character long
      (swiper-isearch-thing-at-point)
    (swiper-isearch)))

(use-package swiper
  :bind (("C-s" . my/swiper-thing-or-region)
         ("C-r" . swiper-isearch-backward)
         ("C-c C-r" . ivy-resume)
         ("C-x C-f" . counsel-find-file))
  :config
  (progn
    (ivy-mode 1)
    (setopt ivy-use-virtual-buffers t)
    (setopt ivy-display-style 'fancy)
    (define-key read-expression-map (kbd "C-r") 'counsel-expression-history)
    ))

(use-package avy
  :bind ("C-:" . avy-goto-char))
#+end_src


** Maximize frame at startup

Below we maximize the Emacs frame when starting up.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(add-hook 'emacs-startup-hook 'toggle-frame-maximized)
#+end_src


* Version control

** Magit: a powerful Git interface for Emacs

Magit is a powerful Git interface for Emacs, providing an intuitive
way to manage Git repositories directly from the editor.

In the Magit status buffer:
- press =!= to open a command prompt where you can run any git command
  directly.
- press =$= to open a buffer named *magit-process: <repository-path>*,
  where you can see the commands ran by Magit.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package magit
  ;; The :commands directive ensures that magit is not loaded into memory until
  ;; magit-status is explicitly called. This can improve startup performance.
  :commands magit-status
  :bind (:map custom-bindings-map ("C-c m" . magit-status)))
#+end_src


** Forge

Forge is an extension for Magit that provides integration with Git hosting
services like GitHub, GitLab, and Bitbucket. It allows you to manage pull
requests, issues, and other repository features directly from Emacs.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package forge
  :pin "MELPA Unstable"
  :after magit
  :config)
#+end_src


** Blamer.el

=blamer.el= is a Git blame plugin for Emacs that brings a "GitLens-like"
experience to your editing environment. It provides inline, contextual
annotations that display the author, date, and commit message of the current
line (or selected ones) directly within your buffer. Because it is designed with
high configurability -- supporting various display modes like overlays, margins, or
floating posframe popups -- it offers a flexible way to surface version control
history without cluttering your code or interfering with other completion
overlays.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package blamer
  :demand t ;; Make sure blamer.el is loaded immediately
  :bind (("s-i" . blamer-show-commit-info)
         ("C-c i" . blamer-show-posframe-commit-info))
  :custom
  (blamer-type 'both)
  (blamer-idle-time 0.3)
  (blamer-overlay-priority 1) ;; Low priority: make sure Copilot/ECA overlays win
  :custom-face
  (blamer-face ((t :foreground "#5c6370"
                   :background unspecified
                   :height 85)))
  :config
  (defun my/toggle-blamer ()
    "Toggle global-blamer-mode on and off."
    (interactive)
    (global-blamer-mode 'toggle))

  (define-key custom-bindings-map (kbd "C-c b") 'my/toggle-blamer))
#+end_src


** ANSI color support in the magit process buffer

When running Git commands through Magit (e.g., hooks, pre-commit scripts), the output
is displayed in the *magit-process* buffer (accessible by pressing =$= in the Magit
status buffer). This buffer shows the raw output from Git commands. However, by
default, ANSI color codes in the output are not interpreted, so the output appears
with raw escape sequences instead of colored text.

There are two approaches to enable ANSI color support in the Magit process buffer:

*** Option 1: Official built-in solution (with delay)

The official way to enable ANSI colors is to set ~magit-process-finish-apply-ansi-colors~.
However, this only applies colors after the Git command has finished executing. For
slow processes (like long-running hooks), you'll see the raw escape sequences until the
command completes, which can be distracting.

#+begin_example emacs-lisp
;; Official built-in solution - applies colors after command finishes
(setq magit-process-finish-apply-ansi-colors t)
#+end_example


*** Option 2: Real-time color application (recommended)

A better solution, suggested in [[https://github.com/magit/magit/issues/3549#issuecomment-1375023283][this GitHub comment]],
applies colors in real-time as the buffer is updated. This provides immediate visual
feedback without showing raw escape sequences during long-running commands. This is
the approach we use in our configuration:

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
;; Apply ANSI colors in real-time as the magit-process buffer is updated
(defun my/magit-color-buffer (proc &rest _args)
  "Apply ANSI colors to the magit-process buffer in real-time.
PROC is the process object for the magit-process buffer.
_ARGS are additional arguments passed by magit-process-filter (unused)."
  (with-current-buffer (process-buffer proc)
    (read-only-mode -1)
    (ansi-color-apply-on-region (point-min) (point-max))
    (read-only-mode 1)))

(declare-function ansi-color-apply-on-region "ansi-color")

(advice-add 'magit-process-filter :after #'my/magit-color-buffer)
#+end_src


** Other magit-related changes

Below we add some visual indications for uncommitted changes with [[https://github.com/dgutov/diff-hl][~diff-hl~]].
In addition, ~diff-hl~ can be automatically updated when interacting with Magit.
~diff-hl~ shows a git diff in the fringe (the left or right margins) of
the editor, indicating added, modified, and deleted lines in the current
file. It provides visual cues for changes made since the last commit,
making it easier to track modifications.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package diff-hl
  :defer t
  :hook ((magit-pre-refresh . ignore)
         (magit-post-refresh . diff-hl-magit-post-refresh))
  :config
  (global-diff-hl-mode 1))
#+end_src

Below we integrate Treemacs with Magit to show Git status in the Treemacs sidebar.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package treemacs-magit
  :after (treemacs magit))
#+end_src

Next, we introduce a utility function and associated hooks to automatically enable
~smerge-mode~ when a file contains merge conflict markers. This is particularly useful
for developers working with version control systems like Git, as it streamlines the
process of resolving merge conflicts.

We can navigate between conflict markers using the following keybindings:
- =C-c ^ n= - Jump to the next conflict marker.
- =C-c ^ p= - Jump to the previous conflict marker.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(defun maybe-enable-smerge-mode ()
  "Enable `smerge-mode' for buffers with merge conflict markers."
  (save-excursion
    (goto-char (point-min))
    (when (re-search-forward "^<<<<<<< " nil t)
      (smerge-mode 1))))

(add-hook 'find-file-hook #'maybe-enable-smerge-mode)
(add-hook 'after-revert-hook #'maybe-enable-smerge-mode)
(add-hook 'after-change-major-mode-hook #'maybe-enable-smerge-mode)
#+end_src
