** Try a new package without installing it

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package try)
#+end_src


** Restart Emacs from within Emacs

The following is a simple package to restart Emacs for within Emacs.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package restart-emacs
  :bind ("C-c C-M-r" . restart-emacs))
#+end_src


** Dired

*** Basic package configuration

Dired is a built-in package. We just set some configurations below:

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package dired
  :ensure nil ; Built-in
  :custom
  (dired-listing-switches "-ah --group-directories-first")
  :config
  (add-hook 'dired-mode-hook (lambda () (setq-local line-spacing 0.1))))
#+end_src


*** Dirvish

Dirvish is the heavyweight champion of Dired improvements. It isn't just a
"skin"; it's a complete rewrite of the Dired user interface that makes Emacs
feel like a high-end file manager (like Ranger or a modern IDE sidebar) while
keeping the power of Dired's underlying Lisp.

Dirvish uses a set if system utilities for giving information about packages. We
install them below:

#+begin_src sh :tangle setup.sh
DIRVISH_UTILITIES=(
  "fd" "git"
  "mediainfo" "imagemagick" "ffmpegthumbnailer" "vipsthumbnail"
  "poppler" "unzip" "glow" "pandoc"
)

is_installed_dirvish_utilities_bundle() {
  are_these_system_packages_installed "${DIRVISH_UTILITIES[@]}"
}

# --- Core Performance & Navigation ---
# Used for fast searching, jumping to files, and directory rendering.
register_system_package "fd" \
                        "alpine:fd" \
                        "fedora:fd-find" \
                        "linuxbrew:fd" \
                        "mac:fd" \
                        "debian-ubuntu:fd-find"

register_system_package "git" \
                        "alpine:git" \
                        "fedora:git" \
                        "linuxbrew:git" \
                        "mac:git" \
                        "debian-ubuntu:git"

# --- Media Metadata & Previews ---
# Used by dispatchers to show technical info and thumbnails for video/audio/images.
register_system_package "mediainfo" \
                        "alpine:mediainfo" \
                        "fedora:mediainfo" \
                        "linuxbrew:mediainfo" \
                        "mac:mediainfo" \
                        "debian-ubuntu:mediainfo"

register_system_package "imagemagick" \
                        "alpine:imagemagick" \
                        "fedora:ImageMagick" \
                        "linuxbrew:imagemagick" \
                        "mac:imagemagick" \
                        "debian-ubuntu:imagemagick"

register_system_package "ffmpegthumbnailer" \
                        "alpine:ffmpegthumbnailer" \
                        "fedora:ffmpegthumbnailer" \
                        "linuxbrew:ffmpegthumbnailer" \
                        "mac:ffmpegthumbnailer" \
                        "debian-ubuntu:ffmpegthumbnailer"

register_system_package "vipsthumbnail" \
                        "alpine:vips-tools" \
                        "fedora:vips-tools" \
                        "linuxbrew:vips" \
                        "mac:vips" \
                        "debian-ubuntu:libvips-tools"

# --- Document & Archive Parsing ---
# Used to peek inside PDFs, archives, and formatted text files.
register_system_package "poppler" \
                        "alpine:poppler-utils" \
                        "fedora:poppler" \
                        "linuxbrew:poppler" \
                        "mac:poppler" \
                        "debian-ubuntu:poppler-utils"

register_system_package "unzip" \
                        "alpine:unzip" \
                        "fedora:unzip" \
                        "linuxbrew:unzip" \
                        "mac:unzip" \
                        "debian-ubuntu:unzip"

register_system_package "glow" \
                        "alpine:glow" \
                        "fedora:glow" \
                        "linuxbrew:glow" \
                        "mac:glow" \
                        "debian-ubuntu:glow"

register_system_package "pandoc" \
                        "alpine:pandoc" \
                        "fedora:pandoc" \
                        "linuxbrew:pandoc" \
                        "mac:pandoc" \
                        "debian-ubuntu:pandoc"

install_dirvish_utilities_bundle() {
  log_info "Starting Dirvish utilities setup..."
  install_system_packages "${DIRVISH_UTILITIES[@]}"
}

uninstall_dirvish_utilities_bundle() {
  log_skip "Dirvish utilities" "The packages used with Dirvish are system packages and will not be uninstalled automatically"
}

register_action "Install Dirvish utilities" "dirvish_utilities_bundle" "Utils"
#+end_src

With the system utilities installed, we can configure the package on Emacs:

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package dirvish
  :pin "MELPA Unstable"
  :bind (:map dirvish-mode-map
              ("TAB" . dirvish-subtree-toggle))
  :init
  (dirvish-override-dired-mode)
  :config
  (defun my/dirvish-toggle-git-msg ()
    "Toggle 'vc-state' and 'git-msg' attributes for Dirvish."
    (interactive)
    (let ((has-git (or (memq 'git-msg dirvish-attributes)
                       (memq 'vc-state dirvish-attributes))))
      ;; Toggle the global attributes
      (if has-git
          (setq dirvish-attributes (remq 'vc-state (remq 'git-msg dirvish-attributes)))
        (setq dirvish-attributes (append dirvish-attributes '(vc-state git-msg))))

      ;; Re-initialize the active dirvish session so git background processes correctly start/stop
      (when (and (derived-mode-p 'dired-mode) (dirvish-curr))
        (let ((dir default-directory)
              (layout-p (dv-curr-layout (dirvish-curr))))
          (kill-buffer (current-buffer))
          (if layout-p
              (dirvish dir)
            (dired dir))))

      (message "Dirvish: git info %s" (if has-git "Disabled" "Enabled"))))

  (setq dirvish-mode-line-format '(:left (path) :right (file-size)))
  (setq dirvish-attributes '(subtree-state nerd-icons file-size))
  (setq dirvish-preview-dispatchers '(dired image gif audio epub archive font pdf video))
  (setq dirvish-cache-dir
        (expand-file-name "dirvish/" user-emacs-directory))
  (setq dirvish-subtree-listing-switches "-Ah --group-directories-first")
  (define-key dired-mode-map (kbd "|") #'dirvish-layout-toggle)
  (define-key dired-mode-map (kbd "g") #'my/dirvish-toggle-git-msg))
#+end_src


*** Dired faces

This package adds extra highlighting to file permissions, sizes, and dates,
making the buffer much easier to parse.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(defun my/apply-diredfl-faces ()
  "Apply diredfl faces based on the current theme."
  (let ((theme-name (format "%s" (car custom-enabled-themes))))
    (cond
     ;; CASE: Nord Themes
     ((string-match-p "doom-nord" theme-name)
      (custom-set-faces
       '(diredfl-date-time  ((t (:foreground "#b8a98f"))))
       '(diredfl-dir-name   ((t (:foreground "#81a1c1" :weight bold))))
       '(diredfl-dir-priv   ((t (:foreground "#88c0d0" :weight bold))))
       '(diredfl-exec-priv  ((t (:foreground "#9683a3"))))
       '(diredfl-file-name  ((t (:foreground "#eceff4"))))
       '(diredfl-group      ((t (:foreground "#434c5e" :inherit nil))))
       '(diredfl-no-priv    ((t (:foreground "#434c5e"))))
       '(diredfl-number     ((t (:foreground "#616e88"))))
       '(diredfl-owner      ((t (:foreground "#4c566a" :inherit nil))))
       '(diredfl-read-priv  ((t (:foreground "#70788d"))))
       '(diredfl-write-priv ((t (:foreground "#a3adcb"))))))

     ;; CASE: Doom-One / Default (The Steel Blue setup)
     (t
      (custom-set-faces
       '(diredfl-date-time  ((t (:foreground "#c6c9cd"))))
       '(diredfl-dir-name   ((t (:foreground "#51afef"))))
       '(diredfl-dir-priv   ((t (:foreground "#3579a8" :weight bold))))
       '(diredfl-exec-priv  ((t (:foreground "#a9a1e1"))))
       '(diredfl-file-name  ((t (:foreground "#bbc2cf"))))
       '(diredfl-group      ((t (:foreground "#282c34" :inherit nil))))
       '(diredfl-no-priv    ((t (:foreground "#3f444a"))))
       '(diredfl-number     ((t (:foreground "#4c566a"))))
       '(diredfl-owner      ((t (:foreground "#3f444a" :inherit nil))))
       '(diredfl-read-priv  ((t (:foreground "#5b6268"))))
       '(diredfl-write-priv ((t (:foreground "#9ca0a4"))))))))

  (when (and after-init-time (derived-mode-p 'dired-mode))
    (revert-buffer)))

;; Hook it so it runs whenever you switch themes
(add-hook 'after-load-theme-hook #'my/apply-diredfl-faces)

(use-package diredfl
  :hook ((dired-mode . diredfl-mode)
         (dirvish-directory-view-mode . diredfl-mode))
  :init
  (my/apply-diredfl-faces))
#+end_src


** Snippets

Yasnippet provides snippet expansion (type a short trigger and press TAB) and

(use-package yasnippet
  :demand t
  :config

** Undo tree

Undo-Tree preserves and visualizes your undo history as a tree, making it
easier to navigate complex edits and to revert to earlier states.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(defvar undo-tree-incompatible-major-modes
  nil
  "List of major modes incompatible with undo-tree.
Declared here so tangling this file defines the variable before the
package is loaded.")

(use-package undo-tree
  :config
  (add-to-list 'undo-tree-incompatible-major-modes #'nxml-mode)
  (global-undo-tree-mode)
  :custom
  (undo-tree-history-directory-alist '(("." . "~/.emacs.d/undo-tree"))))
#+end_src


** Scrollbar on the terminal

Yascroll provides a scrollbar for Emacs running in terminal mode (tty).
It is a nice package, but it has a known bug reported at [[https://github.com/emacs-evil/yascroll/issues/1]].

#+begin_example emacs-lisp
(use-package yascroll
  :config
  (global-yascroll-bar-mode 1)
  (setq yascroll:delay-to-hide nil)
  ;; (setq yascroll:thumb-texture 'rounded)
  (add-hook 'treemacs-mode-hook 'yascroll-bar-mode)
  (with-eval-after-load 'treemacs
    (add-hook 'treemacs-mode-hook
              (lambda ()
                (yascroll-bar-mode 1)
                 (setq yascroll:delay-to-hide nil))))
)
#+end_example

Another suggestion is to use ~smart-mode-line~, but that cannot be active together
with ~doom-modeline~, so we comment it out for now.

#+begin_example emacs-lisp
;; Smart Mode Line for a clean, informative modeline (works in GUI and terminal)
(use-package smart-mode-line
  :init
  (setq sml/no-confirm-load-theme t)
  (setq sml/theme 'respectful) ; or 'dark, 'light, 'powerline, etc.
  :config
  (sml/setup))
#+end_example

As the packages above did not work as expected, I found another package that
seems to work nicely: ~mlscroll~. It adds a scrollbar to the mode line, showing
the current position in the buffer. It needs ~which-func~ (builtin) to work properly.
It is not a full scrollbar, but at least it gives an indication of the position
in the buffer.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package which-func
  :ensure t)

(use-package mlscroll
  :config

  (mlscroll-mode 1))

  ;; If starting Emacs as a server, enable mlscroll for new frames

** Expanding ripgrep

Here we integrate ripgrep with Emacs. Before we proceed, we need to install it
on our system:

#+begin_src sh :tangle setup.sh
is_installed_ripgrep() {
  is_system_package_installed "ripgrep"
}

register_system_package "ripgrep" \
                        "alpine:ripgrep" \
                        "fedora:ripgrep" \
                        "linuxbrew:ripgrep" \
                        "mac:ripgrep" \
                        "debian-ubuntu:ripgrep"

install_ripgrep() {
  log_info "Starting Ripgrep setup..."
  install_system_packages "ripgrep"
}

uninstall_ripgrep() {
  log_skip "Ripgrep" "Ripgrep is system package and will not be uninstalled automatically"
}

register_action "Install Ripgrep" "ripgrep" "Utils"
#+end_src

On Emacs, we configure the ~rg~ package, responsible for the desired integration.
We will be able to search for a string in a directory using =M-x rg= and a directory.
We can also call =rg-dwim= or =rg= and give any path.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package rg
  :config
  (rg-enable-default-bindings)
  (rg-enable-menu)
  (setq wgrep-auto-save-buffer t) ; Save wgrep buffers automatically after editing

  (declare-function project-roots "project")
  (defun my/counsel-rg-project-prompt ()
    "Prompt for directory, default to project root if available."
    (interactive)
    (let* ((proj-root
            (cond
             ((fboundp 'projectile-project-root)
              (projectile-project-root))
             ((fboundp 'project-current)
              (let ((proj (project-current)))
                (when proj
                  (car (project-roots proj)))))
             ((fboundp 'vc-root-dir)
              (vc-root-dir))
             (t default-directory)))
           (dir (read-directory-name "Search directory: " proj-root nil t)))
      (counsel-rg nil dir)))

  (declare-function transient-quit-all "transient")
  (declare-function my/counsel-rg-project-prompt nil)
  (defun my/rg-abort-to-counsel ()
    "Abort the current transient menu and launch counsel-rg."
    (interactive)
    (transient-quit-all)
    (my/counsel-rg-project-prompt))

  (declare-function transient-suffix-object "transient")
  (declare-function transient-lisp-variable "transient")
  (declare-function rg-menu-transient-insert "rg")
  (transient-define-infix my/search-case-strategy ()
    :class 'transient-lisp-variable
    :variable 'rg-ignore-case
    :description
    (lambda ()
      (let* ((val (oref (transient-suffix-object) value))
             (shown (if val (symbol-name val) "nil")))
        (format "Search Case Strategy [%s]" shown)))
    :reader
    (lambda (_prompt _initial _history)
      (let* ((choices '(case-fold-search smart force nil))
             ;; Safe even if current value isn’t in CHOICES.
             (pos (or (cl-position rg-ignore-case choices) 0)))
        (nth (mod (1+ pos) (length choices)) choices))))

  (rg-menu-transient-insert "Search" "z" "Live Search (Counsel)" 'my/rg-abort-to-counsel)
  (rg-menu-transient-insert "Custom" "g" "Search Case Strategy" 'my/search-case-strategy)

  (add-to-list 'display-buffer-alist
               '("\\*rg\\*"
                 (display-buffer-same-window))))
#+end_src


** ~vterm~, a fully capable terminal emulator

We install ~vterm~ below, a compiled C module that feels as fast as a native
terminal. Alternatives like ~Eshell~ or ~Term~ are written in Elisp; while they
integrate deeply with Emacs, they often lag, flicker, or break entirely when
running resource-heavy programs like =htop= or =vim=. By using ~vterm~, we get a
"real" terminal experience that handles complex character encoding and
high-speed scrolling without the compromises found in pure Emacs Lisp shells.

~vterm~ is built as a compiled C module for maximum performance, so you must
have a few development packages installed on your system to allow Emacs to build
the driver during the initial setup:

#+begin_src sh :tangle setup.sh
VTERM_BUILD_PACKAGES=(
  "libvterm" "cmake" "build-essential"
)

is_installed_vterm_build_bundle() {
  are_these_system_packages_installed "${VTERM_BUILD_PACKAGES[@]}"
}

register_system_package "libvterm" \
                        "alpine:libvterm-dev" \
                        "fedora:libvterm-devel" \
                        "linuxbrew:libvterm" \
                        "mac:libvterm" \
                        "debian-ubuntu:libvterm-dev"

register_system_package "cmake" \
                        "alpine:cmake" \
                        "fedora:cmake" \
                        "linuxbrew:cmake" \
                        "mac:cmake" \
                        "debian-ubuntu:cmake"

register_system_package "build-essential" \
                        "alpine:build-base" \
                        "fedora:gcc-c++" \
                        "linuxbrew:gcc" \
                        "mac:xcode" \
                        "debian-ubuntu:build-essential"

install_vterm_build_bundle() {
  log_info "Starting C++ Development environment setup..."
  install_system_packages "${VTERM_BUILD_PACKAGES[@]}"
}

uninstall_vterm_build_bundle() {
  log_skip "vterm build bundle" "The packages used for vterm builds are system packages and will not be uninstalled automatically"
}

register_action "Install vterm build bundle" "vterm_build_bundle" "Utils"
#+end_src

And below the Emacs setup:

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package vterm
  :defer t
  :preface
  (defvar my/vterms nil)

  ;; Don't ask about killing vterm buffers - use advice-add instead of defadvice
  (defun my/vterm-kill-with-no-query-around (orig &rest args)
    (let ((buf (apply orig args)))
      (when (and (bufferp buf) (get-buffer-process buf))
        (set-process-query-on-exit-flag (get-buffer-process buf) nil))
      buf))

  (defun toggle-vterm (&optional n)
    (interactive)
    (setq my/vterms (seq-filter 'buffer-live-p my/vterms))
    (let ((default-directory (or (vc-root-dir) default-directory)))
      (cond ((numberp n) (push (vterm n) my/vterms))
            ((null my/vterms) (push (vterm 1) my/vterms))
            ((seq-contains-p my/vterms (current-buffer))
             (switch-to-buffer (car (seq-difference (buffer-list) my/vterms))))
            (t (switch-to-buffer (car (seq-intersection (buffer-list) my/vterms)))))))

  (declare-function vterm-send-next-key "vterm")
  (defun my/vterm-send-next-key ()
    "Prompt for a key sequence and send it to the vterm terminal."
    (interactive)
    (when (derived-mode-p 'vterm-mode)
      (message "The next key will be sent to vterm")
      (vterm-send-next-key)))

  :bind (:map custom-bindings-map
              ("C-z" . toggle-vterm)
              ("M-1" . (lambda () (interactive) (toggle-vterm 1)))
              ("M-2" . (lambda () (interactive) (toggle-vterm 2)))
              ("M-3" . (lambda () (interactive) (toggle-vterm 3)))
              ("M-4" . (lambda () (interactive) (toggle-vterm 4)))
              ("M-5" . (lambda () (interactive) (toggle-vterm 5)))
              ("M-6" . (lambda () (interactive) (toggle-vterm 6)))
              ("M-7" . (lambda () (interactive) (toggle-vterm 7)))
              ("M-8" . (lambda () (interactive) (toggle-vterm 8)))
              ("M-9" . (lambda () (interactive) (toggle-vterm 9)))
              ("C-c C-x" . my/vterm-send-next-key))

  :config
  (setq vterm-max-scrollback 10000)
  (advice-add 'vterm :around #'my/vterm-kill-with-no-query-around))

;; Here we set the font for vterm to a Nerd Font that supports
;; all the glyphs used by my oh-my-zsh configuration.

;; https://emacsredux.com/blog/2020/11/21/disable-global-hl-line-mode-for-specific-modes/
(add-hook 'vterm-mode-hook (lambda () (setq-local global-hl-line-mode nil)))

(with-eval-after-load 'vterm
  (set-frame-font "JetBrainsMono Nerd Font" nil t))
#+end_src


** Multiple cursors

Multiple Cursors lets you edit many places at once: mark the next or
previous occurrence and type to edit them all simultaneously. Below are
a couple of convenient keybindings.

(use-package multiple-cursors
  :bind
  ("C->"   . mc/mark-next-like-this)

  ("C-M-#" . mc/mark-all-dwim))

(declare-function mc/num-cursors "multiple-cursors")
(declare-function my/mc-count-cursors "multiple-cursors")
(with-eval-after-load 'multiple-cursors
  (defun my/mc-count-cursors ()
    "Return the number of active cursors for the mode line."
    (if (and (boundp 'multiple-cursors-mode) multiple-cursors-mode)
        (format " MC:%d" (1+ (mc/num-cursors)))
      ""))

  (with-eval-after-load 'doom-modeline

** Hiding secrets

Below we introduce the configuration for cloak-mode, a security-focused minor
mode that masks sensitive data (like passwords and API tokens) with asterisks to
prevent accidental exposure during screen shares or demonstrations. By
leveraging custom regular expressions tailored for .env, json and yaml files,
the package automatically detects and hides values following specific keywords.

**Keybindings:**
| Command         | Shortcut | Description                                   |
|-----------------+----------+-----------------------------------------------|
| ~cloak-mode~    | =s-c=    | Toggle masking on/off for the entire buffer.  |
| ~my/cloak-edit~ | =M-s-c=  | Edit the secret at point via a safe prompt.   |

*Note:* The edit command (=M-s-c=) uses a secure minibuffer prompt to update
values, ensuring the plain-text secret never appears in the main buffer.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(defvar my/cloak-keywords '("KEY" "PASSWD" "PASS" "PASSWORD" "SECRET" "TOKEN" "AUTH")
  "List of keywords that trigger cloaking.")

(defun my/get-cloak-regex (sep)
  "Regex for cloak-mode. SEP is the separator between key and value."
  (let ((kw (regexp-opt my/cloak-keywords)))
    (concat "^[ \t]*[^" sep "\n]*" kw "[^" sep "\n]*"
            sep "[ \t]*[\"']?\\(.*?\\)[\"']?$")))

(defconst my/cloak-regex-dot-env (my/get-cloak-regex "="))
(defconst my/cloak-regex-yaml (my/get-cloak-regex ":"))
(defconst my/cloak-regex-whole-line "^\\(.+\\)$")

(defun my/cloak-passwd-file-p ()
  "Return t if the filename has \"passwd\" or \"password\" and only 1 non-blank line."
  (let ((filename (buffer-file-name)))
    (and filename
         (string-match-p "passw\\(d\\|ord\\)" filename)
         (save-excursion
           (save-match-data
             (goto-char (point-min))
             (if (re-search-forward "[^ \t\n]" nil t)
                 (progn
                   (forward-line 1)
                   (not (re-search-forward "[^ \t\n]" nil t)))
               t))))))

(define-derived-mode my/passwd-mode fundamental-mode "Passwd"
  "Major mode for single-line password files to hide their content.")

(add-to-list 'magic-mode-alist (cons #'my/cloak-passwd-file-p #'my/passwd-mode))

(use-package cloak-mode
  :config
  (setq cloak-mode-patterns
        `((my/passwd-mode . ,my/cloak-regex-whole-line)
          (dotenv-mode    . ,my/cloak-regex-dot-env)
          (yaml-mode      . ,my/cloak-regex-yaml)
          (json-mode      . ,my/cloak-regex-yaml)))

  ;; This function ensures cloak-mode is actually turned ON
  (defun my/cloak-ensure-on ()
    "Force cloak-mode on if the filename matches our secret criteria."
    (when (my/cloak-passwd-file-p)
      (cloak-mode 1)))

  ;; Hook into both file opening and mode changes to catch files from terminal
  (add-hook 'find-file-hook 'my/cloak-ensure-on)
  (add-hook 'after-change-major-mode-hook 'my/cloak-ensure-on)
  ;; Hook into mode changes to ensure cloak-mode activates for password files
  (add-hook 'my/passwd-mode-hook 'my/cloak-ensure-on)

  (defun my/cloak-edit ()
    "Edit the secret value at point.
Works for both key-value pairs and naked secrets."
    (interactive)
    (let* ((is-pass-file (my/cloak-passwd-file-p))
           (sep (if (derived-mode-p 'yaml-mode 'json-mode) ":" "="))
           (regex (if is-pass-file my/cloak-regex-whole-line (my/get-cloak-regex sep))))
      (save-excursion
        (beginning-of-line)
        (if (re-search-forward regex (line-end-position) t)
            (let* ((old-val (match-string 1))
                   (beg (match-beginning 1))
                   (end (match-end 1))
                   (new-val (read-string "New Secret Value: " old-val)))
              (let ((inhibit-read-only t))
                (delete-region beg end)
                (goto-char beg)
                (insert new-val))
              (when cloak-mode
                (cloak-mode -1)
                (cloak-mode 1))
              (message "Secret updated."))
          (message "No secret found on this line.")))))

  (global-set-key (kbd "s-c") 'cloak-mode)
  (global-set-key (kbd "M-s-c") 'my/cloak-edit)
  (global-cloak-mode 1))
#+end_src
