#+begin_src emacs-lisp
;; Minimal remap: make comment faces use the `org-block` background inside
;; Org buffers so inline face plists don't hide the block background.
;; This is intentionally tiny and has no undo cookie stored.
(add-hook 'org-mode-hook
          (lambda ()
            (let ((bg (face-attribute 'org-block :background nil)))
              (when (and bg (not (eq bg 'unspecified)))
                (face-remap-add-relative 'font-lock-comment-face `(:background ,bg :extend t))
                (face-remap-add-relative 'font-lock-comment-delimiter-face `(:background ,bg :extend t))
                (font-lock-flush)
                (font-lock-ensure)
                (redisplay)))))
#+end_src


* Programming, IDE and related

** Code completion

Company (COMPLete ANY) provides in-buffer completion popups - automatic
suggestions that appear *while you type* in your code buffers (not in the
minibuffer). This is different from Ivy/Counsel which handle minibuffer
prompts when Emacs asks you for input.

Prefer ~completion-at-point-functions~ (CAPF) as a single, canonical source:
it lets language servers (LSP/eglot), built-in ~treesit~ capf providers, and
other backends expose candidates through Emacs' standard API so Company
becomes a thin, reliable UI on top of those sources. Using CAPF reduces
backend duplication, simplifies ordering, and improves snippet/expand
interactions.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package company
  :demand t

  :bind
  ;; List of choices even when nothing is typed.
  ;; CTRL-ENTER. Because C-RET does not work.
  ([(control return)] . company-complete)

  :config
  (global-company-mode 1)
  ;; sensible defaults
  (setq company-idle-delay 0
        company-echo-delay 0
        company-minimum-prefix-length 1
        company-show-quick-access t
        company-tooltip-align-annotations t
        company-require-match nil)

  ;; Prefer completion-at-point functions (capf) which integrates with LSP/treesit
  (setq company-backends '((company-capf company-files company-dabbrev-code company-dabbrev)))

  ;; Use TAB/RET sensibly
  (define-key company-active-map (kbd "<tab>") 'company-complete-selection)
  (define-key company-active-map (kbd "TAB") 'company-complete-selection)
  (define-key company-active-map (kbd "C-n") 'company-select-next)
  (define-key company-active-map (kbd "C-p") 'company-select-previous)

  ;; Disable company in some modes where it is noisy
  (dolist (m '(org-mode shell-mode term-mode vterm-mode))
    (add-hook (intern (concat (symbol-name m) "-hook")) (lambda () (company-mode -1)))))
#+end_src


Company-box provides a lightweight, frame-based UI for Company popups
in GUI Emacs sessions. It enhances the visual presentation with icons,
doc popups and nicer candidate grouping without changing Company backends.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package company-box
  :after company
  :if (display-graphic-p)
  :custom
  (company-box-frame-behavior 'point)
  (company-box-show-single-candidate t)
  (company-box-doc-delay 1)

  :hook
  (company-mode . company-box-mode))
#+end_src


** Static Analysis with Semgrep

Semgrep ("semantic grep") is a powerful open-source static analysis tool that
transcends standard text-based searching by performing semantic code pattern
matching. Unlike traditional regular-expression-based tools, Semgrep understands
the underlying structure of your code-mapping it to an Abstract Syntax Tree
(AST)--which allows it to identify complex bugs, security vulnerabilities, and
coding anti-patterns with high precision. By using highly readable YAML-based
rules, you can enforce custom security policies, coding standards, and
architectural constraints across multiple languages, catching logical flaws in
your local environment that typical linters often overlook.

For now, we just install it in the system to allow it to be used by Emacs:

#+begin_src sh :tangle setup.sh
is_installed_semgrep() {
  command -v semgrep >/dev/null 2>&1
}

install_semgrep() {
  install_via_pipx "Semgrep" "semgrep"
}

uninstall_semgrep() {
  run_task "Uninstalling Semgrep" "pipx uninstall semgrep 2>/dev/null || true"
}

register_action "Install Semgrep" "semgrep" "standalone"
#+end_src


** General AI Assistant Configuration

This section covers the configuration of AI-powered tools integrated into Emacs
to enhance productivity, code generation, and interactive assistance. These
tools leverage advanced language models and AI capabilities to provide
intelligent suggestions, code completion, and interactive chat interfaces.

*** Encrypted API Keys for the AI providers

This section covers the configuration of encrypted API keys for the AI providers
(see next section). Here's how it works (functions are defined later in this file):

1. ~my/get-token-from-auth-source~ function:
   - Searches auth-source for a credential entry matching the given host
   - Looks for user ="apikey"= (common convention for API keys)
   - Requires both =:user= and =:secret= to be present
   - Returns the secret (API key) as a string, or signals an error if not found

2. ~my/eca-setup-tokens~ function:
   - Iterates over a list of providers, each mapping:
     - Environment variable name (e.g., ="ANTHROPIC_API_KEY"=)
     - Hostname (e.g., ="api.anthropic.com"=)
   - For each provider:
     - Calls ~my/get-token-from-auth-source~ with the hostname
     - If found, sets the environment variable using ~setenv~
     - Handles errors gracefully (missing keys don't crash the setup)

3. Deferred execution with idle timer:
   - Uses ~run-with-idle-timer~ with 0 delay to run after Emacs is idle
   - Why: auth-source backends (gpg-agent, pass, keychain) initialize lazily
     and may rely on async processes
   - During early startup, ~auth-source-search~ can return ~nil~ because:
     - The command loop isn't running yet
     - Async processes haven't completed
     - Backends may not be initialized
   - Waiting for the first idle moment ensures:
     - The command loop is running
     - Async processes can complete
     - Auth-source backends are initialized

The flow is as follows:

1. Emacs starts up
2. ECA package (see next section) loads (after auth-source)
3. Idle timer waits for first idle moment
4. ~my/eca-setup-tokens~ runs:
   - For each provider (Anthropic, OpenAI, etc.)
   - Searches =~/.authinfo.gpg= for entry matching host and extracts the API key
   - Sets it as environment variable
5. ECA can now use these environment variables to authenticate with the AI providers


*** ECA (Editor Code Assistant)

ECA (Editor Code Assistant) is a powerful AI assistant designed to integrate
seamlessly with Emacs. It provides advanced features such as code generation,
refactoring, debugging, and interactive chat. ECA supports multiple AI models
and can be configured to work alongside or as an alternative to GitHub Copilot,
depending on your workflow and preferences.

We start with a few helper functions to retrieve tokens from the auth-source and
check if the machine is authorized for GitHub Copilot. We do not enable ECA if
copilot is enabled in the current machine. In addition, we have a helper to
install the ECA config file to the global path.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(defun my/get-token-from-auth-source (host)
  "Find token in auth-source for HOST."
  (let* ((found (auth-source-search
                 :host host
                 :user "apikey"
                 :require '(:user :secret)
                 :max 1))
         (entry (car found))
         (secret-fn (when entry (plist-get entry :secret))))

    (cond
     ((null found)
      (error "Token not found for host %s user %s" host "apikey"))
     ((null secret-fn)
      (error "Secret not available for host %s user %s (backend may not support secrets)" host "apikey"))
     (t
      (funcall secret-fn)))))

(defun my/ai-copilot-authorized-p ()
  "Check if this machine is authorized for GitHub Copilot.
Returns t only if a valid entry with secret exists in auth-source.
Returns nil if auth-source is not ready or key is missing."
  (let ((auth-file (expand-file-name "~/.config/github-copilot/apps.json")))
    (and (file-exists-p auth-file)
         (file-readable-p auth-file)
         ;; Check if the file is not just an empty JSON object "{}"
         (> (file-attribute-size (file-attributes auth-file)) 5))))
#+end_src


**** ECA Config Installation with Default Model Override

The ~my/eca-install-config~ function syncs =eca-config.json= from your Emacs
directory to =~/.config/eca/config.json=. It supports overriding the
=defaultModel= setting via a local variable:

1. **Default model in config**: The =eca-config.json= file has =defaultModel=
   set to a copilot model by default.

2. **Local override variable**: You can set ~my/eca-default-model~ in =local.el=
   to override the default model for your machine:

#+begin_example
;;; -*- lexical-binding: t -*-
;;; local.el - Local configuration that doesn't need to be in version control
;;;
;;; This file is loaded after custom.el and init.el
;;; Use this for machine-specific settings

;; ECA default model override
;; Set this to override the default model in eca-config.json
;; If nil or not set, uses copilot from eca-config.json
;; Example: (setq my/eca-default-model "mistral/devstral-2512")
(setq my/eca-default-model "mistral/devstral-2512")
#+end_example

3. **Replacement process**: When ~my/eca-install-config~ runs:
   - It reads the source =eca-config.json= file
   - If ~my/eca-default-model~ is set, it replaces the =defaultModel= value
     in the JSON content before comparing or copying
   - The replacement happens =before= comparing with the existing target file,
     so the comparison uses the processed content
   - The processed content is then written to =~/.config/eca/config.json=

4. **Benefits**: This allows you to:
   - Keep ="copilot"= as the default in version control
   - Override it per-machine via =local.el= (which is typically not versioned)
   - Have different models on different machines without conflicts

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(defun my/eca-install-config ()
  "Sync eca-config.json to global path.
Replaces defaultModel with value from my/eca-default-model if set.
Skips if file exists and is identical, backups if different."
  ;; Ensure local.el is loaded to get my/eca-default-model if it exists
  (let ((local-file (concat user-emacs-directory "local.el")))
    (when (and (file-exists-p local-file)
               (not (boundp 'my/eca-default-model)))
      (load-file local-file)))
  (let* ((source (expand-file-name "eca-config.json" user-emacs-directory))
         (dest-dir (expand-file-name "~/.config/eca/"))
         (dest-file (expand-file-name "config.json" dest-dir))
         (default-model (when (bound-and-true-p my/eca-default-model)
                          my/eca-default-model))
         (replace-default-model (lambda (content)
                                 "Replace defaultModel in JSON content if override is set."
                                 (if default-model
                                     (replace-regexp-in-string
                                      "\"defaultModel\"\\s-*:\\s-*\"[^\"]*\""
                                      (format "\"defaultModel\" : \"%s\"" default-model)
                                      content)
                                   content))))

    (unless (file-exists-p dest-dir)
      (make-directory dest-dir t))

    (if (file-exists-p source)
        (let* ((source-content (with-temp-buffer
                                 (insert-file-contents source)
                                 (buffer-string)))
               (processed-content (funcall replace-default-model source-content)))

          (if (file-exists-p dest-file)
              (let ((dest-content (with-temp-buffer
                                    (insert-file-contents dest-file)
                                    (buffer-string))))
                (if (string= processed-content dest-content)
                    (message "ECA [✓]: Config is already installed and up to date.")

                  (if (y-or-n-p "ECA: Global config has changed. Overwrite with Emacs config? ")
                      (progn
                        (rename-file dest-file (concat dest-file ".~" (format-time-string "%Y%m%d-%H%M%S") "~") t)
                        (with-temp-file dest-file
                          (insert processed-content))
                        (message "ECA [✓]: Config updated and backed up.%s"
                                 (if default-model
                                     (format " (defaultModel set to %s)" default-model)
                                   ""))
                        (when (fboundp 'eca-restart-server) (eca-restart-server)))
                    (message "ECA [-]: Config update skipped by user."))))
            (progn
              (with-temp-file dest-file
                (insert processed-content))
              (message "ECA [✓]: Config installed to global path.%s"
                       (if default-model
                           (format " (defaultModel set to %s)" default-model)
                         "")))))
      (message "ECA [x]: Source eca-config.json not found!"))))
#+end_src


Finally, we setup the ECA package and its completion mode:

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
;; ECA tries to use evil-mode functions. If Evil is not installed, we see warnings.
;; To prevent those warnings from appearing, we define placeholders for the
;; Evil functions that ECA tries to use.
(unless (fboundp 'evil-delete)
  (defun evil-delete (&rest _)
    "Placeholder for evil-delete (Evil mode not installed)."
    (interactive)))

(unless (fboundp 'evil-change-line)
  (defun evil-change-line (&rest _)
    "Placeholder for evil-change-line (Evil mode not installed)."
    (interactive)))

(use-package eca
  :after auth-source
  :pin "MELPA Unstable"
  :custom
  (eca-chat-select-model "google/gemini-3-flash-preview") ; Your preferred "Brain"

  :config
  ;; Use ediff for file change diffs (smerge is for merge conflicts, not file changes)
  (setq eca-chat-diff-tool 'ediff)
  (setq eca-extra-args '("--verbose" "--log-level" "debug"))
  (add-hook 'eca-chat-mode-hook (lambda () (setq-local show-trailing-whitespace nil)))

  (defun my/eca-init (&rest _args)
    "Ensure ECA config is installed."
    (unless (and (boundp 'my/eca-initialized-p) my/eca-initialized-p)
      (condition-case err
          (progn
            ;; Ensure local.el is loaded (it's loaded in after-init-hook, but be safe)
            (let ((local-file (concat user-emacs-directory "local.el")))
              (when (and (file-exists-p local-file)
                         (not (boundp 'my/eca-default-model)))
                (load-file local-file)))
            (my/eca-setup-tokens)
            (my/eca-install-config)
            (setq my/eca-initialized-p t))
        (error (message "ECA: Failed to load local.el: %s" (error-message-string err))))))

  (defvar my/eca-initialized-p nil
    "Non-nil when `my/eca-init' has run this session.")

  ;; Define completion setup function after ECA is fully loaded
  ;; ECA uses eca-completion-mode (overlay-based), not company-mode
  (with-eval-after-load 'eca
    (defun my/eca-setup-completion (&optional _arg)
      "Enable ECA completion mode only if Copilot is disabled.
ECA uses its own overlay-based completion system, not company-mode.
Optional _ARG is ignored (for compatibility with advice)."
      (interactive)

      (if (my/ai-copilot-authorized-p)
          ;; If Copilot is ON, disable ECA completion to avoid conflicts
          (when (bound-and-true-p eca-completion-mode)
            (eca-completion-mode -1)
            (message "ECA [-]: Skipping ECA completion because Copilot is enabled"))
        ;; If Copilot is OFF, enable ECA completion
        (when (and (fboundp 'eca-completion-mode) (not (derived-mode-p 'eca-chat-mode)))
          (my/eca-init) ;; Ensure ECA is initialized before enabling completion
          (eca-completion-mode 1)
          (message "ECA [✓]: Using ECA for ghost completions"))))

    ;; Special handling for org-mode: org-mode uses org-self-insert-command instead of self-insert-command
    ;; We need to make ECA's post-command function recognize org-mode's insertion commands
    (when (fboundp 'eca-completion--post-command)
      (defun my/eca-org-post-command-wrapper (orig-fn)
        "Wrapper for ECA post-command that handles org-mode's insertion commands."
        (if (and (derived-mode-p 'org-mode)
                 (eq this-command 'org-self-insert-command))
            ;; In org-mode, treat org-self-insert-command like self-insert-command
            (let ((this-command 'self-insert-command))
              (funcall orig-fn))
          ;; For other modes, call normally
          (funcall orig-fn)))

      (advice-add 'eca-completion--post-command :around #'my/eca-org-post-command-wrapper))

    ;; Set up hooks after ECA is loaded and function is defined
    (dolist (hook '(prog-mode-hook
                    text-mode-hook
                    markdown-mode-hook
                    org-mode-hook
                    yaml-ts-mode-hook
                    json-ts-mode-hook
                    sh-mode-hook
                    dockerfile-mode-hook
                    css-mode-hook
                    sql-mode-hook
                    conf-mode-hook
                    shell-mode-hook
                    term-mode-hook
                    vterm-mode-hook))
      (add-hook hook #'my/eca-setup-completion))

    ;; Advice copilot-mode to update ECA completion when toggled
    (when (fboundp 'copilot-mode)
      (advice-add 'copilot-mode :after #'my/eca-setup-completion)))

  ;; Setup tokens when auth-source backends are initialized
  (defun my/eca-setup-tokens ()
    "Setup ECA provider tokens from auth-source."
    (let ((providers '(("ANTHROPIC_API_KEY"   "api.anthropic.com")
                       ("DEEPSEEK_API_KEY"    "api.deepseek.com")
                       ("GEMINI_API_KEY"      "generativelanguage.googleapis.com")
                       ("GROQ_API_KEY"        "api.groq.com")
                       ("HUGGINGFACE_API_KEY" "router.huggingface.co")
                       ("KAGI_API_KEY"        "kagi.com")
                       ("MISTRAL_API_KEY"     "api.mistral.ai")
                       ("MOONSHOT_API_KEY"    "api.moonshot.cn")
                       ("NOVITA_API_KEY"      "api.novita.ai")
                       ("OPENAI_API_KEY"      "api.openai.com")
                       ("OPENROUTER_API_KEY"  "openrouter.ai")
                       ("PERPLEXITY_API_KEY"  "api.perplexity.ai"))))
      (dolist (provider providers)
        (let ((env-var (car provider))
              (host    (cadr provider)))
          (condition-case err
              (let ((token (my/get-token-from-auth-source host)))
                (if token
                    (progn
                      (setenv env-var token)
                      (message "ECA [✓]: Key %s initialized with token for %s from credentials" env-var host))
                  (message "ECA [-]: Skipping key %s: no token found for host (%s) on credentials" env-var host)))
            (error
             (message "ECA [-]: Skipping key %s: %s"
                      env-var (error-message-string err))))))))

  (defun my/eca-toggle ()
    "Toggle ECA.
If the window is visible, stop the process and kill it.
Otherwise, start a fresh session."
      (interactive)
      (let ((eca-buf (cl-find-if (lambda (b)
                                   (string-match-p "^<eca-chat" (buffer-name b)))
                                 (buffer-list))))
        (if (and eca-buf (get-buffer-window eca-buf))
            (progn
              (eca-stop)
              (delete-window (get-buffer-window eca-buf))
              (dolist (buf (buffer-list))
                (when (string-match-p "^<eca" (buffer-name buf))
                  (kill-buffer buf)))
              (message "ECA Sessions Terminated."))
          (eca))))

  (advice-add 'eca :before #'my/eca-init)

  ;; NOTE: auth-source backends (gpg-agent, pass, keychain, etc.) are initialized
  ;; lazily and may rely on async subprocesses and the Emacs event loop.
  ;;
  ;; During early startup (even in `after-init-hook`), Emacs has not yet entered
  ;; the command loop, so async processes and process filters may not have run.
  ;; As a result, `auth-source-search` can return nil on the first call.
  ;;
  ;; Using an idle timer guarantees that Emacs is fully "alive":
  ;; - the command loop is running
  ;; - async processes can complete
  ;; - auth-source backends are initialized
  ;;
  ;; A 1-second delay ensures ECA and its dependencies (auth-source, package loading,
  ;; server connection) have sufficient time to initialize properly. A 0-second delay
  ;; was originally enough (the key was waiting for the first idle moment), but it
  ;; became insufficient after adding a delay for starting up copilot-mode on buffers,
  ;; causing ECA to start with an empty buffer.
  ;; This also prevents files from opening on top of the ECA chat buffer.
  ;;
  ;; Uncommment to start ECA automatically after startup:
  ;; (run-with-idle-timer 1 nil #'eca)

  (define-key custom-bindings-map (kbd "C-c e") #'my/eca-toggle))
#+end_src


**** ECA over TRAMP

I found a problem where the package suffers from a "paradox": it intends to be
helpful but constantly probes files as if they were local, effectively "DDOSing"
your high-latency TRAMP/Docker connection and causing those persistent freezes.
I solved it with a TRAMP-aware protective wrapper using advice-add, which acts
as a filter to intercept and guard the function calls; this ensures the heavy
cursor-tracking logic only executes on local buffers while safely disabling it
for remote connections, keeping your Docker sessions stable and responsive.

This might limit how ECA works. I will revisit this solution in the future.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(defun my/eca-chat-track-cursor-safeguard (orig-fun &rest args)
  "Wrap the package's tracking function to prevent remote TRAMP spamming.
Applies ORIG-FUN with ARGS."
  (if (file-remote-p default-directory)
      ;; SAFETY: If remote, skip the heavy probe to prevent freezing
      nil
    ;; NORMAL: If local, run the original function as normal
    (apply orig-fun args)))

;; Attach the guard to the package function
(advice-add 'eca-chat--track-cursor :around #'my/eca-chat-track-cursor-safeguard)
#+end_src


**** ECA Chat Copilot autocompletion

This section configures key integration between Copilot's autocompletion and the
ECA Chat (Editor Code Assistant) within Emacs. It ensures that when both Copilot
and ECA Chat are active, pressing ~Tab~ will accept Copilot completions when
available, otherwise, it will fall back to ECA Chat's behavior.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(with-eval-after-load 'eca-chat
  (advice-add 'eca-chat--key-pressed-tab :around
              (lambda (orig-fun &rest args)
                (if (and (featurep 'copilot)
                         (boundp 'copilot-mode)
                         copilot-mode
                         (fboundp 'copilot-accept-completion)
                         (copilot-accept-completion))
                    t  ;; Copilot took the Tab, suppress ECA logic
                  (apply orig-fun args)))))
#+end_src


**** ECA Chat Management (Auto-save and Index)

This section adds automatic saving of ECA chats to a dedicated folder and
maintains an Org-mode index for easy navigation.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(declare-function eca-process-running-p "eca")
(declare-function eca-session "eca")
(declare-function eca-chat--custom-title "eca-chat")
(declare-function eca-chat--get-last-buffer "eca-chat")
(declare-function eca-chat--title "eca-chat")

(defvar eca-chat-mode-map nil "Keymap for ECA chat mode.")

(defvar my/eca-chat-directory (expand-file-name "eca-chats/" user-emacs-directory)
  "Directory to save ECA chats.")

(defvar-local my/eca-chat-last-saved-file nil
  "The last file where this chat was saved.")

(defun my/eca-chat-get-file-name ()
  "Get a filename for the current ECA chat."
  (or my/eca-chat-last-saved-file
      (let* ((title (or (and (boundp 'eca-chat--custom-title) eca-chat--custom-title)
                        (and (boundp 'eca-chat--title) eca-chat--title)
                        "untitled"))
             ;; Sanitize title for filename
             (safe-title (replace-regexp-in-string "[^a-zA-Z0-9_-]" "_" title))
             (id (or (and (boundp 'eca-chat--id) eca-chat--id) "new"))
             (date-time (format-time-string "%Y-%m-%d_%H-%M-%S")))
        (setq-local my/eca-chat-last-saved-file
                    (expand-file-name (format "%s_%s_%s.md"
                                              date-time
                                              id
                                              safe-title)
                                      my/eca-chat-directory)))))

(defun my/eca-chat-update-index (file)
  "Update the index.org file with the new chat FILE."
  (let ((index-file (expand-file-name "index.org" my/eca-chat-directory))
        (title (file-name-base file))
        (relative-path (file-name-nondirectory file)))
    ;; Ensure directory exists
    (unless (file-exists-p my/eca-chat-directory)
      (make-directory my/eca-chat-directory t))
    ;; Initialize index file if it doesn't exist
    (unless (file-exists-p index-file)
      (with-temp-file index-file
        (insert "#+TITLE: ECA Chats Index\n\n")))
    ;; Insert link at the top (after header) if not present
    (save-excursion
      (let ((buf (find-file-noselect index-file)))
        (with-current-buffer buf
          (goto-char (point-min))
          ;; Prune orphaned links while we are here
          (while (re-search-forward "^- \\[\\[file:\\([^]]+\\)\\]" nil t)
            (let ((target (expand-file-name (match-string 1) my/eca-chat-directory)))
              (unless (file-exists-p target)
                (delete-region (line-beginning-position) (line-beginning-position 2)))))
          (goto-char (point-min))
          (unless (search-forward relative-path nil t)
            ;; Find the end of the header or title
            (goto-char (point-min))
            (if (re-search-forward "^- " nil t)
                (goto-char (match-beginning 0))
              (goto-char (point-max)))
            (let ((display-title (if (string-match-p "_reset_" relative-path)
                                     (format "↺ %s" title)
                                   title)))
              (insert (format "- [[file:%s][%s]]\n" relative-path display-title))
              (save-buffer))))))))

(defun my/eca-chat-rebuild-index ()
  "Rebuild the ECA chats index.org from all files in the directory.
Useful if the index gets out of sync or files are manually deleted."
  (interactive)
  (let ((index-file (expand-file-name "index.org" my/eca-chat-directory))
        (files (seq-filter #'file-exists-p
                           (directory-files my/eca-chat-directory t "\\.md$"))))
    (with-temp-file index-file
      (insert "#+TITLE: ECA Chats Index (Rebuilt)\n\n")
      (dolist (file (sort files #'string>)) ; Sort newest first
        (unless (string-match-p "index\\.org$" file)
          (let* ((relative-path (file-name-nondirectory file))
                 (title (file-name-base relative-path))
                 (display-title (if (string-match-p "_reset_" relative-path)
                                    (format "↺ %s" title)
                                  title)))
            (insert (format "- [[file:%s][%s]]\n"
                            relative-path
                            display-title))))))
    (message "ECA: Index rebuilt with %d chats." (length files))))

(defun my/eca-chat-auto-save-h ()
  "Auto-save the current ECA chat buffer."
  (when (and (derived-mode-p 'eca-chat-mode)
             (not (and (boundp 'eca-chat--chat-loading) eca-chat--chat-loading))
             (not (and (boundp 'eca-chat--empty) eca-chat--empty)))
    (unless (file-exists-p my/eca-chat-directory)
      (make-directory my/eca-chat-directory t))
    (let ((file (my/eca-chat-get-file-name))
          (inhibit-message t))
      ;; Use write-region to save without visiting/changing the buffer file name
      (write-region (point-min) (point-max) file nil 'quiet)
      (my/eca-chat-update-index file))))

;; Advice to trigger auto-save when ECA finishes loading a response
(advice-add 'eca-chat--set-chat-loading :after
            (lambda (&rest args)
              (let ((loading (cadr args)))
                (when (not loading)
                  (my/eca-chat-auto-save-h)))))

;; Also save when killing the buffer
(add-hook 'eca-chat-mode-hook
          (lambda ()
            (add-hook 'kill-buffer-hook #'my/eca-chat-auto-save-h nil t)))

(defun my/eca-chat-rename-to-backup-h ()
  "Rename the current chat file to a backup if it exists."
  (let ((session (eca-session)))
    (when (eca-process-running-p session)
      (when-let ((buffer (eca-chat--get-last-buffer session)))
        (with-current-buffer buffer
          (message "ECA: Backing up chat file...")
          (let ((file (my/eca-chat-get-file-name)))
            (when (file-exists-p file)
              (let* ((timestamp (format-time-string "%Y%m%d-%H%M%S"))
                     (backup (concat (file-name-sans-extension file) "_reset_" timestamp ".md")))
                (rename-file file backup t)
                (my/eca-chat-update-index backup)
                (setq-local my/eca-chat-last-saved-file nil)
                (message "ECA: Chat backed up and indexed: %s" (file-name-nondirectory backup))))))))))

;; Advice to backup the chat file before it's reset/cleared
(advice-add 'eca-chat-reset :before #'my/eca-chat-rename-to-backup-h)

;; Navigation commands
(defun my/eca-chat-mark-buffer (&optional proj-context)
  "Mark buffer as ECA and read-only, safely set project context.
Uses PROJ-CONTEXT."
  (read-only-mode 1)
  (setq-local my/eca-chat-buffer t)
  (when proj-context
    (setq-local my/original-project-context
      ;; --- SAFETY FILTER: TRAMP STABILITY ---
      ;; Broken paths  like '/docker:node@4e8...:/home/user/.cache/emacs/(("‘/workspace’") 1 ... )')
      ;; started to appear because the project context was somehow serialized as
      ;; a Lisp list (containing project metadata like the root, status, and
      ;; buffer objects) rather than a simple file path string.
      ;;
      ;; Emacs then treated this serialized object as a valid POSIX file path. When TRAMP
      ;; (the remote file handler) received this mangled path, it attempted to perform
      ;; file operations (like 'stat' or 'test -d') on it within the Docker container.
      ;; Since this is not a real directory, it triggered the "File is missing" errors
      ;; and recursive TRAMP crash loops, freezing Emacs.
      ;;
      ;; We force extraction here to ensure that only a pure, valid path string
      ;; ever enters our state variables.
      (let ((root (cond
                    ((stringp proj-context) proj-context)
                    ((and (listp proj-context) (fboundp 'project-root))
                     (condition-case nil
                         (project-root proj-context)
                       (error nil)))))) ;; Silently catch errors if project-root fails

        ;; FINAL SAFETY CHECK: Only allow the value if it exists as a directory.
        ;; If 'root' is not a valid directory string, we return nil to
        ;; discard the corrupted data entirely.
        (if (and (stringp root) (file-directory-p root))
            root
          nil)))))

(defun my/eca-chat-open-index ()
  "Open the ECA chats index file."
  (interactive)
  (let ((index-file (expand-file-name "index.org" my/eca-chat-directory))
        ;; Save current project BEFORE opening the file
        (current-project (project-current)))
    (find-file index-file)
    (my/eca-chat-mark-buffer current-project)))

(defun my/eca-chat-browse ()
  "Browse ECA chat files using completion."
  (interactive)
  (let* ((default-directory my/eca-chat-directory)
         ;; Save the current project using project.el
         (current-project (project-current)))
    (if (fboundp 'counsel-find-file)
        (counsel-find-file)
      (call-interactively #'find-file))
    ;; Make the opened buffer read-only and mark as ECA chat buffer
    (run-with-idle-timer 0 nil
      (lambda (proj-context)
        (when (buffer-live-p (current-buffer))
          (with-current-buffer (current-buffer)
            (my/eca-chat-mark-buffer proj-context))))
      current-project)))

(declare-function org-element-context "org")
(declare-function org-element-property "org")

(defun my/eca-chat-follow-link-no-project (orig-fun &rest args)
  "Advice for org-open-at-point to open chat files without changing project.
Preserves the current project context when opening linked files.

- ORIG-FUN is the original function passed by advice.
- ARGS: Arguments passed to the original function (ignored)."
  (let* ((context (org-element-context))
         (type (org-element-property :type context))
         (_path (org-element-property :path context))
         ;; Save current project before opening (using project.el)
         (current-project (project-current)))
    ;; Only handle file links in the eca-chats directory
    (if (and (string= type "file")
             (buffer-file-name)
             (string-prefix-p (expand-file-name my/eca-chat-directory)
                              (expand-file-name (buffer-file-name))))
        (progn
          ;; Open the file normally
          (apply orig-fun args)
          (my/eca-chat-mark-buffer current-project))
      ;; For all other cases, use the original function
      (apply orig-fun args))))

(advice-add 'org-open-at-point :around #'my/eca-chat-follow-link-no-project)

;; Recursion guard: This variable prevents the function from calling itself
;; while it is already trying to resolve a context. Without this, TRAMP
;; handshakes (which trigger filesystem probes) can cause an infinite loop.
(defvar my/eca-chat-in-progress nil
  "Flag to prevent recursive calls during project context resolution.")

(defun my/eca-chat-restore-project-context (orig-fun &rest args)
  "Return the original project context for ECA chat buffers, with local filtering.

This function acts as a firewall: it intercepts calls to `project-current'.
If an ECA chat buffer is trapped in a bad state, it cleans the directory
locally before allowing the original `project-current' function to execute.

This avoids infinite recursion by not calling filesystem primitives like
`file-directory-p' through an advice wrapper.

- ORIG-FUN is the original function passed by advice.
- ARGS: Arguments passed to the original function."

  (let ((context (and (boundp 'my/eca-chat-buffer)
                      my/eca-chat-buffer
                      (boundp 'my/original-project-context)
                      my/original-project-context)))

    ;; 1. CHECK CONTEXT LOCALLY:
    ;; We only check the variable content. We avoid calling complex
    ;; filesystem checks here to prevent triggering TRAMP or recursion.
    (if (and (stringp context)
             (not (string-match-p "[()\[\]]" context)))
        context

      ;; 2. ENVIRONMENT HEALING:
      ;; If the context variable is garbage (corrupted), we reset the
      ;; buffer's 'default-directory' to a safe local path (~/).
      ;; This prevents 'project-current' from trying to scan a
      ;; broken/malformed path.
      (when (and (stringp default-directory)
                 (string-match-p "cache/emacs" default-directory))
        (setq-local default-directory (expand-file-name "~/")))

      ;; 3. EXECUTE ORIGINAL:
      ;; Now it is safe to let Emacs run the standard project discovery.
      (apply orig-fun args))))

(advice-add 'project-current :around #'my/eca-chat-restore-project-context)

;; Bindings for ECA chat mode
(with-eval-after-load 'eca-chat
  (define-key eca-chat-mode-map (kbd "C-c C-S-i") #'my/eca-chat-open-index))

;; Global binding for finding chats
(global-set-key (kbd "C-c c f") #'my/eca-chat-browse)
(global-set-key (kbd "C-c c i") #'my/eca-chat-open-index)
(global-set-key (kbd "C-c c r") #'my/eca-chat-rebuild-index)
#+end_src


*** GitHub Copilot

GitHub Copilot integration for Emacs. It provides AI-powered code suggestions as
you type.

**Installation Requirements:**

1. =Node.js= and the =copilot-language-server=

#+begin_src sh :tangle setup.sh
is_installed_copilot() {
  [ -f "$TOOLS_DIR/node_modules/.bin/copilot-language-server" ] || command -v copilot-language-server >/dev/null 2>&1
}

install_copilot() {
  install_nodejs_tools
}

uninstall_copilot() {
  uninstall_nodejs_tools
}

register_action "Install GitHub Copilot" "copilot" "node"
#+end_src

2. Copilot Language Server configuration in Emacs

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(declare-function my/copilot-kubernetes-sensitive-p nil)
(declare-function my/copilot-excluded-file-p nil)

(use-package copilot
  ;; We activate the copilot extension only if it is authorized on this computer.
  ;; This will prevent us from loading it if not in use:
  :if (my/ai-copilot-authorized-p)
  :pin "MELPA Unstable"
  :after eca
  :init
  ;; Disable indentation offset warnings - Copilot will use existing indentation
  ;; See https://github.com/copilot-emacs/copilot.el/issues/312#issuecomment-2729052774
  (setq copilot-indent-offset-warning-disable t)

  ;; Disable buffer size warnings - prevents "file size exceeds copilot-max-char" warnings for large files
  ;; Not working yet. See https://github.com/copilot-emacs/copilot.el/issues/313
  (setq-default copilot-max-char-warning-disabled t)

  :custom
  ;; Provide valid settings structure to prevent "Cannot read properties of null (reading 'copilot')" error
  (copilot-lsp-settings '(:copilot ()))

  :config
  ;; Configure Copilot to use the server from our PATH
  (let ((copilot-server (executable-find "copilot-language-server"))
        (node-binary (executable-find "node")))

    ;; Add NODE_NO_WARNINGS=1 to environment for Copilot server, or it will spam warnings
    ;; about deprecated Node.js APIs on stderr that cause Copilot to malfunction.
    (setenv "NODE_NO_WARNINGS" "1")

    ;; Check Node.js version (Copilot requires >= 22.0.0)
    (if (or (not node-binary)
            (let* ((version-output (shell-command-to-string (concat node-binary " -v")))
                   (major-version (when (string-match "v\\([0-9]+\\)" version-output)
                                    (string-to-number (match-string 1 version-output)))))
              (< (or major-version 0) 22)))
        (progn
          (warn "Copilot disabled: Node.js v22+ required, found %s"
                (if node-binary (string-trim (shell-command-to-string (concat node-binary " -v"))) "none"))
          (setq copilot-server-executable nil))

      (if (and copilot-server node-binary)
          (progn
            (setq copilot-server-executable copilot-server)
            (message "Copilot: Using server at %s" copilot-server))
        (message "Copilot: No language server found. Run setup.sh to install locally.")
        (setq copilot-mode nil))))

  ;; Files to exclude from Copilot
  (defvar my/copilot-excluded-files
    '("\\.env\\(\\..*\\)?$"
      "\\.config$"
      "\\.cfg$"
      "secret"
      "credential"
      "^id_rsa"
      "\\.key$"
      "\\.log$"
      "\\.tmp$"
      "\\.ini$"
      "\\.txt$")
    "List of regex patterns for files to exclude from Copilot.")

  (defun my/copilot-kubernetes-sensitive-p ()
    "Check if current buffer contains Kubernetes sensitive content.
Returns non-nil if buffer contains Kubernetes Secrets or ConfigMaps."
    (save-excursion
      (goto-char (point-min))
      (and (re-search-forward "^apiVersion:" nil t)
           (goto-char (point-min))
           (re-search-forward "^kind:\\s-*\\(Secret\\|ConfigMap\\)\\s-*$" nil t))))

  (defun my/tab-indent-or-complete ()
    (interactive)
    (cond
     ;; 1. If Company menu is open, select the completion
     ((company-tooltip-visible-p)
      (company-complete-selection))
     ;; 2. If Copilot has a suggestion, accept it
     ((copilot-accept-completion)
      (copilot-accept-completion))
     ;; 3. Otherwise, do the default Emacs indentation
     (t
      (indent-for-tab-command))))

  (defun my/copilot-excluded-file-p ()
    "Check if current file should be excluded from Copilot."
    (or
     ;; Check filename patterns
     (and buffer-file-name
          (let ((filename (file-name-nondirectory buffer-file-name)))
            (cl-some (lambda (pattern)
                       (string-match-p pattern filename))
                     my/copilot-excluded-files)))
     ;; Check directory patterns
     (and buffer-file-name
          (string-match-p "/\\(dist\\|build\\|vendor\\)/" buffer-file-name))
     ;; Check content patterns
     (my/copilot-kubernetes-sensitive-p)))

  (defun my/safe-copilot-mode ()
    "Enable copilot-mode with silent error handling, excluding sensitive files.
Defers activation to ensure Copilot server is ready."
    (unless (or (file-remote-p (or (buffer-file-name) default-directory))
                (my/copilot-excluded-file-p))
      (let ((buf (current-buffer)))
        (run-with-idle-timer
         0.5 nil
         (lambda ()
           (when (buffer-live-p buf)
             (with-current-buffer buf
               (condition-case err
                   (when (and (featurep 'copilot)
                              (boundp 'copilot-server-executable)
                              copilot-server-executable
                              (file-executable-p copilot-server-executable))
                     (copilot-mode 1))
                 (error
                  ;; Only report errors if copilot is loaded (real errors)
                  (when (featurep 'copilot)
                    (message "Copilot activation failed: %S" err)))))))))))

  :hook ((prog-mode . my/safe-copilot-mode)
         (text-mode . my/safe-copilot-mode)
         (markdown-mode . my/safe-copilot-mode)
         (yaml-ts-mode . my/safe-copilot-mode)
         (json-ts-mode . my/safe-copilot-mode)
         (sh-mode . my/safe-copilot-mode)
         (dockerfile-mode . my/safe-copilot-mode)
         (css-mode . my/safe-copilot-mode)
         (sql-mode . my/safe-copilot-mode)
         (conf-mode . my/safe-copilot-mode))
  :bind (("C-c c t" . copilot-mode)         ; Toggle copilot in buffer
         ("C-c c l" . copilot-login)         ; Login to copilot
         ("C-c c d" . copilot-diagnose)      ; Check copilot status
         :map copilot-completion-map
         ("C-g" . copilot-clear-overlay)     ; Clear suggestion
         ("M-p" . copilot-previous-completion)
         ("M-n" . copilot-next-completion)
         ("TAB" . my/tab-indent-or-complete)))
#+end_src


*** GitHub Copilot Chat

GitHub Copilot Chat provides an interactive chat interface with GitHub Copilot,
similar to what's available in VS Code or IntelliJ. This is a separate package
from the completion-only ~copilot.el~. It enables natural language interactions
with Copilot, allowing you to ask questions, request code explanations, generate
tests, and more, all within Emacs.

**Features:**
- Interactive chat with GitHub Copilot
- Code explanation, review, and generation
- Multiple frontend options (Org-mode, Markdown, Shell-maker)
- Integration with your codebase context
- Transient menu interface for easy access

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package copilot-chat
  ;; We activate the copilot chat extension only if it is authorized on this computer.
  ;; This will prevent us from loading it if not in use:
  :if (my/ai-copilot-authorized-p)
  :pin "MELPA Unstable"
  :after (org markdown-mode)

  :custom
  ;; Use org-mode as the frontend (recommended for Emacs users)
  (copilot-chat-frontend 'org)

  ;; Configure the default model (will be updated from API)
  (copilot-chat-default-model "gpt-4o")

  ;; Follow chat buffer automatically
  (copilot-chat-follow t)

  :bind (("C-c p c" . copilot-chat-display)           ; Open chat
         ("C-c p h" . copilot-chat-hide)              ; Hide chat
         ("C-c p e" . copilot-chat-explain)           ; Explain selection
         ("C-c p r" . copilot-chat-review)            ; Review code
         ("C-c p f" . copilot-chat-fix)               ; Fix code
         ("C-c p d" . copilot-chat-doc)               ; Generate docs
         ("C-c p t" . copilot-chat-test)              ; Generate tests
         ("C-c p o" . copilot-chat-optimize)          ; Optimize code
         ("C-c p i" . copilot-chat-ask-and-insert)    ; Ask and insert
         ("C-c p m" . copilot-chat-transient)         ; Main menu
         ("C-c p y" . copilot-chat-yank)              ; Yank code block
         ("C-c p M-y" . copilot-chat-yank-pop)        ; Yank previous
         :map global-map
         ("C-c p C-y" . (lambda () (interactive) (copilot-chat-yank-pop -1))))

  :config
  ;; Add current buffer automatically when opening chat
  (declare-function copilot-chat-add-current-buffer "copilot-chat")
  (add-hook 'copilot-chat-mode-hook #'copilot-chat-add-current-buffer))
#+end_src


** IDE functionality with LSP

LSP (Language Server Protocol) brings IDE-like features to Emacs by communicating
with language servers for your programming language. The following packages work
together to provide a full-featured development experience:

*** lsp-mode: Core LSP client

**** The main package

~lsp-mode~ is the main package that connects Emacs to language servers, enabling
code completion, go-to-definition, diagnostics, and more.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package lsp-mode
  :pin "MELPA Unstable"
  :init
  ;; Needed for TRAMP performance
  (setq lsp-auto-register-remote-clients t)
  (setq lsp-auto-require-clients nil) ; Do not load unnecessary clients

  :bind
  (:map lsp-mode-map ("M-RET" . lsp-execute-code-action))

  ;; Save automatically after edits such as lsp-rename
  :hook
  (lsp-after-apply-edits . save-buffer)
  (prog-mode . lsp-deferred)

  ;; Increase timeout for slow language servers
  :custom
  (lsp-response-timeout 20)

  ;; Configure LSP to work with TRAMP/devcontainer
  ;; When using TRAMP to access files inside devcontainer, LSP should use
  ;; the container's environment and paths
  ;;
  ;; lsp-use-plists: set to t for production.
  ;; - t (plists): Improves performance for TRAMP/remote LSP. Some language servers and
  ;;   LSP client code paths work more reliably when using plists rather than hash-tables
  ;;   for representing data, especially over slower or unstable remote connections.
  ;; - nil / unset (hash-tables): dockerfile-ls initializes, but you get "Unknown request
  ;;   method: nil" (incoming server requests use string keys, code expects :method), and
  ;;   semgrep-ls can crash (Jsonrpc: json conversion failed: Id.t) due to id format.
  ;;   Possible readon: dockerfile-ls requires plists.
  (lsp-use-plists t)

  :config
  (setq lsp-idle-delay 0.5
        lsp-enable-symbol-highlighting t
        lsp-signature-auto-activate t
        lsp-enable-on-type-formatting nil
        lsp-enable-indentation t
        lsp-format-on-save nil)

  ;; When using TRAMP with devcontainer, LSP servers run inside the container.
  ;; The container should have Node.js and language server extensions installed.
  ;; LSP-mode automatically handles TRAMP, but ESLint extension must be installed in the container.

  ;; Configure ESLint for Tramp/devcontainer
  ;; The local ESLint server path doesn't exist on remote machines.
  ;; We configure lsp-mode to use `vscode-eslint-language-server` which must be installed globally on the remote.
  ;; Run: `npm install -g vscode-langservers-extracted` on the remote/container.

  ;; Configure ESLint for Tramp/devcontainer
  ;; The local ESLint server path doesn't exist on remote machines.
  ;; Strategy: Disable the default `eslint` client (which hardcodes local paths)
  ;; and register a specialized `eslint-remote` client that uses the global server.

  ;; Performance tuning
  (setq read-process-output-max (* 1024 1024))

  ;; Prevent noisy language servers (like semgrep-ls) from prompting
  ;; to restart if they crash due to pipe breakage during Emacs shutdown.
  (add-hook 'kill-emacs-query-functions
            (lambda ()
              (setq lsp-restart 'ignore)
              t))

  ;; Globally ignore large/irrelevant directories
  (dolist (dir '("[/\\\\]node_modules\\'"
                 "[/\\\\]\\.git\\'"
                 "[/\\\\]\\.emacs\\.d\\'"
                 "[/\\\\]build\\'"
                 "[/\\\\]dist\\'"
                 "[/\\\\]target\\'"   ; Maven/Gradle build output
                 "[/\\\\]\\.mvn\\'"
                 "[/\\\\]\\.gradle\\'"
                 "[/\\\\]out\\'"))
    (add-to-list 'lsp-file-watch-ignored-directories dir))

  ;; Kill workspaces when their last buffer closes.
  ;; Default is t, which permanently accumulates every project you ever open
  ;; into .lsp-session-v1 and re-sends them all to every server on the next
  ;; startup. nil prunes workspaces (and session entries) as soon as their
  ;; last buffer closes, so each session only contains what is currently open.
  (setq lsp-keep-workspace-alive nil)

  ;; Add the following to $HOME/.ssh/config to reuse existing connections:
  ;; Host *
  ;;   ControlMaster auto
  ;;   ControlPath ~/.ssh/sockets/%r@%h-%p
  ;;   ControlPersist 600

  ;; Disable ESLint for TRAMP using connection-local variables
  ;; This sets lsp-eslint-enable to nil BEFORE the activation function runs
  ;; The activation function checks lsp-eslint-enable, so this prevents it from activating
  (connection-local-set-profile-variables
   'remote-lsp-disable-eslint
   '((lsp-eslint-enable . nil)))

  (connection-local-set-profiles
   '(:application tramp) 'remote-lsp-disable-eslint)

  (add-to-list 'lsp-language-id-configuration '(dotenv-mode . "none"))

  ;; Some languages do not have LSP servers, so disable the
  ;; "No LSP clients" warning for those modes
  (defun my/local-disable-lsp-warning-for-known-languages ()
    (when (derived-mode-p 'emacs-lisp-mode)
      (setq-local lsp-warn-no-matched-clients nil)))

  (add-hook 'emacs-lisp-mode-hook #'my/local-disable-lsp-warning-for-known-languages)

  (defun my-lsp-tramp-optimize-clients ()
    "Disable specific LSP clients when working over TRAMP.
The built-in ESLint client ID is 'eslint'. When lsp-auto-register-remote-clients
is t, lsp-mode may create remote versions automatically. We disable both 'eslint'
and use 'eslint-remote' instead for TRAMP buffers.

Add-on clients (like ESLint) are LSP clients registered with :add-on? t.
They can run alongside other language servers (e.g., TypeScript + ESLint).
Because they're add-on clients, they may bypass lsp-disabled-clients checks,
so we also set lsp-eslint-enable to nil for extra reliability."
    (when (file-remote-p (or (buffer-file-name) default-directory))
      ;; Disable the default 'eslint' client which tries to use local paths
      (setq-local lsp-disabled-clients (cons 'eslint (bound-and-true-p lsp-disabled-clients)))
      ;; Also disable via the enable flag (more reliable for add-on clients)
      ;; Add-on clients can bypass lsp-disabled-clients, so this is a safety net
      ;; Note: This is already set via connection-local, but we set it again as a safety net
      (setq-local lsp-eslint-enable nil)
      ;; Also highly recommended: disable file watchers on remote
      (setq-local lsp-enable-file-watchers nil)
      (message "LSP: Disabled 'eslint' client for TRAMP (using 'eslint-remote' instead)")))

  ;; Run on multiple hooks to catch the client before it initializes:
  ;; - lsp-before-initialize-hook: Earliest hook, runs before workspace initialization
  ;; - lsp-configure-hook: Main hook when LSP configures a buffer
  ;; - find-file-hook: Safety net for buffers opened before LSP initializes
  ;; Run on hooks to catch the client before it initializes:
  (add-hook 'lsp-before-initialize-hook #'my-lsp-tramp-optimize-clients)
  (add-hook 'lsp-configure-hook #'my-lsp-tramp-optimize-clients)

  ;; 2. Configure TRAMP paths
  (connection-local-set-profile-variables
   'remote-path-with-node
   '((tramp-remote-path . (tramp-own-remote-path
                           tramp-default-remote-path
                           "/usr/local/share/npm-global/bin"
                           "/usr/local/bin"
                           "/usr/bin"
                           "/bin"))))

  (connection-local-set-profiles
   '(:application tramp) 'remote-path-with-node)

  (with-eval-after-load 'lsp-mode
    ;; Also remove any already-registered remote eslint clients
    (when (gethash 'eslint lsp-clients)
      (let ((eslint-client (gethash 'eslint lsp-clients)))
        (when (lsp--client-remote? eslint-client)
          (remhash 'eslint lsp-clients)
          (message "LSP: Removed pre-existing auto-registered remote 'eslint' client"))))

    ;; Silence "Unknown notification: window/workDoneProgress/create" (semgrep-ls and
    ;; others send it as a notification; lsp-mode only handles the request form).
    (puthash "window/workDoneProgress/create" #'ignore lsp--default-notification-handlers)

    (defun my/lsp-inspect-remote-clients ()
      "Inspect active LSP clients and their processes for the current remote buffer."
      (interactive)
      (when (file-remote-p (or (buffer-file-name) default-directory))
        (message "--- LSP Remote Client Inspection ---")
        (message "Buffer: %s" (buffer-name))
        (message "lsp-disabled-clients: %S" lsp-disabled-clients)
        (message "lsp-eslint-enable: %S" lsp-eslint-enable)
        (if (bound-and-true-p lsp-mode)
            (progn
              (message "Active Clients: %S" (mapcar #'lsp--client-server-id lsp--managed-clients))
              (dolist (client lsp--managed-clients)
                (let* ((server-id (lsp--client-server-id client))
                       (workspace (cl-find-if (lambda (w) (eq (lsp--workspace-client w) client)) lsp--buffer-workspaces))
                       (proc (and workspace (lsp--workspace-proc workspace))))
                  (message " Client: %s | Process: %s | Status: %s"
                           server-id
                           (or proc "None")
                           (if proc (process-status proc) "N/A")))))
          (message "LSP mode NOT active in this buffer"))
        (message "------------------------------------")))

    ;; Register a dedicated client for remote ESLint that relies on PATH
    ;; We use 'vscode-eslint-language-server' which is provided by `vscode-langservers-extracted`
    ;; Install it on remote: `npm install -g vscode-langservers-extracted`
    (lsp-register-client
     (make-lsp-client :new-connection (lsp-stdio-connection '("vscode-eslint-language-server" "--stdio"))
                      :activation-fn (lambda (file-name _mode)
                                       (let* ((filename (or file-name (buffer-file-name)))
                                              (is-match (and filename (string-match-p "\\.\\(js\\|ts\\|jsx\\|tsx\\|html\\|vue\\)\\'" filename))))
                                         ;; Only check for TRAMP if the extension already matched
                                         (and is-match (file-remote-p filename))))
                      :server-id 'eslint-remote
                      :priority 10
                      :add-on? t
                      :environment-fn (lambda ()
                                        '(("NODE_NO_WARNINGS" . "1")))))))
#+end_src


**** Using ~plists~

By default, ~lsp-mode~ uses hash tables to handle the serialization of JSON-RPC
messages from language servers. However, some language servers do not handle
this default implementation correctly and will fail, often attempting to
endlessly search for hash-table structures that aren't being parsed as expected.
Even when you enable =lsp-use-plists= in your standard configuration, the
package may still initialize or compile using its default hash-table
architecture. Setting the =LSP_USE_PLISTS= environment variable acts as a global
override, forcing ~lsp-mode~ to use property lists (~plists~) instead of the
default hash tables from the very start. The variable is needed when compiling
LSP to make sure it has the proper support, and later when running it.
Therefore, we do it in our =early-init.el= to ensure it takes effect before any
LSP code is loaded or compiled.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp :tangle early-init.el
;; Force lsp-mode to use plists instead of the default hash tables.
;; This is required for some language servers that fail when they encounter
;; hash tables. This must be set before lsp-mode loads or compiles to
;; ensure it does not default to the hash-table implementation.
(setenv "LSP_USE_PLISTS" "1")

(defun my/lsp-recompile-for-plists ()
  "Recompile lsp-mode with LSP_USE_PLISTS=1 so diagnostics work with plists.
Run once if you see wrong-type-argument hash-table-p when flycheck runs LSP diagnostics."
  (interactive)
  (setenv "LSP_USE_PLISTS" "1")
  (byte-recompile-directory (file-name-directory (locate-library "lsp-mode")) 0 t)
  (message "Recompiled. Restart Emacs for changes to take effect."))
#+end_src


**** Additional optimizations

In addition to the settings above, we also do some optimization on LSP for
simultanoeus projects. When you have multiple projects open simultaneously,
lsp-mode broadcasts ~workspace/didChangeWorkspaceFolders~ to EVERY running
server whenever a new root is added -- even if that server has no interest in
the language.

Opening a =Node.js= project while =jdtls= is running, for example, would add the
JS root to =jdtls=, causing it to crawl =node_modules=.

The ~my/lsp-server-file-extensions~ below is the single place to declare which
file extensions each server owns. Whenever you add a new language server to your
config, add a matching entry here. Servers with no entry are unrestricted (the
notification passes through unchanged).

The interceptor uses =ripgrep= (=rg=) for the filesystem scan; it falls back to
GNU =find= and logs a one-time hint to install =rg= if it is missing. =rg= is
much faster than =find=, especially on large projects and remote filesystems, so
it's highly recommended to install it for optimal performance.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(defvar my/lsp-server-file-extensions '()
  "Alist mapping LSP server IDs (symbols) to accepted file-extension lists.
Each entry has the form (SERVER-ID . (EXT1 EXT2 ...)) where extensions are
plain strings without a leading dot (e.g. \"java\", \"ts\").

Add a new entry here whenever you register a new language server.
Servers absent from this map are unrestricted: they receive every
workspace/didChangeWorkspaceFolders notification unchanged.")

(defvar my/lsp--rg-warned-p nil
  "Non-nil once the one-time ripgrep-missing warning has been emitted.")

(defun my/lsp--folder-has-extensions-p (folder extensions)
  "Return non-nil if FOLDER has any file matching EXTENSIONS.
EXTENSIONS is a list of extension strings without a leading dot.
Uses ripgrep (rg) when available; falls back to GNU find and emits a
one-time suggestion to install rg for faster workspace filtering."
  (when (and folder (file-directory-p folder) extensions)
    (let* ((dir (shell-quote-argument (expand-file-name folder)))
           (result
            (if (executable-find "rg")
                (shell-command-to-string
                 (concat "rg --files --max-depth 5 "
                         (mapconcat (lambda (e)
                                      (concat "-g " (shell-quote-argument
                                                     (concat "*." e))))
                                    extensions " ")
                         " " dir " 2>/dev/null | head -1"))
              (progn
                (unless my/lsp--rg-warned-p
                  (setq my/lsp--rg-warned-p t)
                  (message (concat "lsp-filter: ripgrep (rg) not found; "
                                   "using find for workspace filtering (slower). "
                                   "Install rg for faster performance.")))
                (shell-command-to-string
                 (format "find %s -maxdepth 5 \\( %s \\) -print -quit 2>/dev/null"
                         dir
                         (mapconcat (lambda (e)
                                      (concat "-name "
                                              (shell-quote-argument
                                               (concat "*." e))))
                                    extensions " -o ")))))))
      (not (string-empty-p (string-trim result))))))

(defun my/lsp--server-extensions (workspace)
  "Return the accepted extension list for WORKSPACE's server if restricted.
If unrestricted, returns nil."
  (let* ((client (lsp--workspace-client workspace))
         (server-id (and client (lsp--client-server-id client))))
    (alist-get server-id my/lsp-server-file-extensions)))

(defun my/lsp--filter-added-folders (added extensions)
  "Filter ADDED workspace-folder entries, keeping only those matching EXTENSIONS."
  (seq-filter
   (lambda (entry)
     (let ((uri (if (listp entry)
                    (plist-get entry :uri)
                  (gethash "uri" entry))))
       (my/lsp--folder-has-extensions-p (lsp--uri-to-path uri) extensions)))
   (if (listp added) added (append added nil))))

(defun my/lsp--rebuild-params (params filtered-added removed)
  "Rebuild a workspace-change PARAMS with FILTERED-ADDED and REMOVED."
  (if (listp params)
      (list :event (list :added filtered-added :removed (or removed [])))
    (let ((tbl (copy-hash-table params))
          (ev  (make-hash-table :test #'equal)))
      (puthash "added" filtered-added   ev)
      (puthash "removed" (or removed []) ev)
      (puthash "event" ev tbl)
      tbl)))

(defun my/lsp--filter-workspace-change-for-server (orig-fun workspace method params)
  "Filter workspace/didChangeWorkspaceFolders using our extensions list.
Servers absent from the map are unrestricted and receive the notification
unchanged.
- ORIG-FUN is `lsp--notify'
- WORKSPACE is the target workspace,
- METHOD is the JSON-RPC method string
- PARAMS are the notification params."
  (if (not (equal method "workspace/didChangeWorkspaceFolders"))
      (funcall orig-fun workspace method params)
    (let ((extensions (my/lsp--server-extensions workspace)))
      (if (null extensions)
          ;; Servers not in the map are unrestricted
          (funcall orig-fun workspace method params)
        (let* ((event (if (listp params)
                          (plist-get params :event)
                        (gethash "event" params)))
               (added (if (listp event)
                          (plist-get event :added)
                        (gethash "added" event)))
               (removed (if (listp event)
                            (plist-get event :removed)
                          (gethash "removed" event)))
               (filtered-added (my/lsp--filter-added-folders added extensions)))
          (when (or filtered-added (and removed (not (seq-empty-p removed))))
            (funcall orig-fun workspace method
                     (my/lsp--rebuild-params params filtered-added removed))))))))

(with-eval-after-load 'lsp-mode
  (advice-add 'lsp--notify :around #'my/lsp--filter-workspace-change-for-server))
#+end_src


**** Useful helpers

Below we add a useful helpers for handling the LSP workspace. See the functions'
documentations for details on these helpers.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(defun my/clean-lsp-workspace-cache ()
  "Delete ~/.emacs.d/workspace/ to force a fresh LSP reindex.
Use this when switching Java major versions or when jdtls shows stale errors.
After this runs, restart LSP (or Emacs) to trigger a full reindex."
  (interactive)
  (let ((workspace-dir (expand-file-name "workspace" user-emacs-directory)))
    (when (and (file-directory-p workspace-dir)
               (yes-or-no-p (format "Delete all files in %s? " workspace-dir)))
      (delete-directory workspace-dir t)
      (message "LSP workspace cache cleared. Restart Emacs or LSP for a fresh index."))))
#+end_src


*** lsp-ui: UI enhancements for LSP

~lsp-ui~ adds visual improvements to LSP, such as inline documentation popups, code actions
in the sideline, and better navigation.

The helper below, copied from https://github.com/themkat/.emacs.d/blob/master/init.org, toggles the documentation popup:

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
;; Silence byte-compiler warnings for lsp-ui doc popup helpers
(declare-function lsp-ui-doc-show "lsp-ui")
(declare-function lsp-ui-doc-hide "lsp-ui")
(declare-function lsp-ui-doc--visible-p "lsp-ui")

(defun lsp-ui-show-doc-helper ()
  "Toggle the visibility of the lsp-ui documentation popup."
  (interactive)
  (if (lsp-ui-doc--visible-p)
      (lsp-ui-doc-hide)
    (lsp-ui-doc-show)))
#+end_src

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package lsp-ui
  :pin "MELPA Unstable"
  :after lsp-mode

  ;; :commands ensures lsp-ui is loaded only when lsp-ui-mode is called
  :commands lsp-ui-mode

  :custom
  (lsp-ui-doc-enable t)
  (lsp-ui-doc-position 'at-point)
  (lsp-ui-sideline-enable t)
  (lsp-ui-sideline-show-hover t)
  (lsp-ui-peek-always-show t)
  (lsp-ui-sideline-show-code-actions t)
  :bind
  (:map lsp-mode-map ("M-s M-d" . lsp-ui-show-doc-helper)))
#+end_src


*** lsp-treemacs: Project and symbol tree views

~lsp-treemacs~ integrates Treemacs with LSP, providing a sidebar for project symbols,
errors, references, and more.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package lsp-treemacs
  :pin "MELPA Unstable"
  :after (lsp-mode treemacs)
  :config
  (setq lsp-treemacs-sync-mode nil) ;; Keep off by default
  (add-hook 'treemacs-mode-hook (lambda () (lsp-treemacs-sync-mode 1)))) ;; Enable only when Treemacs is open
