*** ~dap-mode~: Debug Adapter Protocol (debugging)

~dap-mode~ adds debugging support to Emacs using the Debug Adapter Protocol. It lets
you set breakpoints, step through code, and inspect variables for many languages.


**** Basic configuration

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(defvar-local my/dap-check-requirements #'always
  "Buffer-local predicate called before starting a new DAP session.
Must be a function of no arguments. Override in a mode hook with
`setq-local' to session to start. Override in a mode hook with
`setq-local' to add language-specific pre-flight checks (e.g.
verifying that the language server is running). Defaults to `always',
which always returns t.")

(defvar-local my/dap-start-debug
    (lambda ()
      (message
       (concat "No debug launcher defined for this buffer. "
               "Add (setq-local my/dap-start-debug <fn>) "
               "in the appropriate mode hook.")))
  "Buffer-local function called to start a new DAP debug session.
Must be a function of no arguments. Override in a mode hook with
`setq-local' to provide a language-specific launcher (e.g. calling
`dap-debug' with a launch.json configuration or a built-in template).
The default implementation logs an actionable hint.")

(use-package dap-mode
  :after lsp-mode
  :init
  (dap-auto-configure-mode 1)
  :bind
  (("<f5>" . my/dap-run-continue-dispatch)
   ("<f6>" . dap-next)
   ("<f7>" . dap-step-in)
   ("<f8>" . dap-step-out)
   ("<f9>" . dap-breakpoint-toggle))
  :config
  ;; Fix for newline translation issues on Linux/WSL file-watching.
  ;; See https://github.com/emacs-lsp/dap-mode/issues/214
  (setq inhibit-eol-conversion t)

  (setq dap-auto-configure-features
        '(sessions locals breakpoints expressions controls tooltip repl))

  ;; Force the output terminal to pop up on debug launch.
  (setq dap-auto-show-output t)

  ;; Log DAP protocol JSON to *Messages* to debug communication issues.
  (setq dap-print-io t)

  (declare-function dap--cur-session "dap-mode")
  (declare-function dap--session-running "dap-mode")
  (defun my/dap-run-continue-dispatch ()
    "Smart F5: continue an active session or start a new debug session.
If a DAP session is active and running, calls `dap-continue'.
Otherwise, calls `my/dap-check-requirements' (buffer-local).
If it returns nil, logs a message and stops.
On success, calls `my/dap-start-debug' (buffer-local)."
    (interactive)
    (let ((session (and (fboundp 'dap--cur-session) (dap--cur-session))))
      (if (and session (dap--session-running session))
          (call-interactively 'dap-continue)
        (if (funcall my/dap-check-requirements)
            (funcall my/dap-start-debug)
          (message "DAP pre-flight check failed; not starting debug session.")))))

  ;; ansi-color-compilation-filter (Emacs 28+) correctly uses the
  ;; compilation-filter-start marker to apply colors only to new output.
  (add-hook 'dap-server-log-mode-hook
            (lambda ()
              (add-hook 'compilation-filter-hook
                        #'ansi-color-compilation-filter nil t)))

  (declare-function lsp-treemacs-render "lsp-treemacs")
  (declare-function dap-ui-locals-get-data "dap-ui-locals")
  (declare-function my/dap-ui-repl-smart-context "init")
  (declare-function my/dap-ui-locals--refresh-stable "init")
  (declare-function my/dap-ui-no-treemacs-refresh "init")
  (defvar dap-ui--locals-buffer nil)
  (defvar dap-ui--locals-timer nil)
  (defvar dap-ui-locals-expand-depth nil)

  ;; REPL optimization: Smart Context Evaluation Loop
  ;;
  ;; WHY THIS IS NEEDED: By default, the DAP REPL sends all input with "context: repl".
  ;; For C++, many GDB adapters interpret this as an Instruction to run a raw GDB/MI
  ;; command, which returns ugly JSON and can't handle simple math like "a + 1".
  ;;
  ;; THE FIX: This advice detects if your input is a command (starts with "-") or
  ;; an expression. For expressions, it uses "context: hover".
  ;;
  ;; BENEFITS:
  ;; 1. Complex Objects: You can inspect structs, classes, and nested data easily.
  ;; 2. Dot/Arrow Syntax: Typing "my_obj.field" or "ptr->val" will just work.
  ;; 3. Clean Output: Instead of JSON tokens, you get the literal value (e.g., "42").
  (defun my/dap-ui-repl-smart-context (orig-fun session input)
    "Make the DAP REPL use `hover' context for expressions, and `repl' for commands."
    (let ((context (if (string-prefix-p "-" (string-trim input)) "repl" "hover")))
      (cl-letf* ((old-dap-make-request (symbol-function 'dap--make-request))
                 ((symbol-function 'dap--make-request)
                  (lambda (command arguments)
                    (if (string= command "evaluate")
                        (funcall old-dap-make-request command (plist-put arguments :context context))
                      (funcall old-dap-make-request command arguments)))))
        (funcall orig-fun session input))))

  (advice-add 'dap-ui-input-sender :around #'my/dap-ui-repl-smart-context)

  ;; UI Stability and Freeze Protection
  ;;
  ;; THE PROBLEM: By default, `dap-ui` schedules a refresh every 0.2s using a
  ;; "Smart Sync" (lsp-treemacs-generic-update). This sync logic tries to diff
  ;; the old and new variable trees, which is extremely CPU-heavy and freezes
  ;; the Emacs UI for seconds or more.
  ;;
  ;; THE FIX: This override changes the refresh strategy:
  ;; 1. Stability: Increases the idle timer from 0.2s to 0.5s.
  ;; 2. Speed: Uses 'render' (Full Redraw) instead of 'generic-update' (Smart Sync).
  ;;    A full redraw is much faster because it avoids the tree-diffing logic.
  (declare-function lsp-treemacs-wcb-unless-killed "lsp-treemacs")
  (declare-function lsp-treemacs-render "lsp-treemacs")
  (declare-function dap-ui-locals-get-data "dap-ui")
  (declare-function ht-values "ht")
  (declare-function dap--get-breakpoints "dap-mode")
  (declare-function my/dap--buffers-w-breakpoints-safe nil)

  (defun my/dap-ui-locals--refresh-stable (&rest _)
    "Locals refresh with 0.5s idle timer and FAST rendering.
Avoids the expensive tree-diffing of lsp-treemacs-generic-update."
    (lsp-treemacs-wcb-unless-killed dap-ui--locals-buffer
      (when (and (fboundp 'dap-ui--locals-timer) dap-ui--locals-timer)
        (cancel-timer dap-ui--locals-timer))
      ;; We use the fast 'render' instead of the slow 'generic-update'
      (setq-local dap-ui--locals-timer
                  (run-with-idle-timer 0.5 nil
                                       (lambda ()
                                         (lsp-treemacs-render (dap-ui-locals-get-data)
                                                              " Locals "
                                                              dap-ui-locals-expand-depth
                                                              dap-ui--locals-buffer))))))

  (advice-add 'dap-ui-locals--refresh-schedule :override #'my/dap-ui-locals--refresh-stable)

  ;; The following set of configs are needed to prevent Emacs UI freezes
  ;; or running expensive operations when dap-mode is actively debugging.

  ;; Below we skip Treemacs-based UI for DAP to prevent freezes.
  ;;
  ;; SCENARIO: Even in Graphical Emacs, "Terminal" issues can occur because
  ;; Emacs communicates with GDB/DAP via hidden pseudo-terminals (ptys).
  ;; DAP-UI's aggressive 0.2s idle timers trigger `lsp-treemacs-generic-update`,
  ;; creating a "Redraw Storm."
  ;;
  ;; THE PROBLEM: Emacs is single-threaded. It spends so much CPU calculating
  ;; complex Treemacs tree nodes (the "UI logic") that it becomes "deaf" to
  ;; your keyboard/mouse input, effectively freezing the entire Frame.
  ;;
  ;; INVESTIGATION: If Emacs locks up, use `M-x toggle-debug-on-quit`.
  ;; Pressing `C-g` during the freeze will interrupt the loop and show the
  ;; "Timer Thief" (usually `dap-ui-locals--refresh` or `treemacs-update-node`).
  ;;
  ;; THE FIX: We advise the refresh functions to "mute" the Treemacs-specific
  ;; update logic. This ensures the UI stays responsive during debug sessions.
  (defun my/dap-ui-no-treemacs-refresh (orig-fun &rest args)
    "Inhibit the slow tree-sync logic and just do nothing (for secondary windows)."
    (cl-letf (((symbol-function 'lsp-treemacs-generic-update) (lambda (&rest _) nil)))
      (apply orig-fun args)))

  (advice-add 'dap-ui-breakpoints--refresh :around #'my/dap-ui-no-treemacs-refresh)

  ;; Fix for: dap--buffers-w-breakpoints: Wrong type argument: markerp, nil
  ;;
  ;; dap--buffers-w-breakpoints crashes when a persisted breakpoint has a nil
  ;; :marker (e.g., the breakpoint was saved to disk but its file buffer hasn't
  ;; been visited yet in this session). The original code unconditionally calls
  ;; (marker-buffer (plist-get ... :marker)) which fails on nil.
  (defun my/dap--buffers-w-breakpoints-safe ()
    "Safe version of `dap--buffers-w-breakpoints' that skips nil markers."
    (delq nil
          (--map (-some-> (plist-get (car it) :marker) marker-buffer)
                 (ht-values (dap--get-breakpoints)))))

  (advice-add 'dap--buffers-w-breakpoints :override #'my/dap--buffers-w-breakpoints-safe)

  ;; Kill the JVM process when a compile_attach session ends or fails.
  ;; Without this the JVM keeps running (suspended on JDWP server=y),
  ;; holding its debug port, so subsequent sessions fail with
  ;; "Address already in use".
  (defun my/dap-kill-program-proc (session)
    "Kill the external program process attached to SESSION, if any.
Called from `dap-terminated-hook' to clean up compile_attach JVMs.
Uses pkill -P to kill the JVM child of the sh wrapper process,
since compilation-start wraps commands in `sh -c', making the JVM
a grandchild of Emacs that plain kill-process will not reach.
SESSION is the `dap--debug-session' struct that just terminated."
    (when-let (proc (dap--debug-session-program-proc session))
      (let ((pid (process-id proc)))
        (when pid
          (message "DAP: killing stale JVM process tree under sh pid %d" pid)
          ;; Kill JVM children of the sh wrapper.
          (ignore-errors
            (call-process "pkill" nil nil nil "-KILL" "-P" (number-to-string pid)))
          ;; Kill sh itself.
          (ignore-errors
            (call-process "kill" nil nil nil "-9" (number-to-string pid)))))
      (ignore-errors (kill-process proc))))

  (add-hook 'dap-terminated-hook #'my/dap-kill-program-proc)

  ;; A few other improvements that might be useful in the future. For now I've
  ;; disabled them because they are not freezing Emacs, but they seem to work.
  ;;
  ;; (with-eval-after-load 'lsp-mode
  ;;     (defun my/lsp-deferred-guard (orig-fun &rest args)
  ;;       "Skip `lsp-deferred' if a DAP session is currently active.
  ;; This prevents the 'startup storm' of timers when DAP opens multiple windows."
  ;;       (if (and (fboundp 'dap--cur-session) (dap--cur-session))
  ;;           (message "[DEBUG] Skipping lsp-deferred: DAP session is active.")
  ;;         (apply orig-fun args)))
  ;;
  ;;     (advice-add 'lsp-deferred :around #'my/lsp-deferred-guard))
  ;;
  ;;   (defun my/dap-debug-quiet (orig-fun &rest args)
  ;;     "Run `dap-debug' with lsp-autoconfigure and window hooks inhibited.
  ;; It inhibit expensive hooks while DAP is spinning up windows."
  ;;     (let ((lsp-auto-configure nil)
  ;;           ;; Prevent lsp-mode from reacting to each new debugger window
  ;;           (window-configuration-change-hook nil))
  ;;       (apply orig-fun args)))
  ;;
  ;;   (advice-add 'dap-debug :around #'my/dap-debug-quiet)
  ;; (defun my/lsp-ui-mode-guard (orig-fun &rest args)
  ;;   "Inhibit `lsp-ui-mode' and its components if a DAP session is active."
  ;;   (if (and (fboundp 'dap--cur-session) (dap--cur-session))
  ;;       (message "[DEBUG] Inhibiting lsp-ui-mode: DAP session is active.")
  ;;     (apply orig-fun args)))
  ;;
  ;; (advice-add 'lsp-ui-mode :around #'my/lsp-ui-mode-guard)
  ;; (advice-add 'lsp-ui-doc-mode :around #'my/lsp-ui-mode-guard)
  )
#+end_src


**** Extending DAP controls

The default DAP controls posframe has no dedicated "Force Stop" button --
~dap-disconnect~ uses the graceful DAP ~Disconnect~ request which the debug
adapter may ignore, and leaves the Spring Boot JVM running. We override
~dap-ui--update-controls~ to inject our own stop button that calls
~dap-force-quit~, which sends =SIGKILL= to the adapter process and the JVM
process group. The icon already ships with ~dap-mode~ (=icons/vscode/stop.png=).

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
;; Function created without "my/" prefix to facilitate running it
;; interactively via M-x.
(defun dap-force-quit ()
  "Forcefully terminate all DAP sessions and their JVM processes.
Sends SIGKILL to each session's adapter process and program process
(the JVM started via compile_attach), then clears all dap-mode state.
Safe to call even when the session is completely unresponsive."
  (interactive)
  (ignore-errors
    (dolist (session (dap--get-sessions))
      ;; Kill the DAP adapter process (the debug adapter subprocess).
      (when-let (proc (dap--debug-session-proc session))
        (when (process-live-p proc)
          (kill-process proc)))
      ;; Kill the JVM process started via compile_attach.
      ;;
      ;; Why plain kill-process doesn't work:
      ;; compilation-start runs the command as `sh -c "<java cmd>"', so
      ;; prog-proc is actually the *shell* wrapper, not the JVM.
      ;; kill-process kills only sh; the JVM child survives as an orphan.
      ;; signal-process with -(pid) also fails because the JVM is NOT a
      ;; process group leader -- it keeps its own PGID.
      ;;
      ;; Fix: use pkill -KILL -P <sh-pid> to target sh's children
      ;; (the JVM and anything it spawned), then kill sh itself.
      (when-let (prog-proc (dap--debug-session-program-proc session))
        (let ((pid (process-id prog-proc)))
          (when pid
            ;; Kill the JVM child processes (children of the sh wrapper).
            (ignore-errors
              (call-process "pkill" nil nil nil "-KILL" "-P" (number-to-string pid)))
            ;; Kill sh itself.
            (ignore-errors
              (call-process "kill" nil nil nil "-9" (number-to-string pid)))))
        (ignore-errors (kill-process prog-proc)))))
  (ignore-errors (dap--set-sessions nil))
  (ignore-errors (lsp-workspace-set-metadata "default-session" nil))
  (ignore-errors (dap-ui-hide-many-windows))
  (defvar dap-ui--control-buffer)
  (ignore-errors (posframe-hide dap-ui--control-buffer))
  (message "DAP session and processes nuked."))

;; Replace the non-functional Restart button with Force Stop.
;;
;; We use :around + cl-letf to intercept only the call to
;; `dap-ui--create-command' for "restart.png" and substitute
;; "stop.png" -> `dap-force-quit'. All other buttons are untouched,
;; so we do NOT need to redefine any of the existing icons.
;;
;; Why not Restart? dap-debug-restart opens an empty comint buffer and
;; does nothing useful for compile_attach sessions because the JVM has
;; already been killed -- there is nothing left to re-attach to.
(defun my/dap-ui--inject-stop-button (orig-fun &rest args)
  "Around advice for `dap-ui--update-controls'.
Replaces the non-functional Restart button with a Force Stop button
that calls `dap-force-quit' to SIGKILL the JVM process group."
  (cl-letf* ((orig-create (symbol-function 'dap-ui--create-command))
              ((symbol-function 'dap-ui--create-command)
               (lambda (image command hover-text)
                 (if (string= image "restart.png")
                     (funcall orig-create "stop.png" #'dap-force-quit "Force Stop (kills JVM)")
                   (funcall orig-create image command hover-text)))))
    (apply orig-fun args)))

(with-eval-after-load 'dap-ui
  (advice-add 'dap-ui--update-controls :around #'my/dap-ui--inject-stop-button))
#+end_src


**** Easy toggle for breakpoints

We also add a convenient way to toggle breakpoints by clicking the left fringe.
This works in any buffer where ~dap-mode~ is active, allowing you to quickly set
or remove breakpoints without needing to use keyboard shortcuts or commands.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(with-eval-after-load 'dap-mode
  (defun my/dap-fringe-click-toggle-breakpoint (event)
    "Toggle a DAP breakpoint at the line clicked in the left fringe.
EVENT is the mouse click event."
    (interactive "e")
    (when (and (bound-and-true-p dap-mode)
               (derived-mode-p 'prog-mode))
      (let ((pos (posn-point (event-start event))))
        (when pos
          (save-excursion
            (goto-char pos)
            (dap-breakpoint-toggle))))))

  (add-hook 'prog-mode-hook
            (lambda ()
              (local-set-key [left-fringe mouse-1] #'my/dap-fringe-click-toggle-breakpoint)))

  (with-eval-after-load 'treesit-fold-indicators
    (defun my/dap-treesit-fold-fringe-click-advice (orig-fn event)
      "Handle [left-fringe mouse-1] in *-ts-mode for breakpoints and folds.
By default, clicking the fringe with no fold indicator does nothing (event is
swallowed). This advice ensures the click toggles a DAP breakpoint if no fold
indicator is present, so fringe clicks are always useful. Call ORIG-FN if a
fold indicator is present at the clicked line; otherwise, toggle a DAP
breakpoint if `dap-mode' is active in the current buffer."
      (let* ((pos (posn-point (event-start event)))
             (cur-ln (when pos (line-number-at-pos pos)))
             (ovs (when pos
                    (append (treesit-fold--overlays-in
                             'type 'treesit-fold-indicators-fr-plus)
                            (treesit-fold--overlays-in
                             'type 'treesit-fold-indicators-fr-minus-tail))))
             (has-indicator
              (and ovs cur-ln
                   (cl-some
                    (lambda (ov)
                      (= cur-ln (line-number-at-pos (overlay-start ov))))
                    ovs))))
        (if has-indicator
            ;; Fold-indicator line: if a DAP breakpoint is already there,
            ;; remove it (otherwise it would not be possible to remove
            ;; an orphaned breakpoint stuck on a fold-indicator line by
            ;; clicking on the fringe).
            (let ((bp-on-line
                   (and (bound-and-true-p dap-mode)
                        pos
                        (save-excursion
                          (goto-char pos)
                          (dap--get-breakpoint-at-point)))))
              (if bp-on-line
                  (my/dap-fringe-click-toggle-breakpoint event)
                (funcall orig-fn event)))
          (when (and (bound-and-true-p dap-mode)
                     (derived-mode-p 'prog-mode))
            (my/dap-fringe-click-toggle-breakpoint event)))))
    (advice-add 'treesit-fold-indicators-click-fringe
                :around #'my/dap-treesit-fold-fringe-click-advice)))
#+end_src



** Syntax checking with Flycheck

Flycheck is a modern on-the-fly syntax checking extension for Emacs. It runs
linters and compilers in the background as you edit, highlighting errors and
warnings directly in your code buffer. This helps you catch mistakes early by
providing immediate feedback about code issues (like syntax errors, unused
variables, or style problems).

By default, Flycheck limits the number of errors it will display in a buffer.
Some language servers (such as Lean or large LSP projects) can produce many
diagnostics, which may exceed this limit and cause Flycheck to stop reporting
errors. To avoid missing important diagnostics, we set the error threshold to a
reasonably high value (9999), so Flycheck continues to show all errors without
being excessive.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(defun my/flycheck-maybe-enable ()
  "Enable flycheck-mode only if the file is local (not a TRAMP file).
This is important because Flycheck does not support TRAMP yet."
  (when (and (buffer-file-name)
             (not (tramp-tramp-file-p (buffer-file-name))))
    (flycheck-mode 1)))

(use-package flycheck
  :custom
  (flycheck-indication-mode nil)
  (flycheck-highlighting-mode 'lines)
  ;; Raise the error threshold to avoid missing diagnostics from language servers.
  (flycheck-checker-error-threshold 9999)

  ;; Enable Flycheck globally. Disabled for now. I am trying prog-mode hook instead.
  ;; (global-flycheck-mode t)
  :hook (prog-mode . my/flycheck-maybe-enable))
#+end_src


** Code Formatting

*** Automatic code formatting with Apheleia

Apheleia is a lightweight Emacs package for automatic code formatting. It runs
external formatters (like Prettier, Black, shfmt, etc.) and replaces buffer text
with the formatted result asynchronously, so Emacs stays responsive. You can map
modes to formatters, and Apheleia will format files automatically on save or on
demand. This helps keep your codebase consistent and clean with minimal effort.

For Org mode source blocks, we enable =org-src-tab-acts-natively= so that
TAB indents according to the source block's language mode.

**** Usage

*Manual buffer formatting:*

Press =C-c f= (in any mode) to manually format the current buffer with Apheleia.

*Org source blocks:*

With =org-src-tab-acts-natively= enabled:
- TAB key works natively within source blocks (respects the language's indentation)
- Use =C-c '= to open source blocks in their native major mode (Apheleia will format on save there)
- Use =C-c f= to manually format the current buffer if needed

*Supported languages and formatters:*

The following languages are configured with their respective formatters:

- **Emacs Lisp** (~emacs-lisp-mode~, ~lisp-interaction-mode~) → Built-in ~lisp-indent~
- **JavaScript** (~js-mode~, ~js-ts-mode~) → Prettier (local installation)
- **TypeScript** (~typescript-mode~, ~typescript-ts-mode~, ~tsx-ts-mode~) → Prettier (local installation)
- **Python** (~python-mode~, ~python-ts-mode~) → Prettier with =@prettier/plugin-python=
- **JSON** (~json-mode~, ~json-ts-mode~) → Prettier (local installation)
- **Web/HTML** (~web-mode~) → Prettier (local installation)

Note: Prettier is installed locally in =~/.emacs.d/nodejs_tools/= with the Python plugin.

*Adding new formatters:*

To add a new formatter, add it to =apheleia-formatters= and map it to the
appropriate mode in =apheleia-mode-alist=. For example:

#+begin_example
;; Add a new formatter
(setf (alist-get 'my-formatter apheleia-formatters)
      '("my-formatter-command" "--option" "value" file))

;; Map it to a mode
(setf (alist-get 'my-mode apheleia-mode-alist) 'my-formatter)
#+end_example


**** Basic configuration for Apheleia

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package apheleia
  :pin "MELPA Unstable"
  :config
  ;; Enable globally - formats on save
  (apheleia-global-mode +1)

  ;; Smarter TRAMP support: Only inhibit if the required formatter is missing on the remote.
  ;; This prevents hangs while allowing formatting if the remote has the tool installed.
  (defun my/apheleia-inhibit-remote-if-no-tool ()
    (when (file-remote-p (or (buffer-file-name) default-directory))
      (let* ((formatter (alist-get major-mode apheleia-mode-alist))
             (cmd (if (listp (alist-get formatter apheleia-formatters))
                      (car (alist-get formatter apheleia-formatters))
                    (alist-get formatter apheleia-formatters))))
        (not (executable-find (or (and (stringp cmd) cmd) "") t)))))

  (add-to-list 'apheleia-inhibit-functions #'my/apheleia-inhibit-remote-if-no-tool)

  ;; Add custom formatters - you can extend this list
  (setf (alist-get 'prettier-local apheleia-formatters)
        `(,(expand-file-name "~/.emacs.d/nodejs_tools/bin/prettier.cjs")
          "--stdin-filepath" filepath
          "--plugin" ,(expand-file-name "~/.emacs.d/nodejs_tools/node_modules/@prettier/plugin-python")))

  (setf (alist-get 'emacs-lisp apheleia-formatters)
        '("emacs" "--batch" "-l" "pp"
          "--eval" "(princ (pp-to-string (read)))"
          file))

  ;; Map modes to formatters
  (setf (alist-get 'emacs-lisp-mode apheleia-mode-alist) 'lisp-indent)
  (setf (alist-get 'lisp-interaction-mode apheleia-mode-alist) 'lisp-indent)
  (setf (alist-get 'js-mode apheleia-mode-alist) 'prettier-local)
  (setf (alist-get 'js-ts-mode apheleia-mode-alist) 'prettier-local)
  (setf (alist-get 'typescript-mode apheleia-mode-alist) 'prettier-local)
  (setf (alist-get 'typescript-ts-mode apheleia-mode-alist) 'prettier-local)
  (setf (alist-get 'tsx-ts-mode apheleia-mode-alist) 'prettier-local)
  (setf (alist-get 'json-mode apheleia-mode-alist) 'prettier-local)
  (setf (alist-get 'json-ts-mode apheleia-mode-alist) 'prettier-local)
  (setf (alist-get 'web-mode apheleia-mode-alist) 'prettier-local)
  (setf (alist-get 'python-mode apheleia-mode-alist) 'prettier-local)
  (setf (alist-get 'python-ts-mode apheleia-mode-alist) 'prettier-local))

;; Global keybinding for formatting
(global-set-key (kbd "C-c f") 'apheleia-format-buffer)
#+end_src


*** Prettier setup:

Prettier is a popular opinionated code formatter for JavaScript, TypeScript,
JSON, HTML, CSS, and many other languages. It enforces a consistent style by
parsing your code and reprinting it with its own rules. This helps keep code
clean and readable across teams and editors.

In this config, Prettier is used in two ways:

- Via Apheleia for on-save formatting in many modes (see above)
- Via the ~prettier-js~ package for on-the-fly formatting in JS/TS buffers

The ~prettier-js~ package runs Prettier as you type or on save, depending on
your settings. It is especially useful for JavaScript and TypeScript projects.

#+begin_src sh :tangle setup.sh
is_installed_prettier() {
  [ -f "$TOOLS_DIR/node_modules/.bin/typescript-language-server" ] || command -v typescript-language-server >/dev/null 2>&1
}

install_prettier() {
  install_nodejs_tools
}

uninstall_prettier() {
  uninstall_nodejs_tools
}

register_action "Install Prettier" "prettier" "node"
#+end_src

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package prettier-js
  :hook ((js-ts-mode . prettier-js-mode)
         (typescript-ts-mode . prettier-js-mode)
         (tsx-ts-mode . prettier-js-mode)
         (json-ts-mode . prettier-js-mode)
         ;; (web-mode . prettier-js-mode)
         ;; (python-ts-mode . prettier-js-mode)
         ;; (markdown-mode . prettier-js-mode)
         ;; (css-mode . prettier-js-mode)
         ;; (scss-mode . prettier-js-mode)
         ;; (html-mode . prettier-js-mode)
         )
  :config
  ;; Use the prettier executable found in our PATH
  (setq prettier-js-command (executable-find "prettier")))

Enable native TAB behavior in Org source blocks so that TAB indents according

** Code metrics

The ~cognitive-complexity~ package provides real-time code complexity and metrics
overlays in supported programming modes. It can show cyclomatic complexity,
maintainability index, and other metrics inline, helping you spot complex or
hard-to-maintain code as you work. It is a port of the known ~codemetrics~ package,
but that works with treesit (native on Emacs 29+) instead of tree-sitter (external
package).

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package cognitive-complexity
  :pin "JCS-ELPA"
  :hook ((prog-mode . cognitive-complexity-mode))
  :custom
  (cognitive-complexity-complexity-threshold 15)
  (cognitive-complexity-maintainability-threshold 50)
  :config
  ;; Fix: cognitive-complexity--delete-ovs never resets the overlay list to nil.
  ;; See https://github.com/emacs-vs/cognitive-complexity/issues/4
  (advice-add 'cognitive-complexity--enable :before
              (lambda ()
                (remove-overlays (point-min) (point-max) 'cognitive-complexity t)
                (setq cognitive-complexity--ovs nil))))
#+end_src


** Programming, languages and modes

*** General setup with treesit

Treesit is a modern incremental parsing library that provides fast,
accurate syntax trees for programming languages. Originally known
as ~tree-sitter~, it was included into Emacs 29+ as a built-in
functonality and known as ~treesit~, which provides ~*-ts-mode~
modes with excellent syntax highlighting and structural editing.

Remember to upgrade the versions when possible. New versions might
not be compatible with your current Emacs build.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package treesit-auto
  :pin "MELPA Unstable"
  :config
  (global-treesit-auto-mode)
  (treesit-auto-add-to-auto-mode-alist 'all)

  ;; Preferred grammar sources
  (add-to-list 'treesit-language-source-alist
               '(xml "https://github.com/tree-sitter-grammars/tree-sitter-xml" "master" "xml/src"))
  (add-to-list 'treesit-language-source-alist
               '(yaml "https://github.com/ikatyang/tree-sitter-yaml"))
  (add-to-list 'treesit-language-source-alist
               '(typescript "https://github.com/tree-sitter/tree-sitter-typescript" "v0.23.2" "typescript/src"))
  (add-to-list 'treesit-language-source-alist
               '(tsx "https://github.com/tree-sitter/tree-sitter-typescript" "v0.23.2" "tsx/src"))
  (add-to-list 'treesit-language-source-alist
               '(javascript "https://github.com/tree-sitter/tree-sitter-javascript" "v0.23.1" "src"))
  (add-to-list 'treesit-language-source-alist
               '(python "https://github.com/tree-sitter/tree-sitter-python" "v0.23.6" "src"))
  (add-to-list 'treesit-language-source-alist
               '(java "https://github.com/tree-sitter/tree-sitter-java" "v0.23.5" "src"))
  (add-to-list 'treesit-language-source-alist
               '(bash "https://github.com/tree-sitter/tree-sitter-bash"))
  (add-to-list 'treesit-language-source-alist
               '(json "https://github.com/tree-sitter/tree-sitter-json"))
  (add-to-list 'treesit-language-source-alist
               '(c "https://github.com/tree-sitter/tree-sitter-c"))
  (add-to-list 'treesit-language-source-alist
               '(cpp "https://github.com/tree-sitter/tree-sitter-cpp")))
#+end_src

~treesit-fold~ is a syntax-aware folding engine built for the Treesit system
in Emacs 29+. Unlike traditional folding that relies on indentation "guessing,"
it uses a concrete parser to identify the precise boundaries of YAML blocks,
maps, and lists. This allows for lightning-fast, reliable collapsing of massive
datasets, often enhanced by visual indicators in the margin that let you
navigate complex hierarchies with the same ease as an Org-mode document.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package treesit-fold
  :pin "JCS-ELPA"
  :config
  ;; Enable indicators globally
  (global-treesit-fold-indicators-mode 1)

  ;; Enable folding for all programming modes
  (add-hook 'prog-mode-hook #'treesit-fold-mode)

  (defun my/treesit-fold-toggle-all ()
    "Toggle all folds in the current buffer."
    (interactive)
    (if (bound-and-true-p my/treesit-folded-p)
        (progn (treesit-fold-open-all) (setq-local my/treesit-folded-p nil))
      (progn (treesit-fold-close-all) (setq-local my/treesit-folded-p t))))

  ;; Global Keybindings (using 'C-c f' as a mnemonic for Fold)
  (keymap-global-set "C-<tab>" #'treesit-fold-toggle)
  (keymap-global-set "C-c v f" #'treesit-fold-toggle)
  (keymap-global-set "C-c v F" #'my/treesit-fold-toggle-all))
#+end_src

And the Emacs setup:

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(defvar my/cpp-format-style 'bsd
  "The formatting style for C and C++ files.
Supported values:
- \='gnu        : GNU Style (brackets indented, next line)
- \='bsd        : BSD/Allman Style (brackets not indented, next line)
- \='k&r        : K&R Style (brackets on same line)
- \='stroustrup : Stroustrup Style (K&R variant)
- \='linux      : Linux Kernel Style
- \='google     : Google C++ Style
- \='llvm       : LLVM Default Style
- \='microsoft  : Microsoft Style
- \='webkit     : WebKit Style
- \='mozilla    : Mozilla Style
- \='chromium   : Chromium Style
- \='whitesmith : Whitesmith Style (indented braces)

You can override this in local.el by setting it before init.org is loaded
or using (setq my/cpp-format-style \='bsd) there.")

(defun my/map-cpp-base-indent-style-for-emacs ()
  "Map user-selected style to an Emacs-supported base style.
Emacs built-in modes (`cc-mode' and `c-ts-mode') only understand a
limited set of historical base styles (like \='gnu, \='bsd, \='k&r,
\='linux). By grouping modern styles into the closest base
style, we ensure the editor has a rough fallback to guess where
to place the cursor while typing interactively."
  (pcase my/cpp-format-style
    ((or 'mozilla 'webkit 'microsoft) 'bsd)
    ((or 'google 'llvm 'chromium) 'gnu)
    (style style)))

;; Common indentation settings (fallback styles for interactive typing)
;; These ensure both classic cc-mode and tree-sitter modes use the correct model.
(defvar c-ts-mode-indent-style nil)
(setq c-default-style (symbol-name (my/map-cpp-base-indent-style-for-emacs)))
(setq c-ts-mode-indent-style (my/map-cpp-base-indent-style-for-emacs))
(setq-default c-basic-offset 2)
(use-package cmake-ts-mode
  :mode ("CMakeLists\\.txt\\'" "\\.cmake\\'")
  :config
  (setq cmake-ts-mode-indent-offset 2))

(with-eval-after-load 'lsp-mode
  (defvar lsp-clients-clangd-args nil)
  (setq lsp-clients-clangd-args
        `("--header-insertion=never"
          "--background-index"
          "--log=error"
          ,(format "--fallback-style=%s"
                   (pcase my/cpp-format-style
                     ('bsd "BSD")
                     ('google "Google")
                     ('llvm "LLVM")
                     (_ "LLVM"))))))

;; Register accepted extensions for workspace folder filtering
(add-to-list 'my/lsp-server-file-extensions '(clangd . ("c" "cpp" "h" "hpp" "cc" "cxx" "cu")))

(defun my/apheleia-get-cpp-style ()
  "Determine the best -style argument for clang-format.
If a local .clang-format exists, use -style=file.
Otherwise, use our custom configuration string."
  (let ((config-file (locate-dominating-file (or (buffer-file-name) default-directory)
                                             ".clang-format")))
    (if config-file
        "-style=file"
      (format "-style=%s"
              (pcase my/cpp-format-style
                ('bsd         "{BasedOnStyle: LLVM, BreakBeforeBraces: Allman, IndentWidth: 2}")
                ('k&r         "{BasedOnStyle: K&R, BreakBeforeBraces: Attach, IndentWidth: 2}")
                ('stroustrup  "{BasedOnStyle: Stroustrup, IndentWidth: 2}")
                ('linux       "{BasedOnStyle: Linux, IndentWidth: 2}")
                ('google      "{BasedOnStyle: Google, IndentWidth: 2}")
                ('llvm        "{BasedOnStyle: LLVM, IndentWidth: 2}")
                ('microsoft   "{BasedOnStyle: Microsoft, IndentWidth: 2}")
                ('mozilla     "{BasedOnStyle: Mozilla, IndentWidth: 2}")
                ('chromium    "{BasedOnStyle: Chromium, IndentWidth: 2}")
                ('webkit      "{BasedOnStyle: WebKit, IndentWidth: 2}")
                ('whitesmith  "{BasedOnStyle: Whitesmith, IndentWidth: 2}")
                (_            "{BasedOnStyle: LLVM, BreakBeforeBraces: Allman, IndentWidth: 2}"))))))

(defvar apheleia-formatters nil)
(defvar apheleia-mode-alist nil)

;; Apheleia is enabled for C and C++ via the clang-format-cpp formatter:
(with-eval-after-load 'apheleia
  (setf (alist-get 'clang-format-cpp apheleia-formatters)
        '("clang-format" (my/apheleia-get-cpp-style)))

  (setf (alist-get 'c-mode apheleia-mode-alist) 'clang-format-cpp)
  (setf (alist-get 'c++-mode apheleia-mode-alist) 'clang-format-cpp)
  (setf (alist-get 'c-ts-mode apheleia-mode-alist) 'clang-format-cpp)
  (setf (alist-get 'c++-ts-mode apheleia-mode-alist) 'clang-format-cpp))

(defun my/run-dap-logic (&optional source-buffer)
  "Find binary based on SOURCE-BUFFER and start DAP."
  (interactive)
  (let* (;; Use the provided buffer, or fall back to current if none provided
         (target-buffer (or source-buffer (current-buffer)))
         ;; Get the file name from that specific buffer
         (file-path (buffer-file-name target-buffer))
         (bin-name (if file-path (file-name-base file-path) ""))
         (bin-path (concat default-directory bin-name ".out"))
         ;; Check if the binary actually exists.
         ;; Performance: Skip synchronous 'file-exists-p' on remote paths to avoid freezes.
         (final-path (if (and (not (string-empty-p bin-name))
                              (or (file-remote-p default-directory)
                                  (file-exists-p bin-path)))
                         bin-path
                       (read-file-name "Select binary to debug: " default-directory))))
    (dap-debug
     (list :type "gdb"
           :request "launch"
           :name "GDB::SmartRun"
           :target final-path
           :cwd default-directory
           :valuesFormatting "prettyPrinters"
           :gdb-mi-interpreter "console"
           :gdb-args '("-ex" "set confirm off" "-ex" "set debuginfod enabled off")
           ))))

;; 1. Declare the variable globally so all functions can see it
(defvar my/dap-compilation-source-buffer nil
  "Global storage for the buffer that started a debug compile.")

;; 2. Updated Sentinel with better matching
(defun my/compilation-sentinel (_buf str)
  "Triggered when compilation finishes with status STR."
  (message "Sentinel checking buffer: %s" my/dap-compilation-source-buffer)
  ;; We use string-match-p to find "finished" anywhere in the status string
  (if (and (string-match-p "finished" str)
           my/dap-compilation-source-buffer)
      (let ((source-buf my/dap-compilation-source-buffer))
        (setq my/dap-compilation-source-buffer nil) ; Clear it
        (message "Logic passed! Launching debugger...")
        (run-with-timer 0.1 nil (lambda () (my/run-dap-logic source-buf))))
    (message "Logic failed: Status was '%s', Buffer was %s"
             (string-trim str)
             my/dap-compilation-source-buffer)))

;; 3. Updated Launcher
(defun my/dap-cpp-launch ()
  "The entry point: Remember the buffer, then compile."
  (interactive)
  (setq my/dap-compilation-source-buffer (current-buffer))
  (message "Source buffer saved: %s" (buffer-name my/dap-compilation-source-buffer))
  (add-hook 'compilation-finish-functions #'my/compilation-sentinel)
  (my/smart-compile t))

(add-hook 'c++-ts-mode-hook
          (lambda ()
            (require 'dap-gdb-lldb)
            (declare-function dap-gdb-lldb-setup "dap-gdb-lldb")
            (dap-gdb-lldb-setup)
            ;; Override the global F5 launcher for C++ buffers.
            (setq-local my/dap-start-debug #'my/dap-cpp-launch)))
#+end_src
