*** JVM-based languages (Java & Kotlin)

Java and Kotlin are grouped together here because they share the same runtime
ecosystem: the Java Virtual Machine (JVM). Both languages compile into the same
intermediate Java bytecode, allowing for seamless interoperability and shared
library usage within the same codebase.

We require SDKMAN! to manage our JVM-based ecosystems. It provides a
standardized way to install and switch between Java and Kotlin versions,
eliminating manual path configuration and preventing version conflicts. This
centralization ensures consistent development environments across all projects.
The functions below are helpers used in later installations to manage SDKMAN!.

#+begin_src sh :tangle setup.sh
IS_SDKMAN_INITIALIZED=false

is_installed_sdkman() {
  if [ -s "$HOME/.sdkman/bin/sdkman-init.sh" ]; then
    return 0
  fi

  return 1
}

initialize_sdkman() {
  if [[ "$IS_SDKMAN_INITIALIZED" == "false" ]]; then
    if [ -s "$HOME/.sdkman/bin/sdkman-init.sh" ]; then
      # shellcheck disable=SC1091
      [[ -s "$HOME/.sdkman/bin/sdkman-init.sh" ]] && source "$HOME/.sdkman/bin/sdkman-init.sh" > /dev/null 2>&1

      if command -v sdk >/dev/null 2>&1; then
        IS_SDKMAN_INITIALIZED=true
      fi
    else
      log_error "SDKMAN! initialization script not found."
      return 1
    fi
  fi
}

install_sdkman() {
  if ! is_installed_sdkman; then
    run_task "Installing SDKMAN" "curl -s 'https://get.sdkman.io' | bash"
  fi

  # Initialize will now be quiet
  initialize_sdkman
}
#+end_src


**** Java

***** Eclipse JDT Language Server (jdtls) installation

To enable full IDE features for Java in Emacs, we need to install
the Eclipse JDT Language Server (jdtls): the official, LSP-compliant engine for
Java. It brings Eclipse-grade analysis-including real-time diagnostics, advanced
refactoring, and intelligent autocompletion-directly into Emacs, providing full
IDE capabilities.

**On first use,** ~lsp-java~ will attempt to download the server automatically
if not found, but manual installation is more reliable and allows for custom
configuration.

#+begin_src sh :tangle setup.sh
# Download specific version of JDTLS. Update version/date as needed:
JDTLS_VERSION="1.57.0"
JDTLS_DATE="202602261110"
JDTLS_TARGET_DIR="$HOME/.emacs.d/lsp_language_servers/eclipse.jdt.ls"

is_installed_jdtls() {
  [ -f "$JDTLS_TARGET_DIR/bin/jdtls" ]
}

install_jdtls() {
  if ! is_installed_jdtls; then
    local JDTLS_TMP_DIR
    JDTLS_TMP_DIR=$(mktemp -d)
    mkdir -p "$JDTLS_TARGET_DIR"
    run_task "Downloading JDTLS" "curl -L -o $JDTLS_TMP_DIR/jdtls.tar.gz https://download.eclipse.org/jdtls/milestones/${JDTLS_VERSION}/jdt-language-server-${JDTLS_VERSION}-${JDTLS_DATE}.tar.gz"
    run_task "Extracting JDTLS" "tar -xzf $JDTLS_TMP_DIR/jdtls.tar.gz -C $JDTLS_TARGET_DIR"

    rm -rf "$JDTLS_TMP_DIR"
  fi
}

uninstall_jdtls() {
  if is_installed_jdtls; then
    run_task "Removing existing JDTLS installation" "rm -Rf${VERBOSE:+v} $JDTLS_TARGET_DIR"
  fi
}
#+end_src


***** Installing multiple Java Versions

We have standardized on Eclipse Temurin for our JVM development. We use Temurin
because it provides reliable, TCK-certified, and high-performance binaries that
are fully open-source and vendor-neutral, ensuring consistent behavior across
all developer machines. This configuration defaults to Temurin for its
stability; should your specific project require a different distribution (such
as Oracle or Amazon Corretto), we can implement a custom setup for that
environment as needed.


#+begin_src sh :tangle setup.sh
is_installed_java() {
  return 1 # SDKMAN! will skip already installed versions
}

install_java() {
  install_jdtls
  install_sdkman

  JAVA_VERSIONS_TO_INSTALL=(
    "8.0.482-tem"
    "11.0.30-tem"
    "17.0.18-tem"
    "21.0.10-tem"
    "25.0.2-tem"
  )

  for v in "${JAVA_VERSIONS_TO_INSTALL[@]}"; do
    run_task "Java $v" "sdk install java $v > /dev/null 2>&1"
  done
}

uninstall_java() {
  uninstall_jdtls
  log_skip "Java SDKs (SDKMAN!)."
}

register_action "Install Java" "java" "jvm"
#+end_src


***** Google Java Format

The [[https://github.com/google/google-java-format][Google Java Format]] tool reformats Java source code to comply with the
[[https://google.github.io/styleguide/javaguide.html][Google Java Style Guide]] (2-space indentation). We use the pre-built native
binary (GraalVM-based) so no JDK is required at formatting time, and
formatting is fast ({{{<}}}100 ms per file).

The native binary is downloaded from the latest GitHub release and placed
under =~/.emacs.d/google-java-format/=. Apheleia uses it as an external
formatter for Java buffers (see the Java setup section below).

#+begin_src sh :tangle setup.sh
GOOGLE_JAVA_FORMAT_VERSION="1.25.0"
GOOGLE_JAVA_FORMAT_DIR="$HOME/.emacs.d/google-java-format"
GOOGLE_JAVA_FORMAT_BIN="$GOOGLE_JAVA_FORMAT_DIR/google-java-format"

is_installed_google_java_format() {
  [ -x "$GOOGLE_JAVA_FORMAT_BIN" ]
}

install_google_java_format() {
  if ! is_installed_google_java_format; then
    # Determine platform binary name.
    local arch
    arch="$(uname -m)"
    local os
    os="$(uname -s | tr '[:upper:]' '[:lower:]')"
    local binary_name

    case "${os}_${arch}" in
      linux_x86_64)  binary_name="google-java-format_linux-x86-64" ;;
      linux_aarch64) binary_name="google-java-format_linux-arm64" ;;
      darwin_arm64)  binary_name="google-java-format_darwin-arm64" ;;
      *)
        log_error "No pre-built google-java-format binary for ${os}_${arch}."
        log_error "Install manually: https://github.com/google/google-java-format/releases"
        return 1
        ;;
    esac

    mkdir -p "$GOOGLE_JAVA_FORMAT_DIR"

    local url="https://github.com/google/google-java-format/releases/download/v${GOOGLE_JAVA_FORMAT_VERSION}/${binary_name}"
    run_task "Downloading google-java-format v${GOOGLE_JAVA_FORMAT_VERSION}" \
             "curl -L -o '$GOOGLE_JAVA_FORMAT_BIN' '$url'"
    chmod +x "$GOOGLE_JAVA_FORMAT_BIN"
  fi
}

uninstall_google_java_format() {
  if is_installed_google_java_format; then
    run_task "Removing google-java-format" "rm -Rf${VERBOSE:+v} $GOOGLE_JAVA_FORMAT_DIR"
  fi
}

register_action "Install Google Java Format" "google-java-format" "jvm"
#+end_src


***** ~lsp-java~ configuration

The ~lsp-java~ package integrates the Java language server, providing rich
editing support for Java projects. We need to configure the path in our Emacs
configuration (this is set in the ~lsp-java~ section below).

The block below also contains cross-cutting LSP session hygiene settings that
apply to all language servers:

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
;; Register accepted extensions for workspace folder filtering
(add-to-list 'my/lsp-server-file-extensions '(jdtls . ("java")))

(add-to-list 'load-path "/home/saulo/java-agent-loader/")
(load "/home/saulo/java-agent-loader/jal.el")
(require 'jal)

;; Path to the google-java-format native binary installed by setup.sh.
(defconst my/google-java-format-bin
  (expand-file-name "google-java-format/google-java-format"
                    user-emacs-directory)
  "Path to the google-java-format native binary.")

;; Register google-java-format as an Apheleia formatter for Java.
;; The native binary reads stdin (via "-") and writes formatted
;; code to stdout. Apheleia handles the rest.
(with-eval-after-load 'apheleia
  (setf (alist-get 'google-java-format apheleia-formatters)
        (list my/google-java-format-bin "-"))
  (setf (alist-get 'java-mode apheleia-mode-alist) 'google-java-format)
  (setf (alist-get 'java-ts-mode apheleia-mode-alist) 'google-java-format))

;; Disable LSP-based Java formatting. Apheleia with
;; google-java-format handles formatting instead.
(with-eval-after-load 'lsp-java
  (setq lsp-java-format-enabled nil))

(use-package lsp-java
  :pin "MELPA Unstable"
  :after java-ts-mode
  :init
  ;; These must be set BEFORE jal-lsp-java-setup runs, because jal calls
  ;; (require 'lsp-java) which defines the defcustoms with defaults.
  ;; use-package :custom runs too late (after :init), so we use setq here.
  (setq lsp-java-server-install-dir
        (expand-file-name "~/.emacs.d/lsp_language_servers/eclipse.jdt.ls/"))
  (setq lsp-java-vmargs
        '("-Xmx2G"
          "-XX:+UseG1GC"
          "-XX:+UseStringDeduplication"))
  ;; Disable jdtls auto-build to prevent it from overwriting Maven's
  ;; compiled output in target/classes with its own (sometimes incorrect)
  ;; class files. When auto-build is on, jdtls compiles in the background
  ;; and can write error-stub class files (e.g. "Unresolved compilation
  ;; problem") on top of a clean Maven build. With auto-build off, Maven
  ;; is the sole authority on target/classes and the DAP classpath is
  ;; always consistent with what `mvn compile' produced.
  (setq lsp-java-autobuild-enabled nil)

  (jal-lsp-java-setup)
  (add-to-list 'auto-mode-alist '("\\.java.*\\'" . java-ts-mode))

  :hook
  (java-ts-mode . lsp-deferred)

  :bind
  (:map java-ts-mode-map
        ("C-M-:" . lsp-find-implementation)
        ("M-RET" . lsp-java-organize-imports))

  :config
  (declare-function dap--cur-session "dap-mode")
  (declare-function dap--session-running "dap-mode")

  (defun my/dap-java--check-requirements ()
    "Return non-nil if the Java LSP server is running in this buffer.
Called by `my/dap-run-continue-dispatch' before attempting to start a debug
session. Logs an actionable hint when the check fails."
    (if (and (fboundp 'lsp-find-workspace)
             (lsp-find-workspace 'jdtls (buffer-file-name)))
        t
      (message
       (concat "jdtls is not running in this buffer. "
               "Open a Java file and wait for LSP to initialize."))
      nil))

  (defun my/dap-java--start-debug ()
    "Launch a Java DAP session for the current project.
Uses .vscode/launch.json when present; falls back to the
interactive `dap-java-debug' prompt otherwise."
    (let ((root (lsp-workspace-root)))
      (if (and root (file-exists-p (expand-file-name ".vscode/launch.json" root)))
          (call-interactively 'dap-debug)
        (dap-java-debug))))

  (add-hook 'java-ts-mode-hook
            (lambda ()
              (setq-local my/dap-check-requirements
                          #'my/dap-java--check-requirements)
              (setq-local my/dap-start-debug
                          #'my/dap-java--start-debug)))

  (defun my/lsp-java-discover-runtimes ()
    "Discover SDKMAN! java versions and return them as a vector for lsp-java.

Maps SDKMAN version directories to JDT execution environment names
(e.g. `JavaSE-17'). When multiple patch versions of the same major
exist only the lexicographically latest one is kept. All discovered
runtimes are passed to jdtls; if jdtls does not recognise an environment
it emits an \"Invalid runtime\" warning which is intercepted by
`my/lsp-java-invalid-runtime-advice' to show an actionable hint."
    (let ((sdk-dir (expand-file-name "~/.sdkman/candidates/java")))
      (when (file-exists-p sdk-dir)
        (let* ((dirs (seq-filter
                      (lambda (dir)
                        (and (file-directory-p dir)
                             (not (string-equal (file-name-nondirectory dir) "current"))))
                      (directory-files sdk-dir t "^[^.]")))
               (major->path (make-hash-table :test 'equal)))
          (dolist (path dirs)
            (let* ((dir-name (file-name-nondirectory path))
                   (major (when (string-match "^\\([0-9]+\\)" dir-name)
                            (string-to-number (match-string 1 dir-name)))))
              (when major
                (let ((existing (gethash major major->path)))
                  (when (or (null existing)
                            (string> dir-name (file-name-nondirectory existing)))
                    (puthash major path major->path))))))
          (let (runtime-list)
            (maphash (lambda (major path)
                       (let ((env-name (if (= major 8)
                                          "JavaSE-1.8"
                                        (format "JavaSE-%d" major))))
                         (push `(:name ,env-name :path ,path) runtime-list)))
                     major->path)
            (vconcat runtime-list))))))

  (setq lsp-java-configuration-runtimes (my/lsp-java-discover-runtimes))

  ;; jdtls is the source of truth for supported execution environments.
  ;; When it sends back an "Invalid runtime" warning (meaning a JDK we passed
  ;; is newer than what this jdtls release knows about), intercept it and
  ;; append an actionable hint so upgrading jdtls is the obvious next step.
  (defun my/lsp-java-invalid-runtime-advice (format-string &rest args)
    "Intercept jdtls \"Invalid runtime\" warnings and add an actionable hint."
    (let ((msg (apply #'format format-string args)))
      (when (string-match "Invalid runtime for \\(JavaSE-[0-9.]+\\)" msg)
        (message
         (concat "lsp-java: '%s' is not supported by the installed jdtls. "
                 "Consider upgrading jdtls to a newer release. "
                 "See: https://github.com/eclipse-jdtls/eclipse.jdt.ls/releases")
         (match-string 1 msg)))))

  (advice-add 'lsp--warn :after #'my/lsp-java-invalid-runtime-advice))
#+end_src


***** Workaround: Fix m2e property indirection causing incorrect Java compliance

~lsp-java~ uses the Eclipse JDT Language Server (=jdtls=), which delegates Maven
project import and POM processing to Maven Integration for Eclipse (=m2e=).
During import, =m2e= computes the effective POM, resolves dependencies, and
generates Eclipse project metadata -- such as =.classpath=, =.project=, and
=.settings= -- that =jdtls= consumes to build its project model and classpath.
Because =jdtls= relies on that generated metadata, any incorrect property
resolution performed by =m2e= (for example, resolving a property against a
parent POM instead of a child override) will propagate into the language server
as an incorrect Java compliance level or JRE container, resulting in missing
APIs, spurious diagnostics, and other incorrect language-server behavior.

When =m2e= evaluates Maven properties that use indirection (for example, when
=maven.compiler.source=, =maven.compiler.target= or =maven.compiler.release= are
defined in terms of another property such as =${project.build.java.target}=), it
can sometimes resolve the reference against the parent POM rather than a child
module's override. The result is generated Eclipse settings and workspace
metadata that claim an incorrect, older Java compliance level and place an older
JRE container on the module classpath (for example, =JavaSE-1.8=). That mismatch
causes language servers and editors to report missing APIs or incorrect
diagnostics.

This issue is reported at https://github.com/eclipse-m2e/m2e-core/issues/2156.

Solution implemented here (local workaround): after =m2e= finishes importing
projects (=jdtls= reports "Started"), the functions in this section read the
intended Java compliance value and rewrite the generated JDT workspace and
module files to match. Concretely, the code below:

- updates =.settings/org.eclipse.jdt.core.prefs= entries in the =jdtls=
  workspace metadata to set =org.eclipse.jdt.core.compiler.compliance=,
  =org.eclipse.jdt.core.compiler.source= and
  =org.eclipse.jdt.core.compiler.codegen.targetPlatform= to the resolved value;
- patches module =.classpath= entries that reference the =JRE_CONTAINER= so they
  point to the correct =JavaSE-<version>= container.

These changes are generic and apply to any Maven multi-module project where
property indirection (for example, a parent property =project.build.java.target=
and a child override) leads to a discrepancy between Maven's effective model and
the files =m2e= generates.

*Example:*

~Parent POM properties:~

#+begin_example xml
<project.build.java.target>1.8</project.build.java.target>
<maven.compiler.source>${project.build.java.target}</maven.compiler.source>
#+end_example

~Child POM override:~

#+begin_example xml
<project.build.java.target>25</project.build.java.target>
#+end_example

Follows the implementation of the workaround:

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(defun my/clean-jdtls-project-files (&optional root)
  "Delete stale Eclipse/jdtls project files.
It deletes `.classpath', `.project', `.settings' and `.factorypath'.
ROOT defaults to the current `lsp-workspace-root' or `projectile-project-root'.
These files are regenerated by jdtls/m2e on reimport. Delete them when
jdtls shows a wrong Java compliance level (e.g. reports Java-8 errors
on a Java-25 project) because m2e cached stale settings."
  (interactive)
  (let* ((root (or root
                   (and (fboundp 'lsp-workspace-root) (lsp-workspace-root))
                   (and (fboundp 'projectile-project-root) (projectile-project-root))
                   (read-directory-name "Project root: ")))
         (patterns '(".classpath" ".project" ".settings" ".factorypath"))
         (files '()))
    (dolist (pat patterns)
      (let ((found (directory-files-recursively root (concat "\\`" (regexp-quote pat) "\\'")
                                                t)))
        (setq files (append files found))))
    (if (null files)
        (message "No Eclipse project files found under %s" root)
      (when (yes-or-no-p
             (format "Delete %d Eclipse project files under %s? " (length files) root))
        (dolist (f files)
          (if (file-directory-p f)
              (delete-directory f t)
            (delete-file f)))
        (message "Deleted %d Eclipse project files. Restart LSP to reimport." (length files))))))

(defun my/clean-jdtls-full (&optional root)
  "Full jdtls cleanup: delete workspace cache AND stale Eclipse project files.
Use when jdtls shows wrong Java compliance or persistent stale diagnostics."
  (interactive)
  (my/clean-jdtls-project-files root)
  (my/clean-lsp-workspace-cache))

(defun my/jdtls-fix-m2e-compliance ()
  "Fix m2e setting the wrong Java compliance for Maven projects.
m2e in jdtls 1.57 does not resolve `maven.compiler.release' (or
`maven.compiler.source'/`target') correctly when the property uses
indirection through a parent POM. It falls back to the parent's default
(often Java 1.8) even though the child overrides the property to a higher
version. This function reads the effective `maven.compiler.release' (or
`source') from the project POM and writes the correct compliance to each
module's `.settings/org.eclipse.jdt.core.prefs'. jdtls picks up project-
directory settings with higher priority than its workspace metadata, so
this overrides the wrong m2e-generated values."
  (interactive)
  (let* ((root (or (and (fboundp 'lsp-workspace-root) (lsp-workspace-root))
                   (and (fboundp 'projectile-project-root) (projectile-project-root))
                   (read-directory-name "Project root: ")))
         (java-version (my/jdtls--detect-maven-java-version root)))
    (if (null java-version)
        (message "Could not detect Java version from Maven for %s" root)
      (let ((count 0))
        ;; Write .settings for each submodule that has a src/main/java
        (dolist (pom (directory-files-recursively root "\\`pom\\.xml\\'"))
          (let ((mod-dir (file-name-directory pom)))
            (when (or (file-directory-p (expand-file-name "src/main/java" mod-dir))
                      (string= (file-truename (expand-file-name root))
                               (file-truename mod-dir)))
              (my/jdtls--write-compliance-prefs mod-dir java-version)
              (setq count (1+ count)))))
        (message "Wrote Java %s compliance to %d module(s) under %s. Restart LSP to apply."
                 java-version count root)))))

(defun my/jdtls--detect-maven-java-version (root)
  "Detect the Java version from Maven compiler settings in ROOT.
Reads `maven.compiler.release', falling back to `maven.compiler.source',
then `maven.compiler.target'. Returns the version string (e.g. \"25\")
or nil."
  (let ((pom (expand-file-name "pom.xml" root)))
    (when (file-exists-p pom)
      (with-temp-buffer
        (insert-file-contents pom)
        (or (my/jdtls--extract-pom-property "maven.compiler.release")
            (my/jdtls--extract-pom-property "maven.compiler.source")
            (my/jdtls--extract-pom-property "maven.compiler.target")
            ;; Try resolving the property indirection one level
            (let ((prop-ref (my/jdtls--extract-pom-property-raw "maven.compiler.release")))
              (when (and prop-ref (string-match "\\${\\(.+\\)}" prop-ref))
                (my/jdtls--extract-pom-property (match-string 1 prop-ref)))))))))

(defun my/jdtls--extract-pom-property (name)
  "Extract a literal property value for NAME from the current buffer's POM.
Returns nil if the property is not found or is a property reference."
  (save-excursion
    (goto-char (point-min))
    (when (re-search-forward
           (format "<%s>\\([^$<]+\\)</%s>" (regexp-quote name) (regexp-quote name))
           nil t)
      (string-trim (match-string 1)))))

(defun my/jdtls--extract-pom-property-raw (name)
  "Extract a raw property value (including ${refs}) for NAME from the current buffer."
  (save-excursion
    (goto-char (point-min))
    (when (re-search-forward
           (format "<%s>\\([^<]+\\)</%s>" (regexp-quote name) (regexp-quote name))
           nil t)
      (string-trim (match-string 1)))))

(defun my/jdtls--write-compliance-prefs (dir java-version)
  "Write `.settings/org.eclipse.jdt.core.prefs' in DIR with JAVA-VERSION."
  (let* ((settings-dir (expand-file-name ".settings" dir))
         (prefs-file (expand-file-name "org.eclipse.jdt.core.prefs" settings-dir))
         (content (format "\
eclipse.preferences.version=1
org.eclipse.jdt.core.compiler.codegen.methodParameters=generate
org.eclipse.jdt.core.compiler.codegen.targetPlatform=%s
org.eclipse.jdt.core.compiler.compliance=%s
org.eclipse.jdt.core.compiler.problem.enablePreviewFeatures=disabled
org.eclipse.jdt.core.compiler.problem.forbiddenReference=warning
org.eclipse.jdt.core.compiler.problem.reportPreviewFeatures=ignore
org.eclipse.jdt.core.compiler.processAnnotations=enabled
org.eclipse.jdt.core.compiler.release=enabled
org.eclipse.jdt.core.compiler.source=%s
" java-version java-version java-version)))
    (make-directory settings-dir t)
    (write-region content nil prefs-file)))

(defun my/jdtls--fix-classpath-jre (dir env-name)
  "Fix the JRE container in DIR/.classpath to use ENV-NAME (e.g. \"JavaSE-25\").
This is useful when m2e generates `.classpath' with another JRE (e.g., JavaSE-1.8)
due to a property indirection bug (see https://github.com/eclipse-m2e/m2e-core/issues/2156
for details). The JRE container determines which class library is on the build path,
so even with correct compliance settings, APIs like List.of() are missing if
the container points to a version like JavaSE-1.8, for example."
  (let ((classpath-file (expand-file-name ".classpath" dir)))
    (when (file-exists-p classpath-file)
      (with-temp-buffer
        (insert-file-contents classpath-file)
        (when (re-search-forward
               "JRE_CONTAINER/org\\.eclipse\\.jdt\\.internal\\.debug\\.ui\\.launcher\\.StandardVMType/JavaSE-[0-9.]+"
               nil t)
          (let ((old (match-string 0))
                (new (format "JRE_CONTAINER/org.eclipse.jdt.internal.debug.ui.launcher.StandardVMType/%s"
                             env-name)))
            (unless (string= old new)
              (goto-char (point-min))
              (while (search-forward old nil t)
                (replace-match new t t))
              (write-region (point-min) (point-max) classpath-file))))))))
#+end_src

The next function advises jdtls's =language/status= callback to detect when
jdtls reports "Started" (meaning m2e has finished importing the project). At
that point, =m2e= has already written its (wrong) =.settings= files with
=compliance=1.8= (considering =1.8= as the wrong version resolved from our
examples). We overwrite them with the correct Java version resolved from the POM
in memory, then notify jdtls via =workspace/didChangeWatchedFiles= so it reloads
the settings. This runs on every "Started" event (including after
rebuild/restart) because m2e re-imports and overwrites =.settings= each time.
This way the POM is never modified.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(defun my/jdtls--fix-compliance-after-import (workspace status)
  "Advice :after `lsp-java--language-status-callback'.
When jdtls reports type=\"Started\", overwrite the m2e-generated .settings
and .classpath files with correct compliance and JRE container.
Runs on every \"Started\" event (including after rebuild/restart) because
m2e re-imports and overwrites these files each time.
WORKSPACE is the lsp workspace, STATUS is the notification payload."
  (when-let* ((type (if (hash-table-p status)
                        (gethash "type" status)
                      (plist-get status :type)))
              (_started (string= type "Started"))
              (root (lsp--workspace-root workspace))
              (pom (expand-file-name "pom.xml" root))
              (_has-pom (file-exists-p pom))
              (java-version (my/jdtls--detect-maven-java-version root)))
    (let ((count 0)
          (env-name (if (string= java-version "8")
                        "JavaSE-1.8"
                      (format "JavaSE-%s" java-version))))
      ;; 1. Write .settings in every module that has a pom.xml
      (dolist (mod-pom (directory-files-recursively root "\\`pom\\.xml\\'"))
        (let ((mod-dir (file-name-directory mod-pom)))
          (my/jdtls--write-compliance-prefs mod-dir java-version)
          (setq count (1+ count))))
      ;; 2. Overwrite the jdtls workspace metadata .settings AND .classpath
      (let ((ws-projects (expand-file-name
                          ".metadata/.plugins/org.eclipse.core.resources/.projects"
                          lsp-java-workspace-dir)))
        (when (file-directory-p ws-projects)
          (dolist (proj-dir (directory-files ws-projects t "^[^.]"))
            (when (file-directory-p proj-dir)
              (my/jdtls--write-compliance-prefs proj-dir java-version)
              (my/jdtls--fix-classpath-jre proj-dir env-name)))))
      ;; 3. Overwrite the global workspace JDT prefs
      (let ((global-prefs-dir (expand-file-name
                               ".metadata/.plugins/org.eclipse.core.runtime/.settings"
                               lsp-java-workspace-dir)))
        (when (file-directory-p global-prefs-dir)
          (my/jdtls--write-compliance-prefs
           (file-name-directory global-prefs-dir) java-version)))
      ;; 4. Notify jdtls about the changes via workspace/didChangeWatchedFiles
      (with-lsp-workspace workspace
        (let (changes)
          (dolist (mod-pom (directory-files-recursively root "\\`pom\\.xml\\'"))
            (let* ((mod-dir (file-name-directory mod-pom))
                   (prefs (expand-file-name ".settings/org.eclipse.jdt.core.prefs" mod-dir)))
              (when (file-exists-p prefs)
                (push (list :uri (lsp--path-to-uri prefs) :type 2) changes))))
          ;; Also notify about .classpath changes in workspace metadata
          (let ((ws-projects (expand-file-name
                              ".metadata/.plugins/org.eclipse.core.resources/.projects"
                              lsp-java-workspace-dir)))
            (when (file-directory-p ws-projects)
              (dolist (proj-dir (directory-files ws-projects t "^[^.]"))
                (let ((cp (expand-file-name ".classpath" proj-dir)))
                  (when (file-exists-p cp)
                    (push (list :uri (lsp--path-to-uri cp) :type 2) changes))))))
          (when changes
            (lsp-notify "workspace/didChangeWatchedFiles"
                        (list :changes (apply #'vector changes))))))
      (message "m2e compliance fix: wrote Java %s (%s) to %d module(s) + workspace metadata under %s"
               java-version env-name count root))))

(with-eval-after-load 'lsp-java
  (unless (advice-member-p #'my/jdtls--fix-compliance-after-import
                           #'lsp-java--language-status-callback)
    ;; After m2e finishes importing (jdtls reports "Started"), overwrite the
    ;; wrong .settings files with correct Java compliance resolved from POM.
    (advice-add 'lsp-java--language-status-callback :after
                #'my/jdtls--fix-compliance-after-import)))
#+end_src


***** DAP Java setup, part 1: debug adapter installation

Ensures the Java debug plugin jar is present. The jar ships with =dap-mode= and
is downloaded automatically on first use when opening a Java buffer.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(defun dap-java-debug-install ()
  "Download and install the Java debug plugin jar from Open VSX if absent."
  (let* ((version "0.58.5") ;; You can use latest or a specific version like 0.58.5
         (dest-dir (expand-file-name
                    "github/microsoft.java-debug/extension/server"
                    dap-utils-extension-path))
         (existing-jars (file-expand-wildcards (expand-file-name "com.microsoft.java.debug.plugin-*.jar" dest-dir)))
         (jar-path (car existing-jars))
         ;; Use Open VSX which serves standard ZIP files without gzip streaming wrappers
         (vsix-url (format "https://open-vsx.org/api/vscjava/vscode-java-debug/%s/file/vscjava.vscode-java-debug-%s.vsix" version version)))
    (unless jar-path
      (make-directory dest-dir t)
      (let ((tmp-vsix (make-temp-file "java-debug-vsix-" nil ".zip")))
        ;; 1. Download directly to standard zip format
        (url-copy-file vsix-url tmp-vsix t)
        ;; 2. Extract without needing zcat by using wildcards to find the exact jar name
        (call-process-shell-command
         (format "unzip -j -o -d %s %s \"extension/server/com.microsoft.java.debug.plugin-*.jar\""
                 (shell-quote-argument dest-dir)
                 (shell-quote-argument tmp-vsix)))
        (delete-file tmp-vsix)
        (setq jar-path (car (file-expand-wildcards (expand-file-name "com.microsoft.java.debug.plugin-*.jar" dest-dir))))))
    jar-path))
#+end_src


***** DAP Java setup, part 2: =compile_attach= provider

The Eclipse JDT language server, when told to =launch= with =integratedTerminal=,
builds the java command with =-agentlib:jdwp=...server=n=: the debug adapter
listens and the JVM connects to it. This model is sent via a =runInTerminal=
DAP reverse-request, and none of the standard dap-mode hooks can intercept it
before the connection model is agreed upon.

The solution is to replace the =launch= flow entirely with =compile_attach=:
Emacs starts the JVM itself with =server=y= (the JVM listens), then the debug
adapter connects to it as a client. This also allows =vmArgs=, =args=, and =env=
from =launch.json= to be forwarded correctly.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
;; JDK 17+ --add-opens flags for the debugged process and other variables.
;; Many frameworks (Spring, Hibernate, etc.) require these.
;; Customise as needed.
(defvar my/dap-java--extra-vmargs
  '("--add-opens=java.base/java.util=ALL-UNNAMED"
    "--add-opens=java.base/java.lang=ALL-UNNAMED"
    "--add-opens=java.base/java.math=ALL-UNNAMED"
    "--add-opens=java.base/java.nio=ALL-UNNAMED"
    "--add-opens=java.base/sun.nio.ch=ALL-UNNAMED"
    "--add-opens=java.base/java.util.concurrent=ALL-UNNAMED"
    "--add-opens=java.base/java.net=ALL-UNNAMED"
    "--add-modules=ALL-SYSTEM"
    "-Dsun.zip.disableMemoryMapping=true"
    "-Xms100m"
    "-XX:AdaptiveSizePolicyWeight=90")
  "JDK 17+ reflective access and other flags appended to vmArgs when debugging.")

(defun my/dap-java--normalize-to-list (value)
  "Normalize VALUE to a flat list of strings.
VALUE may be nil, a string, a vector, or a list of strings."
  (cond
   ((null value)    nil)
   ((stringp value) (split-string value "[ \t]+" t))
   ((vectorp value) (append value nil))
   ((listp value)   (seq-filter #'stringp value))
   (t               nil)))

(defun my/dap-java--merge-vmargs (conf)
  "Return merged vmArgs string from CONF plus compat flags.
Reads :vmArgs (or :vmargs) from CONF, normalizes the value,
appends `my/dap-java--extra-vmargs', and returns a single
space-separated string.
CONF is the launch configuration plist or hash-table."
  (let* ((raw (or (plist-get conf :vmArgs)
                  (plist-get conf :vmargs)
                  (when (hash-table-p conf)
                    (or (gethash "vmArgs" conf)
                        (gethash "vmargs" conf)))))
         (user-list (my/dap-java--normalize-to-list raw))
         (lsp-list (when (boundp 'lsp-java-vmargs)
                     (my/dap-java--normalize-to-list lsp-java-vmargs)))
         (merged (delete-dups (append user-list lsp-list my/dap-java--extra-vmargs))))
    (mapconcat #'identity merged " ")))

(defun my/dap-java--normalize-prog-args (conf)
  "Return program args as a single space-separated string from CONF.
Handles nil, string, vector, and list values for :args.
CONF is the launch configuration plist or hash-table."
  (let ((raw (or (plist-get conf :args)
                 (when (hash-table-p conf) (gethash "args" conf)))))
    (cond
     ((null raw)      "")
     ((stringp raw)   raw)
     ((sequencep raw) (mapconcat #'identity (append raw nil) " "))
     (t               ""))))

(defun my/dap-java--env-to-alist (env)
  "Convert an ENV map from launch.json to an alist for dap-mode.
Input ENV may be a hash-table with string keys, or a plist with
keyword keys. Output is an alist of (\"KEY\" . \"value\") pairs."
  (cond
   ((null env) nil)
   ((hash-table-p env)
    (let (result)
      (maphash (lambda (k v) (push (cons k v) result)) env)
      result))
   ((listp env)
    (cl-loop for (k v) on env by #'cddr
             collect (cons (if (keywordp k)
                               (substring (symbol-name k) 1)
                             (format "%s" k))
                           v)))
   (t nil)))

(defun my/dap-java--wait-for-jvm-listening (port &optional timeout)
  "Block until any buffer prints JVM listening on PORT, then return t.
Polls all live buffers once per second for the string the JDWP
agent prints when ready. Returns nil if TIMEOUT seconds elapse
without the message appearing. TIMEOUT defaults to 120 seconds.
Using buffer scanning instead of a TCP probe avoids sending a
bare TCP connection to the JDWP socket, which the JVM interprets
as a malformed handshake and reports as \"connection prematurely
closed\"."
  (let ((pattern (format "Listening for transport dt_socket at address: %d"
                         port))
        (limit   (or timeout 120))
        (found   nil)
        (i       0))
    (while (and (not found) (< i limit))
      (setq found
            (seq-some (lambda (buf)
                        (with-current-buffer buf
                          (save-excursion
                            (goto-char (point-min))
                            (search-forward pattern nil t))))
                      (buffer-list)))
      (unless found
        (sit-for 1)
        (setq i (1+ i))))
    (when found
      (message "DAP Java: JVM listening on JDWP port %d after %ds" port i))
    found))

(defun my/dap-java--is-terminal-launch (conf)
  "Return t when CONF targets an integrated or external terminal.
A config is a terminal launch when :request is \"launch\" and
:console is \"integratedTerminal\" or \"externalTerminal\".
CONF is the launch configuration plist or hash-table."
  (let ((request (or (plist-get conf :request)
                     (when (hash-table-p conf) (gethash "request" conf))))
        (console  (or (plist-get conf :console)
                      (when (hash-table-p conf) (gethash "console" conf)))))
    (and (equal request "launch")
         (member console '("integratedTerminal" "externalTerminal")))))

(defun my/dap-java--default-compilation-command (root)
  "Return the default DAP compilation command for a Java project at ROOT.
ROOT should be the top-level workspace root (not a submodule), obtained
from the jdtls workspace struct via `lsp--workspace-root'. Detects Maven
(pom.xml) or Gradle (build.gradle / build.gradle.kts) and returns the
appropriate compile command string. Logs a message and returns nil when
no recognised build file is found, so the build step is skipped."
  (cond
   ((file-exists-p (expand-file-name "pom.xml" root))
    "mvn compile")
   ((or (file-exists-p (expand-file-name "build.gradle" root))
        (file-exists-p (expand-file-name "build.gradle.kts" root)))
    "gradle compileJava")
   (t
    (message "DAP Java: no pom.xml or build.gradle found in %s; skipping compilation step" root)
    nil)))

(defun my/dap-java--build-compile-attach (conf)
  "Build a compile_attach session plist from CONF.
Implements server=y: the JVM opens a debug socket and the debug
adapter connects to it. This avoids the default server=n path
used by JDT for launch+integratedTerminal configurations.

Keys read from CONF:
  :vmArgs / :vmargs    -- JVM flags merged with compat flags
  :args                -- program arguments after the main class
  :env                 -- environment variables (hash-table or plist)
  :mainClass           -- fully qualified main class name
  :projectName         -- Eclipse project name
  :cwd                 -- project working directory (used to find jdtls)
  :dap-compilation     -- optional shell command to run before attaching
  :dap-compilation-dir -- working directory for the build command

CONF is the launch configuration plist from the launch.json entry."
  (let* (;; Use mainClass and projectName directly from conf.
         ;; dap-java--populate-launch-args is intentionally bypassed: it
         ;; calls (lsp-find-workspace 'jdtls) internally without a path
         ;; arg, which falls back to the current buffer's LSP server when
         ;; called from a non-Java buffer, causing a
         ;; workspace/executeCommand error.
         (main-class    (or (plist-get conf :mainClass)
                            (user-error
                             "No :mainClass in launch configuration")))
         (project-name  (or (plist-get conf :projectName)
                            (user-error
                             "No :projectName in launch configuration")))
         (cwd           (or (plist-get conf :cwd) (lsp-java--get-root)))
         ;; Find the jdtls workspace using the project cwd as the lookup
         ;; key. lsp-find-workspace with a path uses
         ;; lsp-find-session-folder, which searches registered workspace
         ;; roots -- reliable even when called from a non-Java buffer.
         ;; Falls back to any jdtls workspace in the session if the path
         ;; lookup fails (e.g. if cwd is a subdirectory not cached yet).
         (jdtls-ws      (or (lsp-find-workspace 'jdtls cwd)
                            (lsp-find-workspace 'jdtls)
                            (user-error
                             (concat "jdtls is not running for %s. "
                                     "Open a Java file first")
                             cwd)))
         ;; Resolve the classpath via jdtls using the explicit workspace.
         ;; If jdtls's debug plugin is still initializing (e.g. right
         ;; after a failed session) this command may fail; the error
         ;; message instructs the user to wait and retry.
         (classpaths    (or (cl-second
                             (with-lsp-workspace jdtls-ws
                               (lsp-send-execute-command
                                "vscode.java.resolveClasspath"
                                (vector main-class project-name))))
                            (user-error
                             (concat "Cannot resolve classpath for %s. "
                                     "Wait for jdtls to finish "
                                     "initializing (check *jdtls::stderr*)"
                                     " and retry")
                             main-class)))
         (vmargs-str    (my/dap-java--merge-vmargs conf))
         (prog-args-str (my/dap-java--normalize-prog-args conf))
         (env-alist     (my/dap-java--env-to-alist
                         (or (plist-get conf :env)
                             (when (hash-table-p conf) (gethash "env" conf)))))
         (port          (dap--find-available-port))
         ;; The JVM listens on PORT (server=y). Use localhost:PORT form
         ;; explicitly: Java 9+ changed the default binding for bare-port
         ;; addresses and some versions refuse to accept connections without
         ;; the explicit host. Do NOT use quiet=y here so that JDWP errors
         ;; are visible in the compilation log for troubleshooting.
         (jdwp-arg
          (format
           "-agentlib:jdwp=transport=dt_socket,server=y,suspend=y,address=localhost:%d"
           port))
         ;; Reference the classpath via an env variable to avoid shell
         ;; quoting issues when the classpath contains spaces.
         (cp-var (format dap-java--var-format "CLASSPATH_ARGS"))
         ;; Build the full java invocation. seq-filter removes empty
         ;; tokens so no stray spaces appear when args are absent.
         (java-command
          (string-trim
           (mapconcat
            #'identity
            (seq-filter (lambda (s) (not (string-empty-p s)))
                        (list dap-java-java-command
                              vmargs-str jdwp-arg
                              "-cp" cp-var
                              main-class prog-args-str))
            " ")))
         ;; Merge the classpath env var with application env vars from conf.
         (env-variables
          (append
           (list (cons "CLASSPATH_ARGS"
                       (s-join dap-java--classpath-separator classpaths)))
           env-alist)))
    (message "DAP Java (compile_attach): %s:%s port=%d"
             project-name main-class port)
    (let* ((base-conf
            (dap-java--populate-attach-args
             (list :type              "java"
                   :request           "attach"
                   :hostName          "localhost"
                   :projectName       project-name
                   :host              "localhost"
                   ;; Do NOT use :wait-for-port here.
                   ;; dap--wait-for-port opens a raw TCP connection to
                   ;; probe the port and immediately closes it. The
                   ;; JDWP agent treats that bare connection as a
                   ;; malformed handshake and prints "connection
                   ;; prematurely closed". We wait inside
                   ;; :startup-function instead, by scanning buffers for
                   ;; the "Listening for transport" line that the JDWP
                   ;; agent itself prints, which avoids any TCP probe.
                   :program-to-start  java-command
                   :port              port
                   :environment-variables env-variables
                   ;; Forward the optional build step from launch.json.
                   ;; If absent, auto-detect Maven or Gradle from the
                   ;; top-level workspace root (not cwd, which may be a
                   ;; submodule). Using lsp--workspace-root ensures we
                   ;; always compile the root project.
                   :dap-compilation
                   (or (plist-get conf :dap-compilation)
                       (when (hash-table-p conf)
                         (gethash "dap-compilation" conf))
                       (my/dap-java--default-compilation-command
                        (lsp--workspace-root jdtls-ws)))
                   :dap-compilation-dir
                   (or (plist-get conf :dap-compilation-dir)
                       (when (hash-table-p conf)
                         (gethash "dap-compilation-dir" conf))
                       (lsp--workspace-root jdtls-ws))
                   ;; Placeholder so plist-put in :startup-function
                   ;; mutates in-place: dap--create-session reads
                   ;; :debugServer after :startup-function runs.
                   :debugServer       0
                   :__sessionId       (number-to-string (float-time))
                   ;; Wait for the JVM's JDWP agent to print
                   ;; "Listening for transport" in the log buffer,
                   ;; then start the debug adapter. Waiting here (not
                   ;; via :wait-for-port) avoids the raw-TCP probe that
                   ;; breaks the JDWP handshake.
                   :startup-function
                   (let ((ws   jdtls-ws)   ; capture workspace in closure
                         (p    port))
                     (lambda (launch-args)
                       (unless (my/dap-java--wait-for-jvm-listening p)
                         (user-error
                          "JVM did not start listening on port %d" p))
                       (plist-put launch-args :debugServer
                                  (with-lsp-workspace ws
                                    (lsp-send-execute-command
                                     "vscode.java.startDebugSession")))))))))
      base-conf)))

(defun my/dap-java--debug-provider (conf)
  "Java debug provider: use compile_attach for terminal launches.
For launch configs with console integratedTerminal or
externalTerminal, delegates to `my/dap-java--build-compile-attach'
which starts the JVM with server=y and has the debug adapter
attach to it. This avoids the runInTerminal (server=n) path
sent by JDT that cannot be intercepted by standard dap-mode hooks.
For all other configurations (attach, internalConsole), falls
back to `dap-java--populate-default-args'.
CONF is the launch configuration plist or hash-table."
  (if (my/dap-java--is-terminal-launch conf)
      (my/dap-java--build-compile-attach conf)
    (dap-java--populate-default-args conf)))
#+end_src


***** DAP Java setup, part 3: =compile_attach= provider and project integration

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(with-eval-after-load 'lsp-java
  (with-eval-after-load 'dap-mode
    (add-to-list 'lsp-java-bundles (dap-java-debug-install))

    ;; Enable reading debug settings from .vscode/launch.json.
    (setq dap-java-use-project-settings t)

    ;; Override the built-in Java provider with our =compile_attach=-aware one.
    ;; For launch+integratedTerminal, this replaces the server=n+runInTerminal
    ;; flow with server=y, which correctly propagates vmArgs and env from the
    ;; launch configuration.
    (dap-register-debug-provider "java" #'my/dap-java--debug-provider)))
#+end_src


***** DAP Java setup, part 4: project =.vscode/launch.json= configuration

=dap-mode= is the Emacs client for the [[https://microsoft.github.io/debug-adapter-protocol/][Debug Adapter Protocol (DAP)]]. DAP is
the same wire protocol used by VS Code to communicate with language-specific
debug adapters (such as the Microsoft Java Debug Extension). Emacs with
=dap-mode= acts as a DAP client in the same way VS Code does, so the two share
the same configuration file format: =.vscode/launch.json=.

The connection is:

#+begin_example
  =.vscode/launch.json=          (project config, shared with VS Code)
         │
         │  =M-x dap-debug= reads the JSON, expands =${workspaceFolder}=,
         │  and presents the "name" fields as a selection menu.
         ▼
  =my/dap-java--debug-provider=  (part 3 of this config)
         │
         │  Inspects "console" field; if "integratedTerminal" →
         │  converts to compile_attach (=server=y=) flow.
         ▼
  Eclipse JDT Debug Adapter    (=~/.emacs.d/eclipse.jdt.ls/...=)
         │
         │  DAP protocol over TCP (port from =vscode.java.startDebugSession=)
         ▼
  JVM JDWP agent               (=-agentlib:jdwp=…,server=y,address=localhost:PORT=)
#+end_example

Because =dap-mode= understands the standard VS Code launch schema, the same
=.vscode/launch.json= you use with VS Code works in Emacs without
modification. Fields prefixed with =dap-= (e.g. =dap-compilation=) are Emacs
extensions understood only by =dap-mode= and ignored by VS Code.

Therefore, each Java project that you want to debug must have a
=.vscode/launch.json= file at the workspace root. =dap-mode= reads this file
when you invoke =dap-debug= (=M-x dap-debug=) and selects a configuration by
name. The=my/dap-java--debug-provider= installed in part 3 then routes any
=launch= configuration that targets =integratedTerminal= through the
=compile_attach= flow described in part 2.


**Minimum required fields:**

| Field                 | Purpose                                                          |
|-----------------------+------------------------------------------------------------------|
| =type=                | Must be ="java"= - selects the Java debug provider.              |
| =request=             | Must be ="launch"= - the provider converts it to compile_attach. |
| =name=                | Human-readable label shown in the dap-debug selection menu.      |
| =mainClass=           | Fully-qualified main class (e.g. =com.example.app.Application=). |
| =projectName=         | Maven/Gradle project name, used to resolve the classpath.        |
| =cwd=                 | Working directory; use =${workspaceFolder}= for the repo root.   |
| =console=             | Must be ="integratedTerminal"= to trigger the compile_attach     |
|                       | flow. Use ="internalConsole"= to fall back to the default        |
|                       | JDT launch flow (no vmArgs forwarding).                          |
| =vmArgs=              | JVM arguments for the *debugged process* (not the language       |
|                       | server). Spring Boot =−D= properties belong here.                |
| =args=                | Program arguments passed after the main class name.              |
| =env=                 | Extra environment variables (e.g. =SPRING_PROFILES_ACTIVE=).     |


**Optional =dap-*= fields:**

| Field                 | Purpose                                                          |
|-----------------------+------------------------------------------------------------------|
| =dap-compilation=     | **Optional.** Shell command run by dap-mode *before* launching.  |
|                       | If omitted, auto-detected as described above.                    |
| =dap-compilation-dir= | **Optional.** Directory for =dap-compilation=.                   |
|                       | If omitted, defaults to =${workspaceFolder}=.                    |

Fields prefixed with =dap-= (e.g. =dap-compilation=, =dap-compilation-dir=) are
**optional** Emacs extensions. If omitted, our =dap-mode= helpers provide
sensible fallbacks:

- If =dap-compilation= is missing, we will attempt to auto-detect the build tool
  at the workspace root:
  - If a =pom.xml= is found, it uses =mvn compile=.
  - If a =build.gradle= or =build.gradle.kts= is found, it uses =gradle
    compileJava=.
  - If neither is found, no build step is run (the value is nil).
- If =dap-compilation-dir= is missing, the workspace root is used as the working
  directory for the build command.

These fields are ignored by VS Code and only affect Emacs. You only need to
specify them if you want to override the auto-detected defaults (for example, to
use a custom build script or a submodule directory).


**Example =.vscode/launch.json= for a Spring Boot application:**

#+begin_src json
{
  "version": "0.2.0",
  "configurations": [
    {
      "type": "java",
      "request": "launch",
      "name": "App Name - dev - Build and Debug",
      "mainClass": "com.example.app.Application",
      "projectName": "my-spring-application",
      "cwd": "${workspaceFolder}",
      "console": "integratedTerminal",
      "vmArgs": [
        "-Dserver.port=8081",
        "-Dspring.datasource.url=jdbc:postgresql://localhost:5432/orders"
      ],
      "args": [],
      "env": {
        "SPRING_PROFILES_ACTIVE": "local,dev"
      },
      // The following dap-* fields are optional:
      // "dap-compilation": "mvn compile -q", // If omitted, auto-detected or skipped
      // "dap-compilation-dir": "${workspaceFolder}" // If omitted, defaults to workspace root
    }
  ]
}
#+end_src

**Notes:**

- =projectName= must match the =<artifactId>= in the root =pom.xml= (Maven) or
  the Gradle project name exactly--JDT uses it to resolve classpaths.
- =vmArgs= here controls *your application*, not the language server. Do not
  confuse it with =lsp-java-vmargs= which is for the JDT LS process itself.
- The =dap-compilation= step (if present or auto-detected) runs synchronously
  before the JVM starts; if it fails, the debug session is aborted. For Maven,
  use =-q= (Maven quiet) to reduce noise.
- Add =.vscode/launch.json= to your project =.gitignore= if the values contain
  environment-specific secrets or local paths.


***** DAP Java setup, part 5: inline test links in =*Test.java= buffers

In test files (matching =*Test.java=), class names and test method names are
decorated as clickable inline links so you can run or debug a specific test
without leaving the buffer. The links are only added in files whose name
ends with =Test.java=, so production code is not affected.

Each link style is distinct so you can see at a glance what an action will do:

- Class name links are underlined in green -> run or debug all tests in class.
- Method name links are underlined in orange -> run or debug that method only.

Test method detection covers all visibility modifiers (=public=, =protected=,
=private=, or package-private) so it handles both JUnit 4 (=public void=) and
JUnit 5 (package-private =void=) conventions.

Keyboard and mouse shortcuts (only active in =*Test.java= buffers):

- =click= - run the test at point (no debugger, fast feedback).
- =C-S-click= - debug the test at point (attaches the debugger, hits breakpoints).
- =C-c j= - run the test at point (keyboard equivalent of click).
- =C-c C-j= - debug the test at point (keyboard equivalent of C-S-click).

All shortcuts work whether the cursor is on a link or anywhere else in the
file; when on a link the link action is used, otherwise LSP symbols resolve
the enclosing class or method.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(defun my/dap-java-run-test-at-point ()
  "Run the test at point without the debugger.
If point is on an inline test link, activate its run action.
Otherwise fall back to `dap-java-run-test-method'."
  (interactive)
  (if (button-at (point))
      (push-button)
    (dap-java-run-test-method)))

(defun my/dap-java-debug-test-at-point ()
  "Debug the test at point, attaching the DAP debugger.
If point is on an inline test link, call the link's debug-action.
Otherwise fall back to `dap-java-debug-test-method'."
  (interactive)
  (let* ((btn (button-at (point)))
         (debug-fn (and btn (button-get btn 'debug-action))))
    (if debug-fn
        (funcall debug-fn)
      (dap-java-debug-test-method (dap--find-available-port)))))

(defun my/dap-java--ctrl-click-debug (event)
  "Debug the test at the position of a C-S-click mouse EVENT."
  (interactive "e")
  (mouse-set-point event)
  (my/dap-java-debug-test-at-point))

(defun my/dap-java--add-test-links ()
  "Decorate class and method names in *Test.java buffers as inline links.
Links are added for:
- Class names (underlined green): run/debug all tests in the class.
- Method names (underlined orange): run/debug the individual test.
Only activates when the buffer file name ends with Test.java.
Visibility modifiers are optional, covering JUnit 4 (public void)
and JUnit 5 (package-private void) conventions."
  (when (and buffer-file-name
             (string-match-p "Test\\.java\\'" buffer-file-name))
    (save-excursion
      ;; --- Class declarations ---
      (goto-char (point-min))
      (while (re-search-forward
              "^\\s-*\\(?:public\\|protected\\|private\\)?\\s-*class\\s-+\\([A-Za-z0-9_]+\\)"
              nil t)
        (make-button (match-beginning 1) (match-end 1)
                     'action       (lambda (_b) (dap-java-run-test-class))
                     'debug-action (lambda ()
                                     (dap-java-debug-test-class
                                      (dap--find-available-port)))
                     'help-echo    "click: run | C-S-click: debug | C-c j: run | C-c C-j: debug"
                     'face         '(:underline t :foreground "green")))
      ;; --- void methods (any visibility or none) ---
      (goto-char (point-min))
      (while (re-search-forward
              (concat "^\\s-*\\(?:public\\|protected\\|private\\)?\\s-*"
                      "void\\s-+\\([A-Za-z0-9_]+\\)\\s-*(")
              nil t)
        (make-button (match-beginning 1) (match-end 1)
                     'action       (lambda (_b) (dap-java-run-test-method))
                     'debug-action (lambda ()
                                     (dap-java-debug-test-method
                                      (dap--find-available-port)))
                     'help-echo    "click: run | C-S-click: debug | C-c j: run | C-c C-j: debug"
                     'face         '(:underline t :foreground "orange"))))))

(defun my/dap-java--setup-test-buffer ()
  "Set up inline test links and keybindings in *Test.java buffers."
  (my/dap-java--add-test-links)
  (when (and buffer-file-name
             (string-match-p "Test\\.java\\'" buffer-file-name))
    (local-set-key (kbd "C-c j")        #'my/dap-java-run-test-at-point)
    (local-set-key (kbd "C-c C-j")      #'my/dap-java-debug-test-at-point)
    (local-set-key (kbd "<C-S-mouse-1>") #'my/dap-java--ctrl-click-debug)))

(add-hook 'java-ts-mode-hook #'my/dap-java--setup-test-buffer)
(add-hook 'java-mode-hook    #'my/dap-java--setup-test-buffer)
#+end_src


***** Java environment bootstrap

~exec-path-from-shell~ uses a login shell (=-l= flag), which on Linux sources
=.zprofile= / =.profile= but NOT =.zshrc= / =.bashrc=. SDKMAN installs its
initialisation into =.zshrc= / =.bashrc=, so Emacs never picks up the
SDKMAN-managed Java from the login shell alone. The fix is to read the
=~/.sdkman/candidates/java/current= symlink directly -- this is always set by
=sdk use= / =sdk default= and requires no hardcoded version.

If SDKMAN is not installed the function is a no-op and Emacs falls back to
whatever Java is already on =exec-path=.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(with-eval-after-load 'lsp-java
  (defvar lsp-java-java-path)

  (defun my/bootstrap-sdkman-java ()
    "Bootstrap SDKMAN's current Java into Emacs's environment.
Sets JAVA_HOME, exec-path, PATH, and `lsp-java-java-path' from the
`~/.sdkman/candidates/java/current' symlink. No version is hardcoded.
IDempotent: skips PATH/JAVA_HOME patches when SDKMAN is already present.
Falls back silently to system Java when SDKMAN is not installed.
Call interactively after switching Java with `sdk use java X.X.X'
outside Emacs, then invoke `jal-lsp-java-switch-java-version' to
update LSP without restarting Emacs."
    (interactive)
    (let* ((sdkman-java-dir (expand-file-name "~/.sdkman/candidates/java/current"))
           (java-bin-dir    (expand-file-name "bin" sdkman-java-dir))
           (java-bin        (expand-file-name "java" java-bin-dir)))
      (cond
       ((file-executable-p java-bin)
        ;; Only patch JAVA_HOME/PATH if exec-path-from-shell did not already
        ;; pick up SDKMAN (idempotent: skip when JAVA_HOME already points there).
        (unless (string-prefix-p sdkman-java-dir (or (getenv "JAVA_HOME") ""))
          (setenv "JAVA_HOME" sdkman-java-dir)
          (setenv "PATH" (concat java-bin-dir path-separator (getenv "PATH")))
          (add-to-list 'exec-path java-bin-dir))
        ;; Pin lsp-java-java-path only when it has not already been set to a
        ;; non-SDKMAN-current path by the user (e.g. via my/java-switch-version).
        ;; This prevents re-loading the config from undoing a live version switch.
        (let ((current-path (bound-and-true-p lsp-java-java-path)))
          (when (or (null current-path)
                    (string= current-path "java")
                    ;; Still pointing at SDKMAN current: safe to update.
                    (string-prefix-p sdkman-java-dir current-path))
            (setq lsp-java-java-path java-bin)
            (message "SDKMAN Java bootstrapped: %s" java-bin))))
       ((file-directory-p (expand-file-name "~/.sdkman/candidates/java"))
        ;; SDKMAN is installed but no `current' version is selected.
        (message "SDKMAN found but no current Java selected; using system Java."))
       ;; SDKMAN not installed -- silent fallback to system Java.
       )))

  (my/bootstrap-sdkman-java))
#+end_src


***** LSP workspace cache and Java switch workflow

Since =lsp-java-configuration-runtimes= is already populated with all SDKMAN
versions (via =my/lsp-java-discover-runtimes=), there is no need to touch the
system SDKMAN default (=sdk use= / =sdk default=) when switching Java within
Emacs. The switch is Emacs-session-local only: it does not affect your shell,
other terminals, or other projects.

Set =my/java-clean-workspace-on-switch= to =nil= if you want to skip the
workspace cache deletion when switching Java versions (useful when staying on
the same major version and wanting a faster iteration cycle).

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(defvar my/java-clean-workspace-on-switch t
  "When non-nil, delete the LSP workspace cache on every Java version switch.
Set to nil to skip cache deletion when staying on the same major version.")

(defun my/java--candidate-from-runtime (runtime)
  "Return the java binary path from RUNTIME plist, or nil if not executable."
  (when-let ((home (plist-get runtime :path)))
    (let ((bin (expand-file-name "bin/java" home)))
      (when (file-executable-p bin) bin))))

(defun my/java--collect-candidates (current-path)
  "Return a deduplicated list of java executables to offer for selection.
Starts from CURRENT-PATH, adds system java if available, then all
runtimes from `lsp-java-configuration-runtimes'."
  (let* ((from-runtimes
          (delq nil
                (mapcar #'my/java--candidate-from-runtime
                        (append (bound-and-true-p lsp-java-configuration-runtimes) nil))))
         (system-java (executable-find "java"))
         (all (delete-dups
               (delq nil
                     (append (list current-path)
                             (when (and system-java
                                        (not (string= current-path system-java)))
                               (list system-java))
                             from-runtimes)))))
    all))

(defun my/java--sync-env (java-bin)
  "Patch JAVA_HOME, PATH, and exec-path in this Emacs session for JAVA-BIN.
Does not affect the shell or any system processes."
  (let* ((java-home (file-name-directory
                     (directory-file-name (file-name-directory java-bin))))
         (bin-dir   (expand-file-name "bin" java-home)))
    (setenv "JAVA_HOME" java-home)
    (setenv "PATH" (concat bin-dir path-separator (getenv "PATH")))
    (add-to-list 'exec-path bin-dir)))

(defun my/java--clean-workspace ()
  "Delete ~/.emacs.d/workspace/ unconditionally (no prompt).
Respects `my/java-clean-workspace-on-switch'."
  (when my/java-clean-workspace-on-switch
    (let ((workspace-dir (expand-file-name "workspace" user-emacs-directory)))
      (when (file-directory-p workspace-dir)
        (delete-directory workspace-dir t)
        (message "LSP workspace cache cleared.")))))

(defun my/java--apply-selection (chosen current-path)
  "Set `lsp-java-java-path' to CHOSEN and, if changed, sync env and restart LSP.
Also syncs JAVA_HOME/PATH/exec-path and deletes the workspace cache before
restarting, so jdtls starts fresh with the correct JDK."
  (defvar lsp-java-java-path)
  (setq lsp-java-java-path chosen)
  (if (string= chosen current-path)
      (message "Java version unchanged (%s)." chosen)
    (message "Switching to Java at '%s'..." chosen)
    (my/java--sync-env chosen)
    (my/java--clean-workspace)
    ;; jal--lsp-java-restart is still used so jal's vmargs/agent injection runs.
    (when (fboundp 'jal--lsp-java-restart)
      (jal--lsp-java-restart))))

(defun my/java-switch-version ()
  "Interactively switch the Java version for this Emacs session.
Picks from all versions in `lsp-java-configuration-runtimes'. Updates
JAVA_HOME, PATH, exec-path, and `lsp-java-java-path' session-locally.
Optionally cleans the LSP workspace cache before restarting jdtls,
controlled by `my/java-clean-workspace-on-switch'."
  (interactive)
  (require 'lsp-java)
  (defvar lsp-java-java-path)
  (let* ((current (or (bound-and-true-p lsp-java-java-path) "java"))
         (candidates (my/java--collect-candidates current)))
    (if (< (length candidates) 2)
        (message "No alternative Java versions found; only available: %s" current)
      (let ((chosen (completing-read
                     (format "Java version (current: %s): " current)
                     candidates nil t)))
        (my/java--apply-selection chosen current)))))

(defun my/java--detect-build-command (dir)
  "Return the build command for project in DIR, or nil if none is found.
Detects Maven (pom.xml) and Gradle (build.gradle / build.gradle.kts).
Returns nil and messages the user when no recognised build file exists."
  (cond
   ((file-exists-p (expand-file-name "pom.xml" dir))
    "mvn clean install -DskipTests")
   ((file-exists-p (expand-file-name "build.gradle" dir))
    "./gradlew clean build -x test")
   ((file-exists-p (expand-file-name "build.gradle.kts" dir))
    "./gradlew clean build -x test")
   (t
    (message "No pom.xml or build.gradle found in %s; skipping rebuild." dir)
    nil)))

(defun my/java-switch-and-rebuild (project-dir)
  "Switch Java, clean workspace, and rebuild PROJECT-DIR.
Calls `my/java-switch-version' (session-local, no SDKMAN system change),
auto-detects Maven or Gradle, and runs the appropriate build command with
the newly selected JAVA_HOME. Aborts the build step if no build file is found."
  (interactive
   (list (read-directory-name "Project directory: "
                              (or (and (fboundp 'lsp-workspace-root)
                                       (lsp-workspace-root))
                                  default-directory))))
  (my/java-switch-version)
  (let* ((project-dir   (expand-file-name project-dir))
         (build-command (my/java--detect-build-command project-dir)))
    (when build-command
      (let* ((java-bin  (bound-and-true-p lsp-java-java-path))
             (java-home (when java-bin
                          (file-name-directory
                           (directory-file-name (file-name-directory java-bin)))))
             (default-directory project-dir)
             (process-environment
              (if java-home
                  (cons (format "JAVA_HOME=%s" java-home) process-environment)
                process-environment)))
        (message "Rebuilding %s with: %s" project-dir build-command)
        (compilation-start build-command 'compilation-mode)))))
#+end_src


***** Java snippets

A collection of Yasnippet snippets for Java development.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package java-snippets
  :after yasnippet
  :hook
  (java-ts-mode . yas-minor-mode))
#+end_src


**** Kotlin

***** Kotlin Language Server (KLS) installation

To enable IDE features for Kotlin, you need to install the Kotlin Language Server
(KLS), which provides IDE features (like code completion, go-to-definition,
etc.) for the Language Server Protocol (LSP).

#+begin_src sh :tangle setup.sh
KLS_VERSION="1.3.13"
KLS_TARGET_DIR="$HOME/.emacs.d/lsp_language_servers/kotlin.ls"

is_installed_kls() {
  [ -f "$KLS_TARGET_DIR/bin/kotlin-language-server" ]
}

install_kls() {
  if ! is_installed_kls; then
    local KLS_TMP_DIR
    KLS_TMP_DIR=$(mktemp -d)

    rm -Rf "$KLS_TARGET_DIR" # Clean previous install
    mkdir -p "$KLS_TARGET_DIR"
    run_task "Downloading KLS" "curl -L https://github.com/fwcd/kotlin-language-server/releases/download/$KLS_VERSION/server.zip -o $KLS_TMP_DIR/kotlin-language-server.zip"
    run_task "Extracting KLS" "unzip -q $KLS_TMP_DIR/kotlin-language-server.zip -d $KLS_TMP_DIR && mv $KLS_TMP_DIR/server/* $KLS_TARGET_DIR/"

    rm -Rf "$KLS_TMP_DIR"
  fi
}

uninstall_kls() {
  if is_installed_kls; then
    run_task "Removing existing KLS installation" "rm -Rf${VERBOSE:+v} $KLS_TARGET_DIR"
  fi
}
#+end_src


***** Installing Kotlin

#+begin_src sh :tangle setup.sh
is_installed_kotlin() {
  return 1 # SDKMAN! will skip already installed versions
}

install_kotlin() {
  install_kls
  install_sdkman

  KOTLIN_VERSIONS_TO_INSTALL=(
    "2.3.10"
  )

  for v in "${KOTLIN_VERSIONS_TO_INSTALL[@]}"; do
    run_task "Kotlin $v" "sdk install kotlin $v > /dev/null 2>&1"
  done
}

uninstall_kotlin() {
  uninstall_kls
  log_skip "Kotlin SDKs (SDKMAN!)"
}

register_action "Install Kotlin" "kotlin" "jvm"
#+end_src


***** ~kotlin-mode~ for Emacs

Then, we can configure Emacs:

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
;; Copied from https://github.com/themkat/.emacs.d/blob/master/init.org
(declare-function dap-register-debug-template "dap-mode")
(defun my/kotlin-register-debug-templates ()
  "Register DAP debug templates for Kotlin."
  (dap-register-debug-template "Kotlin tests with launcher"
                            (list :type "kotlin"
                                  :request "launch"
                                  :mainClass "org.junit.platform.console.ConsoleLauncher --scan-class-path"
                                  :enableJsonLogging nil
                                  :noDebug nil)))

(defvar lsp-kotlin-debug-adapter-path nil)

(use-package kotlin-mode
  :defer t
  :after (lsp-mode dap-mode yasnippet)
  :config
  (require 'dap-kotlin)
  ;; Check comment from https://github.com/themkat/.emacs.d/blob/master/init.org
  ;; about that having to be in dap-kotlin instead of lsp-kotlin
  (setq lsp-kotlin-debug-adapter-path (or (executable-find "kotlin-debug-adapter") ""))
  (with-eval-after-load 'dap-mode
    (my/kotlin-register-debug-templates))
  :hook ((kotlin-mode . lsp-deferred)
         (kotlin-mode . yas-minor-mode)))

;; Register accepted extensions for workspace folder filtering
(add-to-list 'my/lsp-server-file-extensions '(kotlin-ls . ("kt" "kts")))
#+end_src


*** TypeScript

To enable IDE features for TypeScript, you need to install both the TypeScript
compiler (typescript) and the TypeScript Language Server (TSLS), and configure
Emacs to use them.

Why two packages? The typescript npm package provides the compiler and tsserver,
but not the Language Server Protocol (LSP). The typescript-language-server
package wraps tsserver and exposes its features via LSP, which is required for
lsp-mode and dap-mode in Emacs. This separation allows editors to Emacs. This
separation allows editors to use a standard protocol for IDE features across
many languages.

This repository contains a local setup for TypeScript tools under
=~/.emacs.d/nodejs_tools/= to ensure a consistent version is used regardless of
the project. However, if a project has its own local installation of
=typescript-language-server= in =node_modules/.bin/=, that version will be
preferred.

#+begin_src sh :tangle setup.sh
is_installed_typescript_language_server() {
  [ -f "$TOOLS_DIR/node_modules/.bin/typescript-language-server" ] || command -v typescript-language-server >/dev/null 2>&1
}

install_typescript_language_server() {
  install_nodejs_tools
}

uninstall_typescript_language_server() {
  uninstall_nodejs_tools
}

register_action "Install TypeScript Language Server" "typescript-language-server" "node"
#+end_src

Note that you need to install [[https://nodejs.org/][Node.js]] before proceeding.
Then, we can configure Emacs. The setup below enables TypeScript mode using
treesit (~*-ts-mode~):

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
;; Register accepted extensions for workspace folder filtering
(dolist (entry '((eslint        . ("ts" "tsx" "js" "jsx" "html" "vue"))
                 (eslint-remote . ("ts" "tsx" "js" "jsx" "html" "vue"))))
  (add-to-list 'my/lsp-server-file-extensions entry))

(defun my/typescript-register-debug-templates ()
  "Register DAP debug templates for TypeScript."
  (require 'dap-mode)

  (with-eval-after-load 'dap-mode
    (dap-register-debug-template
     "Node::Attach"
     (list :type "node"
           :request "attach"
           :name "Node::Attach"
           :port 9229
           :address "localhost"
           :restart t
           :protocol "inspector")))

  (with-eval-after-load 'dap-mode
    (dap-register-debug-template
     "Node::Launch Program"
     (list :type "node"
           :request "launch"
           :name "Node::Launch Program"
           :program "${file}"
           :cwd "${workspaceFolder}"
           :runtimeExecutable "node"
           :protocol "inspector"
           :console "integratedTerminal"
           :internalConsoleOptions "neverOpen"))))

(with-eval-after-load 'dap-mode
  (my/typescript-register-debug-templates))

(defvar lsp-typescript-server-path nil)

(use-package typescript-ts-mode
  :defer t
  :mode "\\.tsx?\\'"
  :after (lsp-mode dap-mode yasnippet)
  :config
  ;; Ensure dap-js is loaded for TypeScript/Node.js debugging
  (require 'dap-js)

  ;; Prefer project-local typescript-language-server, fallback to ~/.emacs.d/nodejs_tools.
  ;; Optimization: The remote-TSLS path is now handled by connection-local variables.
  (if (file-remote-p (or (buffer-file-name) default-directory))
      (setq lsp-typescript-server-path "typescript-language-server")
    (let* ((project-root (or (locate-dominating-file (or (buffer-file-name) default-directory) "node_modules") (or (buffer-file-name) default-directory)))
           (project-tsls (expand-file-name "node_modules/.bin/typescript-language-server" project-root))
           (fallback-tsls (executable-find "typescript-language-server")))
      (setq lsp-typescript-server-path (if (file-exists-p project-tsls) project-tsls fallback-tsls))))
  :hook ((typescript-ts-mode . lsp-deferred)
          (typescript-ts-mode . yas-minor-mode)))

;; Register accepted extensions for workspace folder filtering
(add-to-list 'my/lsp-server-file-extensions '(ts-ls . ("ts" "tsx" "js" "jsx" "mjs" "cjs")))

;; Associate tsx and ts files with the correct modes
(add-to-list 'auto-mode-alist '("\\.tsx\\'" . tsx-ts-mode))
(add-to-list 'auto-mode-alist '("\\.ts\\'" . typescript-ts-mode))
#+end_src
