*** General Node.js development setup

Node.js REPL integration with ~nodejs-repl~ package. This provides an interactive
Node.js shell within Emacs, allowing you to run JavaScript code snippets and
test functions on the fly.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package nodejs-repl
  :commands nodejs-repl)

;; Ensure dap-js is loaded for TypeScript/Node.js debugging
(with-eval-after-load 'dap-mode
  (require 'dap-js)
  (setq-default dap-js-debug-path (expand-file-name "vscode-js-debug" "~/.emacs.d/nodejs_tools/")))
#+end_src


In addition to that, here goes a setup for Flycheck to use eslint from either
Yarn or npm, preferring Yarn if both are available in the project. This affects
all Node.js projects.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(defun my/flycheck-use-eslint ()
  "Configure Flycheck to use eslint via Yarn or npm, preferring Yarn."
  (when-let ((root (locate-dominating-file
                    (or buffer-file-name default-directory)
                    "package.json")))
    (cond
     ((file-exists-p (expand-file-name "yarn.lock" root))
      (setq-local flycheck-javascript-eslint-executable "yarn")
      (setq-local flycheck-javascript-eslint-args '("eslint" "--format" "unix" "--stdin")))
     (t
      (setq-local flycheck-javascript-eslint-executable "npm")
      (setq-local flycheck-javascript-eslint-args '("run" "eslint" "--"))))))

(add-hook 'flycheck-mode-hook #'my/flycheck-use-eslint)
#+end_src


*** General Web development (HTML, CSS)

We start by adding CSS/SASS inline documentation with eldoc.

Eldoc is a built-in Emacs minor mode that displays function signatures, variable
documentation, or other context-sensitive help in the minibuffer as you type or
move the cursor. For CSS and SASS, the ~css-eldoc~ package integrates with eldoc
to show documentation for CSS properties and functions inline, making it much
easier to remember valid values and arguments without leaving your editor.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package css-eldoc
  :hook
  (css-mode . turn-on-css-eldoc)
  (scss-mode . turn-on-css-eldoc))
#+end_src

Then, we setup web-mode for web templates:

~web-mode~ is a major mode for editing web templates and component files, such as
HTML, Vue, EJS, Nunjucks, Jinja, Twig, and ERB. It provides syntax highlighting,
indentation, and structural editing for these formats.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package web-mode
  :defer t
  :after (css-eldoc)
  :mode ("\\.html?\\'" "\\.ejs\\'" "\\.vue\\'" "\\.njk\\'" "\\.jinja\\'" "\\.twig\\'" "\\.erb\\'")
  :custom
  (web-mode-enable-current-element-highlight t))
#+end_src


*** Advanced shell development

To enable advanced shell scripting development in Emacs, we enable IDE features
(completion, go-to-definition, linting) through ~lsp-mode~,
~bash-language-server~ and ~company-shell~ for shell completion.

#+begin_src sh :tangle setup.sh
LSP_BASH_LANGUAGE_SERVER_PATH="$HOME/.emacs.d/.cache/lsp/npm/bash-language-server"

is_installed_bash_language_server() {
  [ -f "$TOOLS_DIR/node_modules/.bin/bash-language-server" ] || command -v bash-language-server >/dev/null 2>&1
}

install_bash_language_server() {
  install_nodejs_tools
  # Small hack for bash-language-server to work with lsp-mode: setting `lsp-bash-bash-language-server-path`
  # did not work as expected for unknown reasons.
  run_task "Linking Bash LS" "rm -Rf $LSP_BASH_LANGUAGE_SERVER_PATH && mkdir -p $LSP_BASH_LANGUAGE_SERVER_PATH/bin/ && ln -s $TOOLS_DIR/node_modules/.bin/bash-language-server $LSP_BASH_LANGUAGE_SERVER_PATH/bin/bash-language-server"
}

uninstall_bash_language_server() {
  uninstall_nodejs_tools
  run_task "Cleaning Bash LS cache" "rm -Rf $LSP_BASH_LANGUAGE_SERVER_PATH"
}

register_action "Install Bash Language Server" "bash-language-server" "node"
#+end_src

We also install =shellcheck= a static analysis tool that automatically scans
your shell scripts to identify syntax errors, common pitfalls, and subtle logic
bugs.

#+begin_src sh :tangle setup.sh
is_installed_shellcheck() {
  command -v shellcheck >/dev/null 2>&1
}

register_system_package "ShellCheck" \
                        "alpine:shellcheck" \
                        "fedora:shellcheck" \
                        "linuxbrew:shellcheck" \
                        "mac:shellcheck" \
                        "debian-ubuntu:shellcheck"

install_shellcheck() {
  install_system_packages "ShellCheck"
}

uninstall_shellcheck() {
  log_skip "ShellCheck" "ShellCheck is a system package and will not be uninstalled automatically"
}

register_action "Install ShellCheck" "shellcheck" "standalone"
#+end_src

Finally, we setup the Emacs packages:

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package sh-mode
  :ensure nil
  :mode ("\\.sh\\'" . sh-mode)
  :hook ((sh-mode . lsp-deferred)
         (sh-mode . company-mode)
         (sh-mode . flycheck-mode)))

(use-package company-shell
  :after company
  :defer t
  :hook (sh-mode . (lambda ()
                     (add-to-list (make-local-variable 'company-backends) 'company-shell))))

(use-package bash-completion
  :defer t
  :hook (shell-mode . bash-completion-mode))
#+end_src

This setup provides a modern shell scripting experience in Emacs with LSP features, completion, and linting.


*** Build Systems (Makefile & Autotools)

**** Makefile Configuration

***** Basic configuration

Makefiles are scripts used by the =make= tool to automate software builds. They
have specific requirements: all recipe lines must be indented with a tab
character by default, not spaces, for =make= to work correctly. This section
provides Emacs settings to enforce tab indentation in Makefiles and tips for
handling these unique editing constraints efficiently.

*IMPORTANT:* EditorConfig might override this setting! If your =.editorconfig=
file explicitly sets ~indent_style = space~ for Makefiles, it will force spaces
even if you configure Emacs to use tabs. To guarantee tab indentation, ensure
your =.editorconfig= uses ~indent_style = tab~ for ~[Makefile]~ or remove
conflicting settings.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(add-hook 'makefile-mode-hook
          (lambda () (setq indent-tabs-mode t)))

(add-hook 'makefile-gmake-mode-hook
          (lambda () (setq indent-tabs-mode t)))
#+end_src

We also need to configure LSP to support it properly:

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(with-eval-after-load 'lsp-mode
  (add-to-list 'lsp-language-id-configuration '(makefile-gmake-mode . "makefile")))
#+end_src


***** Smart compilation

Below we add a "smart compile" to Emacs based on Makefile when they are
available:

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(defun my/smart-compile (&optional debug-p)
  "Build the project using a Makefile if found, otherwise prompt for a command.
If DEBUG-P is non-nil (e.g., via `\\[universal-argument]'),we validate that
the Makefile contains the `DEBUG' variable, as well as `clean' and `build'
targets before attempting a debug build. If any are missing, it alerts the user
and falls back to a manual prompt. Standard builds (non-debug) proceed with
`make -k' if a Makefile exists."
  (interactive "P")
  (let* ((m-file-dir (locate-dominating-file default-directory "Makefile"))
         (m-file (when m-file-dir (expand-file-name "Makefile" m-file-dir)))
         (has-makefile (and m-file (file-exists-p m-file))))
    (cond
     ;; Debug requested. Perform the triple-check (Variable + Targets):
     ((and debug-p has-makefile)
      (let ((requirements-met
             (with-temp-buffer
               (insert-file-contents m-file)
               (let ((has-var (re-search-forward "^[ \t]*DEBUG[ \t]*[?:]?=" nil t))
                     (has-clean (progn (goto-char (point-min))
                                       (re-search-forward "^clean[ \t]*:" nil t)))
                     (has-build (progn (goto-char (point-min))
                                       (re-search-forward "^build[ \t]*:" nil t))))
                 (and has-var has-clean has-build)))))
        (if requirements-met
            (let ((default-directory m-file-dir))
              (compile "make -k clean build DEBUG=1"))
          (message "One or more of these are missing in Makefile: DEBUG variable, clean target, or build target. Falling back to manual compile...")
          (sit-for 1.5)
          (call-interactively 'compile))))
     ;; Standard build (No prefix arg) with a Makefile:
     ((and (not debug-p) has-makefile)
      (let ((default-directory m-file-dir))
        (compile "make -k")))
     ;; Fallback (No Makefile found or other cases):
     (t (call-interactively 'compile)))))

(add-hook 'prog-mode-hook
          (lambda ()
            (local-set-key (kbd "M-<f4>") 'compile)
            (local-set-key (kbd "C-<f4>") (lambda () (interactive) (my/smart-compile t)))
            (local-set-key (kbd "<f4>") 'my/smart-compile)))
#+end_src


***** Makefile executor

The =makefile-executor= package provides a convenient minibuffer interface for
discovering and running targets. To make it more project-aware, I've added a
wrapper that climbs the directory tree to find the nearest Makefile, allowing it
to be triggered from any source file within a project.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package makefile-executor
  :config
  (add-hook 'makefile-mode-hook 'makefile-executor-mode)

  (defun my/makefile-executor-smart-run ()
    "Find the closest Makefile and run an executor command."
    (interactive)
    (let ((makefile (locate-dominating-file default-directory "Makefile")))
      (if makefile
          (let ((default-directory makefile))
            (makefile-executor-execute-project-target))
        (message "No Makefile found in the current hierarchy."))))

  (global-set-key (kbd "C-c c m") 'my/makefile-executor-smart-run))
#+end_src


**** Autotools (Autoconf/Automake) Support

For projects using the GNU Autotools suite (=configure.ac=, =Makefile.am=), we
use the ~autotools-language-server~. This provides navigation, diagnostics, and
autocompletion. We install this via pipx to keep your global environment clean.

We install it here via =pipx= (a tool to manage Python applications in isolated
environments) and use it via the Language Server Protocol (LSP).

#+begin_src sh :tangle setup.sh
is_installed_autotools_language_server() {
  command -v autotools-language-server >/dev/null 2>&1
}

install_autotools_language_server() {
  install_via_pipx "Autotools Language Server" "autotools-language-server"
}

uninstall_autotools_language_server() {
  run_task "Uninstalling Autotools LS" "pipx uninstall autotools-language-server 2>/dev/null || true"
}

register_action "Install Autotools Language Server" "autotools-language-server" "standalone"
#+end_src


*** YAML, JSON and TypeScript

YAML and JSON files are used for configuration in many tools and languages.
The ~yaml-ts-mode~ package provides syntax highlighting and indentation for
YAML files.

We additionally set up the language id configuration for JSON, TypeScript,
TSX, and JavaScript:

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(add-to-list 'auto-mode-alist '("\\.json\\'" . json-ts-mode))

(with-eval-after-load 'lsp-mode
  (add-to-list 'lsp-language-id-configuration '(yaml-ts-mode . "yaml"))
  (add-to-list 'lsp-language-id-configuration '(json-ts-mode . "json"))
  (add-to-list 'lsp-language-id-configuration '(typescript-ts-mode . "typescript"))
  (add-to-list 'lsp-language-id-configuration '(tsx-ts-mode . "typescriptreact"))
  (add-to-list 'lsp-language-id-configuration '(js-ts-mode . "javascript"))
  ;; Enable LSP support for YAML gives real-time schema validation, smart
  ;; completion, hover help, and automatic formatting:
  (add-hook 'yaml-ts-mode-hook #'lsp-deferred))

(use-package yaml-ts-mode
  :mode "\\.ya?ml\\'"
  :config
  (setq treesit-font-lock-level 4)
  (unless (treesit-language-available-p 'yaml)
    (treesit-install-language-grammar 'yaml)))
#+end_src

~yaml-pro~ provides a specialized toolkit for structural refactoring and advanced
visibility. It allows you to treat YAML nodes as physical objects, enabling you
to swap entire nested blocks up or down without breaking indentation. Beyond
movement, it offers "tree convolution" for restructuring hierarchies
and adds specific highlighting for advanced YAML features like anchors and
tags, ensuring that the logical structure of your configuration is always
transparent and easy to manipulate safely.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package yaml-pro
  :after yaml-ts-mode
  :hook ((yaml-ts-mode . yaml-pro-mode)
         (yaml-ts-mode . outline-minor-mode))
  :preface
  (defun my/yaml-pro-toggle-feature (var name)
    (set (make-local-variable var) (not (symbol-value var)))
    (font-lock-flush)
    (font-lock-ensure)
    (message "YAML %s highlighting: %s" name (if (symbol-value var) "Enabled" "Disabled")))

  :config
  (defvar yaml-pro-set-colors)
  (declare-function yaml-pro-move-up "yaml-pro")
  (declare-function yaml-pro-move-down "yaml-pro")
  (declare-function yaml-pro-up-level "yaml-pro")
  (declare-function yaml-pro-down-level "yaml-pro")
  (declare-function yaml-pro-convolute-tree "yaml-pro")
  (setq yaml-pro-set-colors t)

  (let ((map custom-bindings-map))
    ;; Structural Movement
    (define-key map (kbd "C-c v k") #'yaml-pro-move-up)
    (define-key map (kbd "C-c v j") #'yaml-pro-move-down)
    (define-key map (kbd "C-c v h") #'yaml-pro-up-level)
    (define-key map (kbd "C-c v l") #'yaml-pro-down-level)

    ;; Folding & Magic
    (define-key map (kbd "C-c v c") #'yaml-pro-convolute-tree)

    ;; Toggles
    (define-key map (kbd "C-c v a") (lambda () (interactive) (my/yaml-pro-toggle-feature 'yaml-pro-highlight-anchors "Anchor")))
    (define-key map (kbd "C-c v t") (lambda () (interactive) (my/yaml-pro-toggle-feature 'yaml-pro-highlight-tags "Tag")))))
#+end_src

Finally, here are practical schema configurations for ~lsp-yaml~ in Emacs,
covering well-known YAML use cases. Other schemas might be detected
automatically:

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(with-eval-after-load 'lsp-yaml
  (defvar lsp-yaml-schemas)
  (setq lsp-yaml-schemas
        '(:https://raw.githubusercontent.com/compose-spec/compose-spec/master/schema/compose-spec.json
          ["**/docker-compose*.ya*ml"]

          :https://raw.githubusercontent.com/yannh/kubernetes-json-schema/master/v1.30.3-standalone-strict/all.json
          ["*-k8s.yaml" "*-k8s.yml" "**/k8s/*.ya*ml"])))
#+end_src


*** XML

~nxml-mode~ is the classic Emacs XML mode and is not a tree-sitter (-ts-) major
mode. That means buffers visiting XML with ~nxml-mode~ do not automatically get
a tree-sitter parser attached. Tree-sitter features require either a
treesit-backed major mode (which does not exist for XML at the moment) or tools
that explicitly use the installed grammar regardless of major mode.

The snippet below enables code folding in ~nxml-mode~ by configuring the built-in ~hs-minor-mode~ (Hide/Show minor
mode) for XML syntax. By default, ~hs-minor-mode~ does not support XML or SGML
out of the box. This adaptation lets you fold and unfold XML sections, making it
easier to work with large, deeply nested XML files.

The configuration works by adding XML-specific patterns to the
=hs-special-modes-alist=, which tells ~hs-minor-mode~ how to recognize the start
and end of foldable blocks in XML. It also sets up a hook so that whenever you
enter ~nxml-mode~, ~hs-minor-mode~ is automatically enabled. Furthermore, it
customizes the key binding =C-<tab>= to toggle code folding-but if
~treesit-fold~ is not currently active, to prevent conflicts.

This solution was adapted from the approach discussed on Emacs Stack Exchange:
https://emacs.stackexchange.com/questions/2884/the-old-how-to-fold-xml-question

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(require 'sgml-mode)

(add-to-list 'hs-special-modes-alist
              '(nxml-mode
                "<!--\\|<[^/>]*[^/]>"
                "-->\\|</[^/>]*[^/]>"
                "<!--"
                sgml-skip-tag-forward
                nil))

(add-hook 'nxml-mode-hook
          (lambda ()
            (hs-minor-mode 1)
            ;; Bind C-<tab> to hs toggle only when noxml-fold or treesit-fold
            ;; are not active to avoid conflicts with those handlers.
            (unless (bound-and-true-p treesit-fold-mode)
              (declare-function hs-toggle-hiding "hideshow")
              (local-set-key (kbd "C-<tab>") #'hs-toggle-hiding))))
#+end_src


*** Markdown

Adding support for editing Markdown files with ~markdown-mode~.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package markdown-mode
  :defer t
  :hook (markdown-mode . (lambda () (setq fill-column 72))))
#+end_src


** EditorConfig

Using [[https://editorconfig.org/][EditorConfig]] is a must when collaborating with others. It is also a way
of having multiple tools that want to format your buffer to agree (e.g. both
the language's Emacs mode and some external formatter/prettifier).


#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package editorconfig
  :config
  ;; Ensure JSON modes respect EditorConfig indentation
  (add-to-list 'editorconfig-indentation-alist '(json-mode json-indent-level))
  (add-to-list 'editorconfig-indentation-alist '(json-ts-mode json-ts-mode-indent-level))
  (add-to-list 'editorconfig-indentation-alist '(js-json-mode js-indent-level))

  (editorconfig-mode 1))
#+end_src


** Dotenv

Dotenv is a module that loads environment variables from a =.env= file into
=process.env=, allowing you to keep configuration secrets decoupled from your
codebase. To accommodate different deployment stages, we can extend this
practice using environment-specific files such as =.env.development=,
=.env.test=, or =.env.production=.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package dotenv-mode
  :mode (("\\.env\\(?:\\..*\\)?\\'" . dotenv-mode)))

'dotenv-mode
(add-hook 'dotenv-mode-hook
          (lambda ()
            (declare-function lsp-mode "lsp-mode")
            (lsp-mode -1)
            (font-lock-add-keywords nil
                                    '(("\\(^\\|\\s-\\)\\([A-Z0-9_]+\\)\\s-*="
                                       (2 font-lock-variable-name-face))
                                      ("\\(?:=\\)\\s-*\\([^#\n]+\\)"
                                       (1 font-lock-string-face))))))
#+end_src


** PlantUML

[[https://plantuml.com/][PlantUML]] is a tool for creating UML diagrams from a simple text description.
The ~plantuml-mode~ package provides syntax highlighting, autocompletion, and preview
capabilities for PlantUML diagrams within Emacs.

*** Installation

To use ~plantuml-mode~, you need to have PlantUML installed. The setup script
downloads the PlantUML JAR file to =~/.emacs.d/plantuml/plantuml.jar=.

#+begin_src sh :tangle setup.sh
# Update PlantUML version as needed:
PLANTUML_VERSION="1.2026.2"
PLANTUML_DIR="$HOME/.emacs.d/plantuml"

is_installed_plantuml() {
  [ -f "$HOME/.emacs.d/plantuml/plantuml.jar" ]
}

install_plantuml() {
  local PLANTUML_JAR="$PLANTUML_DIR/plantuml.jar"
  local PLANTUML_URL="https://github.com/plantuml/plantuml/releases/download/v${PLANTUML_VERSION}/plantuml-${PLANTUML_VERSION}.jar"

  if [ -f "$PLANTUML_JAR" ]; then
    log_skip "PlantUML" "EXISTS"
  else
    run_task "Downloading PlantUML" "mkdir -p $PLANTUML_DIR && curl -L $PLANTUML_URL -o $PLANTUML_JAR"
  fi
}

uninstall_plantuml() {
  run_task "Removing PlantUML" "rm -Rf $PLANTUML_DIR"
}

register_action "Install PlantUML" "plantuml" "standalone"
#+end_src


*** Configuration

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package plantuml-mode
  :mode (("\\.wsd\\'" . plantuml-mode)
         ("\\.pu\\'" . plantuml-mode)
         ("\\.puml\\'" . plantuml-mode)
         ("\\.plantuml\\'" . plantuml-mode)
         ("\\.iuml\\'" . plantuml-mode))
  :custom
  (plantuml-jar-path (expand-file-name "~/.emacs.d/plantuml/plantuml.jar"))
  (plantuml-default-exec-mode 'jar)
  (plantuml-output-type "svg")
  (plantuml-indent-level 2)
  :config
  ;; Enable PlantUML integration with org-mode
  (with-eval-after-load 'org
    (add-to-list 'org-src-lang-modes '("plantuml" . plantuml))))

(use-package flycheck-plantuml
  :after flycheck
  :config
  (flycheck-plantuml-setup))
#+end_src


*** Usage

- Open or create a file with extensions =.wsd=, =.pu=, =.puml=, =.plantuml=, or =.iuml=
- Write your PlantUML diagram
- Preview with =C-c C-c= (~plantuml-preview~)
- When editing PlantUML code blocks in org-mode, use =C-c '= to edit in ~plantuml-mode~


** Docker and related

*** Dockerfile and docker-compose

Dockerfile support is provided by the ~dockerfile-mode~ package, which enables
syntax highlighting and editing for Dockerfiles. For Docker Compose YAML files,
we do not use the available ~docker-compose-mode~ because it cannot be used
together with the ~yaml-ts-mode~ (they are both major modes).

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package dockerfile-mode
  :mode "Dockerfile\\'")
#+end_src


*** Development containers

[[https://github.com/johannes-mueller/devcontainer.el][devcontainer.el]] provides support for managing and working with [[https://containers.dev/][Development
containers (devcontainers)]] in Emacs. Devcontainers allow you to develop inside
Docker containers, ensuring consistent development environments across different
machines.

The package provides commands to build, launch, stop, and manage devcontainer,
and automatically forwards =compile= commands into the devcontainer so builds
and tests run inside the container environment.

**** Prerequisites:

To prepare our environment, we need the following:

+ [[https://github.com/devcontainers/cli][devcontainers/cli]]: The official command-line interface for the Dev Containers
  specification. It allows you to build, run, and manage development container
  environments defined by a =devcontainer.json= file independently of the IDE
  extension. It is already added to the =nodejs_tools= in this folder, but it
  requires you to run =npm install=.

+ =Container Runtime= - Have a functioning installation of either Docker or Podman.

+ =Docker Compose= - Required only if your Dev Containers configuration relies on it.


**** Configuration:

The devcontainer.el package is configured as follows:

- =Global Mode= - The ~devcontainer-mode~ is enabled globally to automatically
  intercept and forward compilation commands to the active container.
- =Backend Engine= - The ~devcontainer-container-engine~ can be toggled between
  ~'docker~ (default) or ~'podman~, depending on your environment.
- =Host Execution= - Programs listed in ~devcontainer-execute-outside-container~
  (e.g., =grep=) run directly on the host to avoid container latency and improve
  performance.
- =CLI Integration= - The package automatically detects and utilizes the local
  Dev Containers CLI from =nodejs_tools/=, ensuring portability without requiring
  global Node.js installations.

When ~devcontainer-mode~ is active, all =compile= commands are automatically forwarded
into the devcontainer. Your local working directory is typically mounted into the
container, so you edit files normally, but builds and tests run inside the container.

Alternatively, you can use TRAMP to edit files directly inside the container using
=M-x devcontainer-tramp-dired=.

All interactive functions use the =devcontainer-= prefix:

+ =M-x devcontainer-up= - Start the devcontainer for the current project
+ =M-x devcontainer-restart= - Stop and restart the devcontainer
+ =M-x devcontainer-rebuild-and-restart= - Rebuild and restart (useful after Dockerfile changes)
+ =M-x devcontainer-execute-command= - Execute an arbitrary command inside the container
+ =M-x devcontainer-term= - Launch a terminal inside the container

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package devcontainer
  :config
  ;; Utilize the Dev Containers CLI from our PATH
  (let ((devcontainer-cli-path (executable-find "devcontainer")))
    (when devcontainer-cli-path
      (add-to-list 'exec-path (file-name-directory devcontainer-cli-path))))

  (devcontainer-mode 1)
  (unless (boundp 'devcontainer-engine)
    (setq devcontainer-engine 'docker))

  (setq devcontainer-execute-outside-container '("grep" "rg" "ripgrep"))
  (setq devcontainer-term-environment '(("TERM" . "xterm-256color")))

  ;; Optional: Hook to open dired inside container after startup
  (add-hook 'devcontainer-post-startup-hook 'devcontainer-tramp-dired)

  (declare-function devcontainer--read-devcontainer-json "devcontainer")
  (defun my/get-devcontainer-shell ()
    "Searches for a shell preference in devcontainer.json.
Falls back to VS Code settings if a shell is not found."
    (condition-case nil
        (when-let* ((config (devcontainer--read-devcontainer-json))
                    (customizations (gethash "customizations" config)))
          (or
           ;; 1. Check for Emacs-specific shell setting
           (when-let ((emacs (gethash "emacs" customizations)))
             (gethash "shell" emacs))
           ;; 2. Fallback to VS Code default profile
           (when-let ((vscode (gethash "vscode" customizations)))
             (when-let ((settings (gethash "settings" vscode)))
               (gethash "terminal.integrated.defaultProfile.linux" settings)))))
      (error nil)))

  (declare-function my/get-devcontainer-shell nil)
  (defun my/vterm-use-devcontainer-shell-advice (orig-fun &rest args)
    "Advice for `vterm' to use `devcontainer-term-shell' when in a container.
Applies ORIG-FUN with ARGS."
    (let ((vterm-shell (if (and (bound-and-true-p devcontainer-mode)
                                (tramp-tramp-file-p default-directory)
                                (let ((method (tramp-file-name-method (tramp-dissect-file-name default-directory))))
                                  (member method (list "docker" "podman"))))
                           (or (my/get-devcontainer-shell)
                               (bound-and-true-p devcontainer-term-shell)
                               vterm-shell)
                         vterm-shell)))
      (apply orig-fun args)))

  (with-eval-after-load 'vterm
    (advice-add 'vterm :around 'my/vterm-use-devcontainer-shell-advice))

  ;; Optimization: Skip customizations on remote files to prevent TRAMP freeze
  (defun my/devcontainer-skip-remote-customization-advice (orig-fun &rest args)
    "Skip `devcontainer--read-and-apply-customizations' for remote files.
Applies ORIG-FUN with ARGS."
    (unless (file-remote-p (or (buffer-file-name) default-directory))
      (apply orig-fun args)))

  (advice-add 'devcontainer--read-and-apply-customizations :around 'my/devcontainer-skip-remote-customization-advice))
#+end_src


**** Flycheck limitations

Running Flycheck within TRAMP currently involves a few known friction points. The technical challenges and specific limitations are well-documented in the following pull requests, and the maintainers are planning to address these through a future redesign of the system:

- [[https://github.com/flycheck/flycheck/pull/1842][Flycheck PR #1842]]
- [[https://github.com/flycheck/flycheck/pull/1922][Flycheck PR #1922]]

For now, we are skipping Flycheck in this scenario.


**** Running tools inside devcontainer

To make tools like flycheck, linters, and other executables run inside the devcontainer,
we configure them to use devcontainer's command forwarding. This ensures that tools
running in your project use the container's environment and dependencies.


**** LSP integration with devcontainer/TRAMP:

LSP servers run inside the container when using TRAMP to access files. The
container should have Node.js and language server extensions installed.


* Project management

Project management in Emacs is handled by the built-in ~project~ package, which provides
a unified interface for working with projects. To enhance the project management experience,
we can integrate Magit, a popular Git interface for Emacs, into the project switch commands

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package project
  :preface
  (defun my/treemacs-sync-with-project ()
    "Restrict Treemacs to display only the current project.
Skips TRAMP paths as treemacs cannot reliably read remote paths."
    (with-eval-after-load 'treemacs
      (when (and (project-current) (treemacs-get-local-window))
        (let ((project-root (project-root (project-current))))
          ;; Skip TRAMP paths - treemacs cannot reliably read remote paths
          (unless (file-remote-p project-root)
            (save-selected-window
              (save-current-buffer
                (treemacs-add-and-display-current-project-exclusively))))))))

  :config
  (add-to-list 'project-switch-commands '(magit-project-status "Magit" ?m))

  ;; If running as a daemon, prevent 'project.el' from asking for a project root.
  ;; It should only prompt if run interactively (i.e., when you open emacsclient).
  (when (daemonp)
    (setq-default project-find-project-by-default nil))

  ;; Ignoring temporary/emacs files in projects
  (add-to-list 'project-vc-ignores "*~")
  (add-to-list 'project-vc-ignores ".#*~")
  (add-to-list 'project-vc-ignores "#*")
  (add-to-list 'project-vc-ignores ".#*")

  (add-hook 'emacs-startup-hook #'my/treemacs-sync-with-project)
  (add-hook 'find-file-hook #'my/treemacs-sync-with-project)
  (add-hook 'project-switch-functions #'my/treemacs-sync-with-project))
#+end_src


* Window management

Added keybindings using Alt Gr and the numeric keyboard.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(use-package windmove
  :ensure nil
  :bind (:map custom-bindings-map
              ("M-<left>" . windmove-left)
              ("M-<down>" . windmove-down)
              ("M-<up>" . windmove-up)
              ("M-<right>" . windmove-right)

              ("M-S-<left>" . windmove-swap-states-left)
              ("M-S-<down>" . windmove-swap-states-down)
              ("M-S-<up>" . windmove-swap-states-up)
              ("M-S-<right>" . windmove-swap-states-right)))
#+end_src


* PDF Tools

[[https://github.com/vedang/pdf-tools][PDF Tools]] makes a huge improvement on the built-in [[http://www.gnu.org/software/emacs/manual/html_node/emacs/Document-View.html][doc-view-mode]]! Removing
the =header-line-format= gives a very clean PDF-viewer; let's add that to a
key.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
;; Preventing compile-time warning
(declare-function pdf-loader-install "pdf-tools" nil)
(declare-function pdf-view-desired-image-size "pdf-view")
(declare-function pdf-view-create-page "pdf-view")
(declare-function pdf-util-frame-scale-factor "pdf-util")
(declare-function pdf-util-hexcolor "pdf-util")
(declare-function pdf-view-buffer-file-name "pdf-view")
(declare-function pdf-util-munch-file "pdf-util")
(declare-function pdf-util-highlight-regexp-in-string "pdf-util")
(declare-function image-mode-window-get "image-mode")
(declare-function image-set-window-hscroll "image-mode")
(declare-function image-set-window-vscroll "image-mode")

;; Emacs support library for PDF files
(use-package pdf-tools
  :defer t
  :mode "\\.pdf\\'"
  :bind (:map pdf-view-mode-map
              ("c" . (lambda ()
                       (interactive)
                       (if header-line-format
                           (setq header-line-format nil)
                         )))
              ("j" . pdf-view-next-line-or-next-page)
              ("k" . pdf-view-previous-line-or-previous-page))
  :config
  (unless (daemonp)
    (pdf-loader-install))
  (add-to-list 'revert-without-query ".pdf"))
#+end_src


* Other user functions

** Autosave hooks

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(defun my/trim-whitespace ()
  "Trim trailing whitespace before saving."
  (when (derived-mode-p
    'conf-unix-mode
    'markdown-mode
    'nxml-mode
    'org-mode
    'prog-mode
    'text-mode
    'yaml-ts-mode); only certain modes
    (delete-trailing-whitespace)))

(defvar my/save-hooks
  '(my/trim-whitespace
    ;; more functions here
    )
  "List of functions to run before saving a file.")

(defun my/run-save-hooks ()
  "Run all functions in `my/save-hooks'."
  (run-hooks 'my/save-hooks))

(add-hook 'before-save-hook #'my/run-save-hooks)
#+end_src


** Yarn integration

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(defun yarn-run-script (script)
  "Run SCRIPT with Yarn in the project root."
  (interactive "sYarn script: ")
  (compile (concat "yarn " script)))

(global-set-key (kbd "C-c y") 'yarn-run-script)

;; To load local variables, run:

* TRAMP debugging

If you experience slowdowns, lockups, or unexpected behavior when using TRAMP
(Transparent Remote Access, Multiple Protocols), the problem may be caused by
custom functions, third-party packages, or complex configurations interacting
with remote files.

Enabling verbose TRAMP logging can help you pinpoint the source of these issues.
With high verbosity, all TRAMP operations and protocol dialogs will be recorded
in detail. Review the output in the *tramp/debug* buffer to identify problematic
commands, bottlenecks, or failing operations.

Notes:
- Logging is disabled by default to avoid performance overhead.
- Only enable when actively debugging TRAMP issues.
- Remember to disable (or lower) verbosity after troubleshooting.

#+begin_src emacs-lisp :tangle early-init.el
;; Uncomment to enable verbose TRAMP debugging:
;; (setq tramp-verbose 10)
#+end_src

display_installed_tools

** Configuration End Markers (Lisp Integrity)

These lines mark the end of each configuration file.
When Emacs loads .el files, it reads Lisp code line by line. If the file ends
abruptly (for example, with an open parenthesis or unfinished expression),
Emacs will stop with an error such as "End of file during parsing."

Including a clear end marker like this helps avoid confusion and ensures that
the file is read completely and correctly:

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
;;; init.el ends here
#+end_src

#+begin_src emacs-lisp :tangle early-init.el
;;; early-init.el ends here
#+end_src

These markers are a long-standing Emacs convention. They help maintain code
integrity and make it immediately obvious that the file ends where it should.
